| of which a joint's world position (`lb::joint::world_position`) | 8.1% | 6.4% | 8.3% | 7.7% | 7.1% | 8.5% | 8.9% |
| `jobj::make_matrix` | 20.0% | 17.1% | 18.2% | 19.7% | 17.5% | 18.0% | 20.1% |
| `PSMTXConcat` (`Mtx34::concat`) | 9.5% | 9.2% | 9.7% | 8.8% | 8.7% | 9.5% | 8.1% |
| Animation (`fobj::interpret`, `aobj::interpret`) | 12.9% | 9.0% | 10.1% | 13.0% | 9.9% | 12.1% | 13.9% |
| Collision (`mp`) | 11.9% | 10.3% | 11.9% | 11.8% | 10.6% | 10.9% | 9.8% |
| of which the ECB's load | 5.2% | 4.2% | 5.3% | 5.2% | 4.8% | 6.0% | 6.4% |
| Audio | 3.3% | 2.4% | 2.3% | 3.7% | 2.5% | 3.1% | 4.6% |
| The camera | 2.8% | 2.5% | 2.3% | 2.9% | 2.6% | 2.3% | 3.2% |
| The computer players | 2.7% | 2.0% | 3.8% | 2.2% | 2.2% | 3.5% | 2.4% |

Within the swing chains on `ics-peach-ps` (28.2% of the thread): the chains' own physics 19.6% (`Game::swing_chain`: the matrix products 6.3, `Mtx34::srt` 5.1, sine and cosine 4.9, `acos` 2.1, the square roots 3.2, `atan` 1.6, the Euler matrices and quaternions 3.2, the floor search 0.7) and the skeleton's dirty matrices they make first 8.4% (`setup_dirty_matrices`). **With `ics-chains`, `PSMTXConcat` falls from 9.2 to 9.7% of the thread to 5.0 to 5.9%** on the three Stadium profiles, the swing chains from 26.5 to 28.2% to 23.8 to 27.2% and `setup_matrix_sub` by 1.5 to 3 points; nothing else moves.

So the profile is the second survey's with the dispatch long gone and the joints' matrix work a little smaller: **the costs left are the original's own work done bit for bit**, a third of the thread in the joints' matrices and the swing chains (which overlap: the chains ask for matrices), a quarter in the display pass, a tenth each in animation and collision. Task 19 and `ics-chains` each found single-digit wins in the first; the next leads are **animation** (9 to 14% inclusive, the FObj and AObj interpreters decoding each track's compressed stream every frame, which a decoded form could skip without changing the arithmetic; task 30 profiles that split first), the **display pass's walk** (7.5 to 10.6% self, which [design call 5](#design-calls)'s follow-on would shorten only by changing compared state in re-simulated ticks), and the **audio DSP** (2 to 5% inclusive, its voices mixed every 5 ms of game time, also under the state-only pass).

**A Vanilla match from power-on, in the player** (the vanilla-speed agent's script: `melee-hd --power-on --ruleset vanilla` from no card, through the menus into a two-minute VS match, Fox against a level 9 computer Yoshi on Fountain of Dreams, and out to the character select, 8,760 ticks, `--bench` paced, Extrapolate at two frames a tick, 1280×960; frame-pointer builds, one unprofiled run and one under `perf record -g -F 1000` each, the base against the base with `perf/vanilla-speed` merged, scratch commit `633a173a`):

| | Base | Base with `vanilla-speed` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Simulation a tick (the player's own figure) | 4.97 ms (4.98 profiled) | 1.39 ms (1.42) |
| Predicting the next tick: restoring the copy, the tick | 2.38 + 3.67 ms | 0.66 + 0.85 ms |
| A tick with its prediction, p50 / p99 / max | 11.9 / 17.1 / 120 ms | 2.9 / 5.1 / 88 ms |
| The frame's renderer time, presenting with the GPU's wait | 3.58, 2.03 ms | 2.53, 0.50 ms |

**Before `vanilla-speed`, a Vanilla session's tick cost about ten times a bench match's, and the player's p99 tick with its prediction was over a 60 Hz frame.** The game thread's profile at the base (143,784 samples): the song's lookup on the disc every tick 24.1% (`session::read_song` through `nod`'s partition metadata), the heap image's snapshots about 20% (`hsd::image::Image::clone_from` 10.3%, its spans' `BTreeMap`s dropped 10.2%), the image's writes 10.9%, `memmove` 20.9% self. With `vanilla-speed` (67,224 samples) the frame's GX build is the game thread's largest part (46%, melee-render's `Ctx::draw`, which [F5](fast-renderer.md)'s records from the `Draw` list would take off it), the session's tick 14% and its prediction 22%; what's left of the image is 7% (`image::write` 4.3%, `image::cut` 2.8%), and the disc's metadata 3.7%. That is a cost of Vanilla's exactness the [list](#what-vanillas-exactness-cost) didn't have: with the song's lookup, the heap's image made a Vanilla session's simulation 3.6 times as long a tick (the Slippi ruleset's from power-on 1.41 against 1.04 ms, in `vanilla-speed`'s own runs), until `vanilla-speed`.

The `ics-peach-ps` rollback windows weren't measured again: `ics-chains` measured them on the worker ([performance](performance.md)'s section on the branch), 5,000 at 4.39 to 4.46 ms and 12,500 at 4.73 to 4.82, both still over 4 ms, accepted by Sarah after one time-boxed round.

**For the worker** (the scripts are this survey's scratch files: the builds, `w-meas.sh`'s four parts and the reports):

```sh
CARGO_PROFILE_RELEASE_DEBUG=line-tables-only cargo build --locked --release -j 3 -p melee-bench --bin melee-bench --example perfsplit
RUSTFLAGS="-C force-frame-pointers=yes" CARGO_PROFILE_RELEASE_DEBUG=line-tables-only cargo build --locked --release -j 3 -p melee-player
perfsplit data/bench/fox-fox-fd.slp [--rules slippi --heap off --fp slippi] --runs 1
melee-bench native --runs 3; MELEE_BENCH_FP=slippi melee-bench native --runs 3 --dir "$SLIPPI_REPLAYS/2026-01" Game_20260129T130802
perf record -g -F 2000 -D -1 --control fifo:CTL,ACK -- perfsplit ...      # PERF_CTL_FIFO, PERF_ACK_FIFO set
melee-hd --power-on --ruleset vanilla --saves DIR --holds HOLDS --bench 8760 --high-refresh extrapolate --frames-per-tick 2
```

### What checking costs

**A merge check today** (batch 14, `bfcd8f5c`, 2026-11-22). On this PC with the other agents winding down (`merge108b`): fmt and the ledger 12 minutes (the ledger's build), clippy 6, melee-sim's unit tests 6 (195 tests in 5 s: the rest is the `cfg(test)` build), the bench's allocation and ruleset tests 11, the sync tests 8, record 1, the player's 20, the renderers' unit tests 5, **the ruleset sessions at one thread 41** (12 tests, 2,426 s), **a 65-diff sample of the replay diffs 48**, the fast harness 33, **the frames tests about 50** (535 frames at three threads; 2,884 s on 2026-11-17) and **the leaf checks about three hours** (`merge108d`, beside the frames: 26 test binaries, then the items' three shards of 12 minutes each and the stages', each built and run in turn; 1 h 54 min in when this survey ended): about seven hours. On the worker at once (`checks53`): the oracle's and the tests' build 18 minutes, 12 replay diffs 31, **the sessions 3 h 7 min** at three threads (128 tests in five chunks; the `a` to `c`, `p` to `s` and `t` to `z` chunks 52 to 62 minutes each), **the single-player suite 1 h 35 min**, and the ruleset and leaf steps, which **ran nothing**: the worker has neither ssbm-rs's captures (`local/calls`, 16 GB) nor the Slippi ruleset's traces (`traces/m6s-*`), and those tests skip without their data (`ruleset`'s 12 "passed" in 0.00 s, each leaf binary in 0.01 s), so both ran again on this PC.

**The full verify** (verify-par's timings, `verify-logs/durations.tsv`, the milestone verify of 2026-11-17): 256 hours of jobs over its slots. **The sessions' rollback runs are 134 hours of it (52%)** (`session-sync --rollback 7` re-simulates seven ticks for every tick of every session), the sessions' diffs 49 (19%), the replays' diffs 36 and their sync tests 12, the leaf checks 15.5, the crates' test suites 8, the frames 1.7. Today's verify ran out at 7 hours, 4 h 17 min of it building under the other agents' load.

**What the building is.** melee-sim, 455,000 lines in one crate, takes 3 min 45 s to 4 min at `-j 3` on the idle worker and 9.7 GB, and a merge check builds it about five times: with no features (the bench, the player, the renderers), with `stubs` (leaf-check), with `stubs` and `oracle-hooks` (the oracle), with `cfg(test)` (its unit tests), and clippy's check; then 98 leaf-check test binaries of about 70 MB each and 8 of the oracle's, each linked apart. Under load each full build stretches to 20 to 40 minutes.

**Cheaper without weakening it** (task 26; the checks run stay the same, and each item is measured on the worker before and after):

1. **One melee-sim per check run.** `stubs` and `oracle-hooks` as switches set at run time (per melee-sim's `Cargo.toml`, both only add stand-ins and entry points the simulation never takes on its own; to be confirmed function by function), so the bench, the player, leaf-check and the oracle link one build: two of the five full builds gone.
2. **leaf-check as one test binary** (its 98 files as modules of one `tests/leaf/main.rs`, sharded by `LEAF_CHECK_SHARD` as now): one link where there are 98, and one `cargo test -p leaf-check` where the merge scripts run 26.
3. **A session's diff and its rollback in one run.** `session-sync` plays each session straight, from power-on with no trace, and rolls each tick of its matches back against that run; comparing the same straight run with the trace (as `session` does) plays every session once where the verify plays it twice: the diffs' simulation, part of their 49 job-hours, saved, their comparison kept.
4. **The ruleset sessions as processes** (three shards at a thread each) where they run at one thread now: 41 minutes to about 15.
5. **No step that passes by running nothing.** Copy the captures (16 GB) and the `m6s-*` traces to the worker (it has 507 GB free), so leaf-check and the ruleset sessions run there and leave this PC; and a variable (`MELEE_CHECKS_REQUIRE_DATA=1`, set by the merge scripts) that turns a test's "skipping: no trace" into a failure.

The rollback runs (item 3 leaves them whole) are the one large cost no change above touches; sampling them would weaken the check, so it isn't proposed.

### What the survey proposes

The structure the refactor set out to make is mostly there: the registries, the ruleset, the callback table, the pools, two boundaries, the dedup's large families, and, with `wave6-rest` and `own-structs` merged, every generated field named and the first structs owned. What's left is in four places. **Checking**, which now costs more than most changes: task 26 first, before the next round's merges. **The last names and lists**: the offset-named helpers, types and locals (task 29), the player kinds and the computer players' behaviours (task 28). **Ownership and boundaries**: `Fighter` and `Item` (in progress), then the stages' boundary on the owned `StageInfo` (task 27), the HUD's with its structs (task 32), the modes' and the player's structs (task 31), the per-kind unions left as they are (design call 14). **Speed**: the simulation is about half the headless target, its costs the original's own math, and animation the one large lead not yet tried (task 30); the target itself is design call 15. The branches above merge first. The proposed tasks are 26 to 33 [below](#the-refactor), with design calls 14 to 16.

## Proposed tasks

Sizes: S a few days of one agent's work, M a subsystem, L more. Every refactor task keeps the oracle identical: the golden diffs and sessions it reaches (`melee-oracle` with `--write-golden` where states change shape), `sync-test --rollback 7` on the bench, `melee-bench native` before and after on the worker or a quiet machine, the ledger's `--check`, and the frames tests where the display pass is touched. A new check is mutation-tested; a defect found goes in [Melee's bugs](melee-bugs.md). The fast renderer's tasks are checked against the exact renderer (geometry within a tolerance and perceptual image comparisons, [above](#the-fast-renderer)), not the oracle.

**The Slippi ruleset's state** (2026-11-17): its task 4 is done; task 5's second part (`feat/m6s-moot-2`: the model off in the player's Slippi matches, `Settings::vs_with`) and task 6 (`feat/m6s-player`) have branches at `f0fa4c81`, and the live match's audio (`feat/m6s-live-audio`) is queued. Tasks below that touch `vs_match::Settings`, `game::live`, `game::session` or `game::record` wait for those to merge; they're marked.

### Task 1, the callback table

**Done (2026-11-18, `perf/callback-table` from `53429b6c`).** Every callback the state holds as an address (`CodeRef`) runs through one table from the address to its port: fighter callbacks, GObj processes (the stages', items' events, the HUD's, the scenes'), functions without arguments (stages' hooks, scenes' frames), item predicates, render callbacks and `code::call`. `CodeRef` is still the address, so the state, the oracle's comparisons and saved states are unchanged.

**How.** Each module's dispatch function keeps the `match` on the address its port wrote, marked `#[melee_callbacks::callbacks(KIND)]` (`Fighter`, `Proc`, `Event`, `Pred`, `Call`, `Render`). The attribute (`crates/melee-callbacks`, a proc-macro crate of its own so the oracle's coverage hash, which covers `melee-derive`, doesn't change) turns the function into one that adds an entry per arm: the arm's body as a function of the callback's parameters. A delegation arm (`a if fox::run(g, gobj, a) => {}`, or a body `init::run(..) || special_n::run(..)`, `a(..).or_else(|| b(..))`) adds the other function's entries. `game::code::table` walks the roots in `game::code` once, on the first dispatch, into one open-addressing hash table per kind (at most half full; a multiply and one or two probes). The conversion was mechanical: a script read rustc's diagnostics for each delegation called with the table's builder and marked the function it named, round by round, 718 functions in all, the roots in `game::code` included; the attribute refuses, at compile time, any arm that tests more than the address. Hand edits where the shapes differed: the target stages' role tables and hooks (a registration written out in `gr::target_test`), the legendary beasts' shared dispatch (each beast's own match now), the crowds' join callbacks (a constant list), the All-Star intro's frame and render, the album's renders, Camera Mode's calls, the characters' `call` fallbacks to their `run` (one generic fallback in `code::call` now: a fighter callback called with its GObj alone), and five direct callers of a module's `run` (Kirby's mushroom, the hands' dispatch, the hookshot's and grapple's frame hooks), which now go through the table.

**Order.** The chains ran the first module that knew an address. The table refuses an address registered twice by name with both places (`the_callback_table_builds`), and the build found 53: the common states' arms inlined in `code::run_fighter_cb` that `ft::co::dispatch` (earlier in the chain) shadowed, gone, and two of `dispatch`'s that earlier modules shadowed (`0x800CEE00`, Link's forward smash's interrupt; `0x800E8864`, Fox's down special's physics), gone from `dispatch`. No call is also a fighter callback except efLib's pause and resume, which the chain's first link took as the table does (`no_call_is_also_a_fighter_callback`).

**Kirby** is converted last, in a commit of its own (`sim: Put Kirby's callbacks in the table`) on top of a merge of `refactor/dedup-kirby`'s first round (seven copies sharing their originals' specials), so the two land together; until that commit his chain ran after the table. A later round of the dedup marks its new dispatch functions the same way: the build names each delegation that needs the attribute.

**Dispatch's share of self time: 23.7 to 29.7% before, 0.9 to 1.4% after** (the survey's sampler and buckets, below; the fn-pointer shims and the lookups counted as dispatch, an entry's own body as the callback's work). The survey's 17 to 26% counted the old chains' inlined callee bodies as dispatch too, which is why the speed-up is smaller than removing a quarter of self time would give.

| Profile (sampler, 2 kHz, `melee-bench native --runs 1`) | Base: the survey's dispatch bucket | Branch: the same bucket | Branch: with the shims and lookups |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `fox-fox-fd` | 25.7% | 0.2% | 1.35% |
| `fox-marth-ps` | 23.7% | 0.0% | 1.38% |
| Sarah's `Game_20260129T130802` (`MELEE_BENCH_FP=slippi MELEE_BENCH_HEAP=off`) | 29.7% | 0.1% | 0.88% |

**Match speed on the worker: +12.7 to +14.9%, just short of the +15% target** ([design call 9](#design-calls): these are the deciding numbers). **Sarah, 2026-11-19: the target counts as met.** The +15% was set from the busy PC's profile (dispatch 18 to 26% of self time); from the worker's (8 to 11%) it would have been about +10%, and dispatch's own target (under 3%) is met at 0.0%. `perf/callback-table` `18354866` against main `c6e274d5`, both `cargo build --locked --release -p melee-bench` with `CARGO_PROFILE_RELEASE_DEBUG=line-tables-only`, on [the worker](performance.md#machine) in one exclusive reservation: `melee-bench native --runs 5` in three rounds alternating base and branch, median frames/s of the 15 runs each, every run in sync:

| Match | Main | Branch | Change |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `fox-fox-fd` | 2,193 (2,167–2,242) | 2,519 (2,497–2,572) | **+14.9%** (rounds +15.0, +15.0, +14.4%) |
| `fox-marth-ps` | 1,877 (1,864–1,929) | 2,115 (2,100–2,155) | **+12.7%** |
| Sarah's `Game_20260129T130802` (`MELEE_BENCH_FP=slippi`, the model on) | 2,321 (2,264–2,340) | 2,657 (2,640–2,677) | **+14.5%** |

The branch's `fox-fox-fd` profile there (`perf record -F 2000`, the survey's buckets) puts the dispatch bucket at 0.0% and the table's call shims at 0.7%: the self-time target is met. The worker's own survey puts the old chains at 8 to 11% of self time ([above](#measured-on-the-worker-2026-11-19)), so the gain is larger than their self time: the chains also slowed the code around them. The busy PC's numbers below are kept as indicative.

**On this PC, before the worker measured: about +8 to +13%, indicative only** (superseded by the worker's above). Median frames/s, base and branch alternating in rounds of five runs, on this PC; every run in sync. First, 15 runs each in three rounds with other agents' builds and oracle runs keeping the PC busy (the branch before the dedup merge and Kirby's commit):

| Match | Base | Branch | Change | Runs' spread, base / branch |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `fox-fox-fd` (Vanilla) | 2,921 | 3,254 | +11.4% | 2,809–3,021 / 3,111–3,435 |
| `fox-marth-ps` (Vanilla) | 2,511 | 2,702 | +7.6% | 2,428–2,604 / 2,624–3,228 |
| Sarah's `Game_20260129T130802` (`MELEE_BENCH_FP=slippi`, the model on) | 2,882 | 3,249 | +12.7% | 2,742–3,066 / 3,174–3,338 |

Then 10 runs each in two rounds with the PC quieter (63% load), the final branch (with `refactor/dedup-kirby`'s merge):

| Match | Base | Branch | Change | Runs' spread, base / branch |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `fox-fox-fd` | 4,729 | 5,176 | +9.5% | 4,467–4,964 / 5,071–5,382 |
| `fox-marth-ps` | 3,929 | 4,324 | +10.0% | 3,874–4,130 / 4,165–4,469 |
| Sarah's replay | 4,780 | 5,303 | +10.9% | 4,573–4,925 / 5,214–5,488 |

The commands, to rerun on the worker (`SSBM_DISC` and `SSBM_RS_LOCAL` set; the base built at `53429b6c`, the branch at its tip, both `cargo build --locked --release -p melee-bench`):

```sh
melee-bench native --runs 5 fox-fox-fd fox-marth-ps
# Sarah's replay copied into DIR as sarah-130802.slp
MELEE_BENCH_FP=slippi melee-bench native --dir DIR --runs 5 sarah-130802
```

Profiles: the same two builds with `CARGO_PROFILE_RELEASE_DEBUG=line-tables-only`, `python -I sampler.py OUT.json 2000 -- melee-bench native --runs 1 MATCH` (Sarah's with `MELEE_BENCH_FP=slippi MELEE_BENCH_HEAP=off`), bucketed by the survey's `cats.py`.

**Checks.**

- **The whole state, tick by tick.** Two scratch oracles, the base's (`53429b6c`) and the branch's, each patched to write the state's hash after every tick of a golden check rather than compare it (the golden's staleness ignored, so it only sets how many ticks run), gave the same hash every tick on all 329 of the verify list's replay diffs, 3,534,697 ticks, with no stop (the branch before Kirby's commit, his chain on the compatibility path). The final branch (the dedup merge and Kirby's commit on top) against the same base dumps: the same, all 329, 3,534,697 ticks.
- **Mutation:** `ftCo_Wait_Anim`'s and `ftCo_Wait_IASA`'s entries swapped in `ft::co::dispatch` (each address run by the other's port) makes `fox-fox-fd`'s hashes differ from the base's in tick 124, its first, and the full diff names the fighters' joints there (`p_link 8 #1 jobj/ aobj: ours None, theirs 0x80eeac40`). An address added twice fails `the_callback_table_builds`, naming both places (how the 53 shadowed ones were found).
- `sync-test --rollback 7 --fp hardware`: the six bench replays (`fox-fox-fd` 15,569 ticks checked, `fox-marth-fd` 15,235, `fox-marth-bf` 15,104, `fox-marth-ps` 13,579, `ganon-captain-fd` 12,061, `ics-peach-ps` 11,217) and Sarah's replay with `--fp slippi` (13,773), no difference.
- `melee-oracle --test ruleset`: all 9 pass. `--test session`, 31 named: `the_vanilla_runs_from_power_on`, `a_session_plays_its_match_from_the_selects`, `a_second_match_sets_its_spawner_up_again`, `a_session_match_rolls_back`, `the_approach_match_rolls_back`, `training_unloads_its_computer_players`, `the_debug_menus_go_back_laid_out`, `a_tournament_match_on_peachs_castle_goes_back_laid_out`, `a_tournament_tie_runs_over_the_stage_selects_preview`, `the_selects_left_by_l_r_and_start`, `matches_from_the_menus_on_three_stages`, `the_attract_match_on_brinstar_reads_a_leftover`, `rainbow_cruises_blocks_hold_the_heaps_leftovers`, `the_modes_exits`, `single_player_setups`, `a_brick_blocks_item_pops_out_at_random`, `the_trophy_lottery_draws_and_saves`, `the_trophy_gallery_toured`, `the_album_browsed`, `camera_mode_takes_and_saves_a_snapshot`, `target_test_cleared`, `the_home_run_contest`, `multi_man_melee`, `all_star`, `single_player_intros`, `the_debug_endings`, `master_hand_in_debug_vs`, `crazy_hand_in_debug_vs`, `giga_bowser_in_debug_vs`, `adventure_game_over`, `the_debug_cutscenes`: all pass (the whole suite and single-player's are the merge check's).
- The ledger's `--check` (19,827 of 19,827; only `docs/port-ledger.csv`'s line numbers moved, an attribute line above each dispatch function), melee-sim's unit tests, `fmt` and `clippy -D warnings`.
- `ticks_and_restores_dont_allocate` fails, `fox-marth-bf: tick 1029 made 1 allocations`, on the base `53429b6c` as on the branch: not this change's.

### Task 2, the character registry

**Done (2026-11-19, `refactor/char-registry` from batch 6 and `refactor/dedup-kirby-2`, `9c490493`).** Every character is one entry of `game::ft::registry::CHARACTERS`, indexed by its fighter kind, and nothing else lists characters. A new character's port writes its module and fills its entry ([porting](porting.md#characters)).

**Two kinds, both the game's.** The game numbers its characters twice, as it does its stages ([design call 2](#design-calls); Sarah, 2026-11-19, on task 3: "two enums is fine"): `FighterKind` (`Ft_Kind_*`, 33, `#[repr(u8)]`) says which fighter's code and files run, `Fighter::kind`, the index of the executable's per-kind tables; `CharacterKind` (`CKind_*`, 33) says which character a player chose, the character select's, the rules', the replays' and the save's number, which `ftMapping_list` (`pl::player::ckind_ftkind`) takes to its fighter kinds. One type can't hold both: Fox is 1 in one and 2 in the other, the Ice Climbers are one character and two fighters (Popo and Nana), Zelda and Sheik two characters each its own fighter. The state keeps the numbers (`Fighter::kind`, the players' and the rules' `ckind`), so it, the comparer and saved states are unchanged; code reads them through the enums: `fp.kind == FighterKind::Fox`, `match fp.fighter_kind() { FighterKind::Link | FighterKind::Clink => .. }`, `FighterKind::from_id(kind)` where the number isn't a fighter's own, `CharacterKind::Zelda.id()`, `CharacterKind::NONE`.

**An entry** (`ft::Character`): its `kind`, `name`, `tables` (its row of `ft::kind_tables`: files, costumes, animations, action states, the hooks' addresses), `callbacks` (its row of `ft::co::kind_callbacks`, regenerated as one struct per kind: the specials' state changes, the knockback and item hooks the common code calls by kind), the ports of its hooks (`first_init`, `on_load`, `on_death`, `load_special_attrs`), its module's `#[callbacks]` dispatch (`fighter`, `calls`; one module's for kinds that share one: Popo's for Nana, Captain Falcon's for Ganondorf, the male wireframe's for the female), `attrs_size`, and its roster line (`vs`, the `CharacterKind` a match takes it as; `keys`). All 33 kinds are ported.

**What became reads of it**:

| Was | Now |
| --- | --- |
| 227 integer definitions of fighter and character kinds in 136 files (the survey's `KIND_*` and the `FT_KIND_*`, `FTKIND_*`, `CKIND_*` and `CHKIND_*` ones), and the matches and comparisons on them | `FighterKind::*`, `CharacterKind::*`; matches on a fighter's kind on `Fighter::fighter_kind()` |
| `code::fighter_callbacks`' 30 character delegations and `code::calls`' 27 | each entry's `fighter` and `calls` (`ft::registry::register`, from `AllEntries::collect`) |
| `create_model`'s `on_load`, `on_death` and `load_special_attrs` (93 arms on the tables' addresses) and `create`'s first-setup arms | the entry's hooks |
| `ft::co::kind_callbacks`' 16 tables of 33 addresses (14 generated, 2 added by hand) | one generated `KindCallbacks` per kind (`tools/tables/kind_callbacks.py` emits all 16 now), read as `character(kind).callbacks` |
| `roster::CHARACTERS` (26 lines) | the entries with a `vs`; `roster::characters()`, `character`, `character_by_kind`, `offered_characters` read them, by name (strictly alphabetical now: the old list had Jigglypuff before the Ice Climbers) |
| `ft::kinds`' attribute sizes | each entry's `attrs_size` (the ports keep their constants, which the entries name) |

The original's tables by number stay tables and are typed by the enums' counts: the names and their widths, `lb::audio`'s character banks and slots, the sound test's songs, the endings' fall sizes, the events' character list (`CharacterKind::COUNT`), Kirby's copy files and effects (`FighterKind::COUNT`). The conversion was scripted (the entries generated from `kind_tables`, `create_model`'s arms and the roster; the constants, imports, comparisons and match arms rewritten by pattern, their types kept), then compiled clean by hand where a match mixed kinds with bare numbers. The item kinds that live in fighter files (Peach's `KIND_CAPSULE` and `KIND_PARASOL`, the fireballs' `KIND_*_FIRE`) are task 4's.

**A port defect found, kept.** Classic's lone Popo (`gm_8017CE34`) stores 0x0A as the player's character where the original stores `ChKind_Popo`, 0x20 (`li r4, 0x20` at 0x8017D130); the constant was named `CHKIND_POPO`. The refactor keeps the number with a comment, for a fix of its own (fixed since on `fix/popo-classic`: `CharacterKind::Popo`, with a session through Classic's targets round as the Ice Climbers, [the oracle](oracle.md#sessions)). A constant named `CKIND_NESS` there was 0xD, Pikachu's character kind, as the original compares: now `CharacterKind::Pikachu`.

**Task 3's stage lists.** The shadow checks (`gr::display`, 40 arms on `stage_info.on_check_shadow_render`) and the line hazards (`ft::attack::line_hazard_desc` on `on_touch_line`) are the stage entry's `shadow` and `touch_line`, found by the address the state holds, the loaded stage's data first (`gr::registry::by_address`), in a commit of their own; the four route stages' own line-touch ports, which the old list skipped for a bare `None`, are now called.

**Checks.**

- **The whole state, tick by tick.** The base build (`9c490493`) checked the goldens of all 330 of the verify list's replay diffs (every character, the bench matches, the stages, items and specials): the 296 the Kirby round wrote at `4770a3ca` matched it every tick (their coverage line set to the base's: the comparer's sources changed only by task 3's renames), and the 34 others, whose stored goldens predate the base, were written anew by its full diffs (each comparing every traced tick with no difference). The branch's build then checked all 330: 3,552,272 ticks, every tick's state hash the same (the coverage line set to the branch's: the comparer changed only by `tools/capture`'s constants becoming the enum).
- **Mutation:** Fox's and Falco's hooks swapped in their entries: `fox-fox-fd`, `mewtwo-falco-fd-1690697d` and `gnw-falco-bf-e43c32ae` fail their goldens in tick 1. Two entries swapped whole fail `the_registry_is_indexed_by_fighter_kind`.
- `melee-oracle --test ruleset`: all 12 pass. `--test session`, 22 named for the characters (`a_session_plays_its_match_from_the_selects`, `a_random_character_and_stage`, `computer_players_at_three_levels`, `a_team_battle_with_handicaps`, `dr_marios_pills_in_his_results_pose`, `a_match_with_dk_peach_and_yoshi_leaves_their_written_attributes`, `master_hand_in_debug_vs`, `crazy_hand_in_debug_vs`, `giga_bowser_in_debug_vs`, `the_debug_endings`, `single_player_intros`, `all_star`, `multi_man_melee`, `target_test_cleared`, `the_hookshot_lengths_live_in_their_file`, `the_results_sessions_end_with_their_cards`, `the_unlock_sessions_end_with_their_cards`, `the_challenger_and_stage_sessions_end_with_their_cards`, `training_unloads_its_computer_players`, `adventure_game_over`, `the_trophy_lottery_draws_and_saves`, `a_session_match_rolls_back`): all pass. `--test single_player`, 6 named (`each_mode_runs_to_its_first_stop`, `adventures_giga_bowser_path_runs_clean_to_its_card_after`, `adventures_shells_on_mushroom_kingdom_run_clean`, `sarahs_all_star_clear_runs_clean_to_its_card_after`, `the_events_run_clean_to_their_cards_after`, `classic_clear_runs_clean_to_its_card_after`): all pass.
- `sync-test --rollback 7 --fp hardware`: `fox-marth-ps` (13,579 ticks checked), `ics-peach-ps` (11,217) and `ganon-captain-fd` (12,061), no difference.
- leaf-check: `fox`, `marth`, `kirby`, `nana`, `peach`, `samus`, `link`, `young_link`, `zelda`, `mario`, `bowser`, `game_and_watch`, `ftcommon`, `ftcommon_entries`, `passive_wall`, `fighter_create`: all pass.
- melee-sim's unit tests (the registry's own: each entry at its index with its kind's tables, each roster character's kind playing its fighter kind, the first setups following the tables; the stage registry's new one: every stage but the dummy with its shadow and line ports, found by their addresses), melee-player's `roster_tests` (4), `ticks_and_restores_dont_allocate` at one thread, the ledger's `--check` (19,827 of 19,827; the same 18,078 `#[doc(alias)]` attributes before and after, a sorted list diffed; only `docs/port-ledger.csv`'s line numbers moved), `fmt` and `clippy -D warnings`.
- **Speed (indicative, this PC with other work on it):** `melee-bench native --runs 5 fox-fox-fd fox-marth-ps`, base and branch alternating in three rounds, median of the 15 runs: `fox-fox-fd` 4,467 frames/s before, 4,403 after (runs 4,258–4,611 and 4,174–4,564); `fox-marth-ps` 3,740 before, 3,677 after (3,500–3,932 and 3,518–3,772). Within the runs' spread; the hooks run once a fighter is made or respawns, and the callback table holds the same entries. The worker's runner decides.

### Task 3, the stage registry

**Done (2026-11-19, `refactor/stage-registry` from `4770a3ca`).** Every stage is one entry of `game::gr::registry::STAGES`, indexed by its ground kind, and nothing else lists stages. A new stage's port writes its module and fills its entry ([porting](porting.md#stages)).

**Two kinds, both the game's.** The game numbers its stages twice ([design call 2](#design-calls) asks for the game's own numbers, so both are enums with them): `GrKind` (`Gr_Kind_*`, 71, `#[repr(u8)]`) says which stage's code and archive run, `stage_info.grkind`, the index of the executable's `stage_datas`; `StKind` (`St_Kind_*`, `#[repr(u16)]`) says which stage a mode starts, `StartMeleeRules`' and the replays' stage and the stage select's number: 286 of them, the VS stages and then the single-player and event variants, which `stage_id_map` takes to their ground kind (`StKind::ground`). One type can't hold both, Battlefield being 31 in one and 36 in the other. `StKind` names the decompilation's kinds and the ones the code names (the Home-Run Contest's 84, All-Star's rest area 85, Event 50's 251, the game cache's none 328); the others stay numbers, as the event and single-player tables hold them. The state keeps the numbers (`stage_info.grkind`, `gr.stkind`, the rules' `stkind`), so it, the comparer and saved states are unchanged; code reads them through the enums: `stage_info.grkind == GrKind::Castle` (`PartialEq` between the enum and the number), `match stage_info.ground_kind() { GrKind::Kongo => .. }`, `StKind::Battle.id()`.

**An entry** (`gr::Stage`): its `kind`, `name`, `data` (the `StageData`: archive, objects' callbacks, hook addresses, collision joints), the ports of its hooks (`setup`, `load`, `start`, `demo`), its module's `#[callbacks]` dispatch (`procs`, `renders`), `vars` (which `GroundVars` member each map's object uses) and its roster line (`vs`, the `StKind` a VS match starts it with; `keys`; `audit`). The unported kinds (23, 26) are `None`.

**What became reads of it**:

| Was | Now |
| --- | --- |
| 79 integer definitions of 63 stage-kind names, 43 in `gr/mod.rs` and 36 in 25 other files (`GR_KIND_*`, `GR_*`, `ST_KIND_*`, `STKIND_*`), and integer arms naming ground kinds in `ft::cpu_decide`, `ft::cpu_move` and `gr::setup`'s camera and blast-zone defaults | `GrKind::*`, `StKind::*`; the benchmark's named stages (`tools/bench`) are now spelled from `StKind` |
| `code::events`' 45 stage arms (38 delegations, Battlefield's and Final Destination's hooks, the dummy stage's); each module's `run_event` (37 functions, their addresses written a second time beside `data.rs`'s) | the entry's `setup`, `load`, `start`, by the addresses in its `data` (`registry::register`); `gr::setup` calls the entry's hooks directly |
| `code::run_demo_init`'s 40 arms | the entry whose `data.on_demo_init` is the address |
| `code::procs`' 40 stage delegations, `render::run`'s 9 | the entry's `procs`, `renders` (Corneria's talk window and the Home-Run Contest's labels' camera are now their modules' `run_render`) |
| `gr::data::stage_data`'s 44 arms and `target::stage_data`, `stage_file` | `registry::stage(grkind).data`, `Stage::file` |
| `gr::vars_kind`'s 115 arms | each entry's `vars` (`gr::vars_kind` reads them) |
| `roster::STAGES` (29 lines) | the entries with a `vs`; `roster::stages()`, `stage`, `stage_by_kind`, `offered_stages` read them, by name as before |

The original's tables indexed by the number stay as tables and are read through the enums: `lb::audio`'s `stage_id_map` and the ground kinds' sound banks, `mn::stagesel`'s entries, the events' and single-player modes' stage kinds. The conversion was scripted (the registry generated from the roster's lines, `vars_kind` and the stage table; the constants, imports and integer arms rewritten by pattern), then compiled clean by hand where a `match` on a number needed the enum.

**Left.** Two dispatches by address still listed stages: the shadow checks (`gr::display`) and the line hazards (`ft::attack::line_hazard_desc`, in `ft`, which this task stayed out of). [Task 2](#task-2-the-character-registry) made them the entry's `shadow` and `touch_line`. `gr::mod`'s stage items' callbacks (`yaku_dealt` and its siblings) are callbacks by address rather than a list of stages: task 1's table's, given a kind of their own.

**How task 2 and task 4 copy this.**

1. One enum per numbering the game has, `#[repr]` with the game's numbers and the decompilation's names, `ALL`, `from_id`, `id`, and `PartialEq` with the number; the state keeps the number, so nothing the oracle compares changes. Characters have two as stages do (`CharacterKind`, the external one the replays use, and the internal fighter kind, `ckind`); items have one.
2. One static array of `Option<Entry>` indexed by the kind, an entry holding what was listed elsewhere: its name, its data (the executable's tables), the ports of its hooks as fields, its module's `#[callbacks]` dispatch functions, and its roster line. A test that each entry sits at its index.
3. The callback table takes the hooks by the addresses in the entry's data, and each module's dispatch from the entry (`registry::register`, called from `AllEntries::collect`), so the roots in `code` lose their per-kind arms; a hook called by address (leaf-check, `code::run_event`) still finds its port.
4. Generate the array from the lists it replaces, then rewrite the constants by script, file by file; matches on a number become matches on `from_id(..)` or a typed accessor (`StageInfo::ground_kind`).
5. Check: the ledger's aliases diffed sorted, the whole state's per-tick hashes before and after on the replays that reach the kinds, and a mutation that swaps two entries' hooks.

**Checks.**

- **The whole state, tick by tick** (the Rollout pilot's way): the branch's oracle ran the full diff of 32 of the verify's replays (every `stage-*` diff, 23, and the benchmark's `fox-fox-fd`, `fox-marth-fd`, `fox-marth-bf`, `fox-marth-ps`, `ganon-captain-fd`, and for the VS stages those leave out `stadium-slippi`, `ics-fox-dl-a7365e16 --heap`, `roy-puff-fod`, `gnw-zelda-ys-4e9d13cb --heap`), each comparing every traced tick with no difference and no stale read, writing a per-tick golden; the base's (`4770a3ca`) then checked each golden: 529,290 ticks, every tick's state hash the same. The goldens' coverage line was set to the base's, since the comparer's sources changed only by the constants' renames in `tools/capture` and `tools/oracle/src/diff.rs`; the state hash's own sources (`golden.rs`, `state_eq.rs`, `mirror*.rs`, `melee-derive`) are unchanged.
- **Mutation:** Battlefield's and Final Destination's `setup` swapped in their entries: `fox-fox-fd` stops before its first tick (the fighters' heap addresses differ from the trace's), and `fox-marth-bf` stops in Final Destination's setup (`HSD_JObjSetScale: jobj`).
- `melee-oracle --test ruleset`: all 12 pass. `--test session`, 22 named (`target_test_from_the_menu`, `target_test_cleared`, `the_home_run_contest`, `multi_man_melee`, `all_star`, `single_player_setups`, `single_player_intros`, `adventure_game_over`, `race_in_debug_vs`, `the_fzero_race_in_debug_vs`, `the_debug_cutscenes`, `matches_from_the_menus_on_three_stages`, `the_selects_left_by_l_r_and_start`, `the_random_stage_rule`, `a_random_character_and_stage`, `the_extra_rules_and_the_stage_switch`, `a_tournament_tie_runs_over_the_stage_selects_preview`, `master_hand_in_debug_vs`, `crazy_hand_in_debug_vs`, `the_attract_match_on_brinstar_reads_a_leftover`, `rainbow_cruises_blocks_hold_the_heaps_leftovers`, `the_debug_endings`): all pass. `--test single_player`, 6 named (`each_mode_runs_to_its_first_stop`, `adventures_giga_bowser_path_runs_clean_to_its_card_after`, `adventures_shells_on_mushroom_kingdom_run_clean`, `sarahs_all_star_clear_runs_clean_to_its_card_after`, `the_events_run_clean_to_their_cards_after`, `classic_clear_runs_clean_to_its_card_after`): all pass.
- `sync-test --rollback 7 --fp hardware`: `stage-corneria` (14,518 ticks checked) and `stage-poke-floats` (28,918), no difference.
- melee-sim's unit tests (the roster's stage audits and the registry's own: each entry at its index, each roster stage kind starting its ground kind, each hook's address in the table), melee-player's `roster_tests` (`every_roster_stage_plays`, `every_offered_stage_plays_with_items`), `ticks_and_restores_dont_allocate` at one thread, the ledger's `--check` (19,827 of 19,827; the same 18,078 `#[doc(alias)]` attributes before and after, a sorted list diffed; only `docs/port-ledger.csv`'s line numbers moved), `fmt` and `clippy -D warnings`.
- **Speed (indicative, this PC with other work on it):** `melee-bench native --runs 5 fox-fox-fd fox-marth-ps`, base and branch alternating in three rounds, median of the 15 runs: `fox-fox-fd` 5,068 frames/s before, 5,111 after (runs 4,724–5,399 and 4,715–5,586); `fox-marth-ps` 4,378 before, 4,487 after (3,938–4,506 and 4,059–4,794). Within the runs' spread: the hooks run once a scene, and the callback table is the same size.

### Task 4, the item registry

**Done (2026-11-19, `refactor/item-registry` from `f2b6d007`, task 3's tip).** Every item kind is one entry of `game::it::registry::ITEMS`, indexed by its kind, and nothing else lists items. A new item's port writes its module and fills its entry ([porting](porting.md#items)).

**One kind, the game's.** The game numbers its items once (`ItemKind`, `It_Kind_*`), so there is one enum, `ItKind` (`#[repr(u8)]`, 237 kinds, the decompilation's names at first, `It_Kind_G_Shell` as `GShell`, and since [task 12's wave 3](#task-12-wave-3-the-items-names) the names players know, `GreenShell`; `It_PKind_Random`, the number the stages' hurtbox items take, is `StageHurtbox`). The decompilation's markers that share a kind's number are associated constants: `COMMON_END` (`It_Common_End`, the Ray Gun's shot), `FIRST_POKEMON` (`It_PKind_Start`, Goldeen), `POKEMON_END` (`It_PKind_Terminate`, Chikorita's leaf); `UNSELECTED` is -1. The executable's tables have a 238th kind, empty, which no enum value names. The state keeps the numbers (`Item::kind`), so it, the comparer and saved states are unchanged; code reads them through the enum: `ip.kind == ItKind::HeartContainer` and `kind < ItKind::Goomba` (`PartialEq` and `PartialOrd` between the number and the enum), `match ip.item_kind() { .. }` or `ItKind::of(kind)` for a number a caller knows is a kind (it stops by name if it isn't), `ItKind::HeartContainer.id()` where a function takes the number.

**An entry** (`it::ItemKind`): its `kind`, `name`, `events` and `preds` (the `#[callbacks(Proc)]` and `#[callbacks(Pred)]` dispatch functions its logic table's and state tables' callbacks are in: its module's, and the ones it shares, `zako`'s for the enemies, `rabbitc`'s predicate the Screw Attack's tables name, `maril`'s for Cyndaquil's flame) and `vars` (the member of the variables' union its code reads). Its tables are the executable's, read through the enum (`ItKind::logic`, `ItKind::render`; `tables::group` keeps `Item_80267978`'s split). Unlike the stages, every kind has an entry (a plain array, not `Option`s): every kind has tables and a union member (the Pokémon's for 161 to 207 whether or not a port runs them), and a kind no port runs has empty `events` and `preds`. The callbacks no kind's tables name (the states Kirby's inhale and Yoshi's tongue give any item, the Pokémon's effects' hitlag hooks) are the registry's `HELD_EVENTS` and `HELD_PREDS`.

**What became reads of it**:

| Was | Now |
| --- | --- |
| `it::kinds::run_event` (165 modules joined by `\|\|`, a delegation from `code::procs`) and `run_pred` (161 by `or_else`) | each entry's `events` and `preds` (`registry::register`, called from `AllEntries::collect`); the probe that built the entries found every module of the chains in a kind's tables but those three |
| `it::vars`' `members!` lists (the kinds each union member's code uses) and `vars_kind`'s range for the Pokémon | each entry's `vars` (`registry::vars_kind`) |
| 174 integer constants in `it::kinds`, and 121 copies of item kinds in 67 other files (`IT_KIND_*`, `IT_*`, `KIND`, `PKIND_*`, local names such as `HEART_KIND`, `COIN`, `BOMBHEI`, in `it`, `ft`, `gr`, `gm`, `pl`, `db`, the audits and the tools) | `ItKind::*` |
| Bare item numbers the code compared or passed (`drop_by_kind`'s arms, `healing_on`, `is_empty_shooter`, the CPU's enemy and container tests, `pl_8003E17C`'s statistics map, the items audit, the item switch's `ITEM_SWITCH_KINDS`, the benchmark's item switch lists, leaf-check's `item_kind(c) == N`) | `ItKind::*` |
| The generated tables' `// kind N` | `// ItKind::Name` (`tools/tables/item_tables.py` reads the decompilation's `forward.h` for the names; the tables' content is unchanged) |

The original's tables indexed by the number stay as tables and are read through the enum: the logic, render and state tables, the articles' groups (`article_of`, `CHAR_ARTICLES`), the CPU's `ITEM_WORTH`, the Pokémon's tables, the spawner's switch mask. The conversion was scripted: a probe test dumped each chained module's addresses and each kind's table callbacks, from which the entries were generated; a rewrite resolved each constant's uses (qualified paths, imports, `self as` aliases, module `KIND`s) to `ItKind`, then `match` arms on numbers became matches on `ItKind::of(..)`, and the rest compiled clean by hand.

**`refactor/dedup-kirby-2`'s files** (Kirby's copies, the bombs, arrows and bows, the pan, the blasters, the needles, Toad, PK Flash, Shadow Ball, Ice Shot, the fire breath; 40 files) are converted in the branch's second commit (`sim: Name the copied specials' item kinds by ItKind`), so the coordinator can drop it and redo it after that branch merges: the first commit keeps those files compiling through `it::kinds` constants defined as `ItKind` values, which the second removes. If that branch adds item-kind constants or numbers of its own, the redo converts them the same way.

**Left.** The bare item numbers were found by searching the kind comparisons, the item functions' arguments and the lists the item switch and the benchmark hold; a number that reaches a kind through a name not about kinds may remain, and a search's hit there is a one-line `ItKind` change. The spawner's switch mask (`x20`, bit N for kind N) and the bench's `items_switch` test's mask literal stay bits.

**Checks.**

- **The whole state, tick by tick** (task 3's way): the branch's oracle ran the full diff of 82 of the verify's replays (every `items-*` diff, 41; every `stage-*` diff, 23, for the stages' own items; the items-on CPU diffs `oracle-cpu-items-*` and `oracle-cpu-approach-*`, 8; the Ice Climbers' `oracle-ics-items-*`, 2; `coins-fd` and `coins-items-fd`; and the benchmark's `fox-fox-fd` (with and without `--heap`), `fox-marth-fd`, `fox-marth-bf`, `fox-marth-ps`, `ganon-captain-fd`), each comparing every traced tick with no difference and no stale read, 959,092 ticks, writing a per-tick golden; the base's (`f2b6d007`) then checked each golden: all 82, 959,092 ticks, every tick's state hash the same. The goldens' coverage line was set to the base's, since the comparer's sources changed only by the constants' renames in `tools/capture/src/items.rs` and an unused import in `tools/oracle/src/birdo_sweep.rs`; the state hash's own sources are unchanged.
- **Mutation:** the Capsule's and the Crate's `events` and `preds` swapped in their entries fail `each_kinds_callbacks_are_in_its_entry` (`Capsule: 0x8027cf00 isn't in its entry's modules`, and on through both kinds' callbacks). The oracle can't see that swap (the table registers the union of the entries' modules, the same addresses either way), so the unit test is the check. Their `vars` swapped instead pass `items-heavy-ps` (7,425 ticks, no difference): `vars` is read only when the importer takes a captured item's union (an item's code makes its member when it first reads it), so a match from the start of a trace never reads it; a captured state with items would.
- `melee-oracle --test ruleset`: all 12 pass. `--test session`, 15 named, item-heavy (`a_brick_blocks_item_pops_out_at_random`, `celebi_out_of_a_poke_ball`, `celebi_out_of_another_poke_ball`, `the_item_switch_saves`, `the_extra_rules_and_the_stage_switch`, `the_switches_left_as_they_come_in`, `a_vs_coin_match_on_yoshis_story`, `the_trophy_lottery_draws_and_saves`, `the_home_run_contest`, `multi_man_melee`, `all_star`, `adventure_game_over`, `target_test_cleared`, `master_hand_in_debug_vs`, `crazy_hand_in_debug_vs`): all pass. `--test single_player`, 15 named, its item runs (`each_mode_runs_to_its_first_stop`, `the_events_run_clean_to_their_cards_after` and the third, fourth, fifth and seventh rounds' events, `the_egg_carried_off_the_stage_runs_clean_to_its_card_after`, `the_egg_thrown_into_a_shield_runs_clean_to_its_card_after`, `wire_frames_hide_a_held_item_and_never_show_one`, and the Adventure shells: `adventures_giga_bowser_path_runs_clean_to_its_card_after`, `adventures_shells_on_mushroom_kingdom_run_clean`, `adventures_shells_met_by_shields_a_reflector_and_each_other_run_clean`, `adventures_like_like_ko_and_ledge_run_clean`, `adventures_polar_bear_flung_toward_the_screen_and_a_leaving_team_run_clean`, `adventures_red_shell_stopped_on_a_slope_runs_clean`): all pass.
- `sync-test --rollback 7 --fp hardware`: `items-ported-fd` (7,318 ticks checked) and `items-pokeball1-fd` (7,318), no difference.
- melee-sim's unit tests (the registry's own: each entry at its index, each kind's table callbacks in its own entry's modules, each entry's callbacks in the table; the callback table's; the items audit, which now holds `it/registry`), melee-player's `every_offered_stage_plays_with_items`, `ticks_and_restores_dont_allocate` at one thread, the ledger's `--check` (19,827 of 19,827; the same 18,078 `#[doc(alias)]` attributes before and after, a sorted list diffed; only `docs/port-ledger.csv`'s line numbers moved), `fmt` and `clippy -D warnings`.
- **Speed (indicative, this PC with other work on it):** `melee-bench native --runs 5 fox-fox-fd fox-marth-ps`, base (`f2b6d007`) and branch alternating in three rounds, median of the 15 runs: `fox-fox-fd` 4,387 frames/s before, 4,315 after (runs 3,916–4,674 and 3,694–4,700); `fox-marth-ps` 3,544 before, 3,721 after (3,143–3,918 and 3,165–3,915). Within the runs' spread: the registry is read once, when the callback table is built, and the table holds the same addresses.

### Task 12, wave 2: the stages' names

**Done (2026-11-20, `refactor/rename-stages` from `7871ac5b`, batch 10).** Sarah: "agreed, i am generally team rename" and "sure, i said rename freely". `game::gr`'s own names by offset are gone, and its stage modules take their stages' names; each old name stays findable as a list-form `#[doc(alias("..."))]` on what replaced it, as in [wave 3](#task-12-wave-3-the-items-names).

| Was | Now |
| --- | --- |
| 509 fields of the port's own structs named by offset: each stage's `GroundVars` members (`KongoBarrelVars::xC4`, `CorneriaVars::x108`, `RCruiseVars::x14` to `x38`, `Inishie1Vars::x100`), their entries (`AcidState::x00`, `GreensBlock::x0`, `YorsterBlock::x04`, `BigBluePlatform::x18`, `RCruisePlank::x2`), Mute City's cars (`Car::x0`), Brinstar's bubbles (`Bubble::x08`), the fighters' devices' counts (`FtDevices::x6570`, `x6578`) and `Globals::onett_x69c4` | named from their docs and uses (`turn`, `guns_state`, `tilt`, `platform_a`, `state`, `bits`, `index`, `swing`, `coll_joint`, `distance`, `x`, `coll_count`, `wind_count`, `onett_unread`). A word nothing writes is `unwritten`, one written and never read `unread` (or `cleared`, where its setup only clears it). The docs keep the offsets, and the comparer's labels stay the original's offsets |
| 17 functions named after the `stage_info` field they read or write (`ground::x724_and_step`, `set_x6d8`, `x740`, `x98`, `grlib::x708`, `stage::cam_x20`) | named from their callers: `acid_level_and_step`, `set_route_event`, `cars_state`, `bgm`, `trophy_coin_wait`, `cam_pan_rate` and the rest |
| 14 locals named by the offset of the field they copy (`x110`, `x34`, Yoshi's Island's blocks' `x04` and `x08`) | the fields' new names, or what they compute |
| 36 stage modules named by the decompilation's file names (`kongo`, `oldkongo`, `izumi`, `pura`, `yorster`, `inishie1`, `zebes`, `last`, `pushon`, `figure`, `heal`) | the stages' names (`kongo_jungle`, `kongo_jungle_64`, `fountain_of_dreams`, `poke_floats`, `yoshis_island`, `mushroom_kingdom`, `brinstar`, `final_destination`, `race_to_the_finish`, `trophy_stages`, `rest_area`); the old name is an alias on the `mod` line. The docs' paths and links follow |

The renames were scripted, one kind per commit: the fields by a map (definitions renamed, rustc's errors placing every use), the functions and the modules likewise by rustc's errors, the docs' and comments' qualified paths by pattern, the locals within their blocks. In all, the module's uses of offset names fell from 6,026 to 796 (the same scan as [naming](#naming)'s), and almost all of the 796 are the generated structs' fields (`unk8C`, `x11_flags`, `stage_info.x724`), which are wave 6's.

**Left in `game::gr`.** `ground::param_x4`, the getter of `GroundParam::x4`, which nothing but leaf-check reads (named when wave 6 names the field); the padding fields (`pad28`, `pad_ca`, `pad_74`: bytes nothing writes, named as padding); `GrKind::Unk23`, `Unk26`, `Unk35` and `StKind::Unk328`, the decompilation's names for kinds no stage uses; locals holding coordinates (`x0`, `x1`) or a caller's argument (`x50`); `gr::zako`'s three fields and two locals, left to `refactor/dedup-enemies-2`, which owns the spawner until it lands; the `*Vars` structs' and `VarsKind`'s names, which follow the decompilation's union members; the helper modules' names (`anime`, `grlib`, `ground`, `fzerocar`, `zako`), which aren't stages; and `tools/capture`'s constants named by address (`GRONETT_X69C4`).

**Checks.**

- **The whole state, tick by tick:** 45 replays, a replay per stage and more: task 3's 32 (the benchmark's Vanilla replays, `stadium-slippi`, `gnw-zelda-ys-4e9d13cb --heap`, `roy-puff-fod`, `ics-fox-dl-a7365e16 --heap` and every `stage-*` diff, 23) and the stage pairs' 13 more on Corneria, Venom and the Kongo Jungles (`long-*`, `taunt-*`, `cpu-*`, `barrel-*`, `items-box-kongo-jungle`). The base's oracle (`7871ac5b`) ran each one's full diff, every traced tick compared with no difference and no stale read, writing a per-tick whole-state golden; the branch's oracle checked each golden: all 45, 674,953 ticks, every tick's state hash the same. The goldens' coverage line was set to the branch's, since the comparer's sources changed only by the renames in `tools/capture` (`stage.rs`'s fields and paths, `izumi.rs`'s and `stadium.rs`'s module paths); the state hash's own sources (`golden.rs`, `diff.rs`, `mirror*.rs`, `state_eq.rs`, `melee-derive`) are unchanged, and the hash reads fields in order, not by name.
- **Sessions:** task 3's set on the worker (each compares every tick against its trace): `melee-oracle --test ruleset`, all 12 pass; `--test session`, its 22 stage-heavy and 1P sessions (`target_test_from_the_menu`, `target_test_cleared`, `the_home_run_contest`, `multi_man_melee`, `all_star`, `single_player_setups`, `single_player_intros`, `adventure_game_over`, `race_in_debug_vs`, `the_fzero_race_in_debug_vs`, the debug cutscenes and endings, the menus' matches on three stages, the selects, the random stage rule, the extra rules and the stage switch, the tournament tie, Master Hand and Crazy Hand in debug VS, the attract match on Brinstar, Rainbow Cruise's blocks): all 22 pass; `--test single_player`, its 6 (`each_mode_runs_to_its_first_stop`, Adventure's Giga Bowser path and Mushroom Kingdom's shells, Sarah's All-Star clear, the events, Classic's clear): all 6 pass.
- The ledger's `--check`: 19,827 of 19,827. The sorted `#[doc(alias = "...")]` list is the same 18,078 lines before and after; 562 list-form aliases were added (509 fields, 17 functions, 36 modules) and none removed. `docs/port-ledger.csv` changed only by paths and line numbers.
- `cargo check --workspace --all-targets`, melee-sim's unit tests (174 passed, 2 ignored; the roster's and the single-player modes' audits read the stage modules' sources by path, so their paths follow the modules), melee-player's `roster_tests`, leaf-check's `stage` (62) and `stages` (177), `fmt` and `clippy -D warnings`. No type in the state changed, only names, so the allocation test wasn't rerun.

### Task 12, wave 3: the items' names

**Done (2026-11-20, `refactor/rename-items` from `510e5139`, batch 8).** Sarah, 2026-11-20: "agreed, i am generally team rename." `game::it`'s names by address and the port's own names by offset are gone; each old name stays findable as a `#[doc(alias)]` on what replaced it. The aliases of renamed items use rustdoc's list form, `#[doc(alias("BombHei"))]`, which the port ledger doesn't read: its `#[doc(alias = "NAME")]` marks a port of the function `NAME`, and a ledger `--check` refuses a name ssbm-rs has no function for.

| Was | Now |
| --- | --- |
| `ItKind`'s 237 variants spelled the decompilation's development names (`BombHei`, `Kuriboh`, `Kyasarin`, `Unk4`, `SeakNeedleThrow`) | 165 of them take the names players know: the in-game item, move, character, enemy and Pokémon names, or a plain description for internal kinds (`BobOmb`, `Goomba`, `Birdo`, `VsCoin`, `SheikNeedleThrown`); the other 72 keep theirs (`Capsule`, `FoxLaser`, `Lugia`, `Invalid1`). No new name was another kind's old one (`Coin` was kind 159, the trophy, so kind 158, the Coin match's coin, is `VsCoin` and 159 `Trophy`), so code written against the old names fails to build rather than naming another kind. `tools/tables/item_tables.py` takes the names from its `RENAME_KINDS` |
| The 176 state tables `item_tables.py` transcribes, named by their symbols' addresses (`STATES_IT_803F5468`) | named by the first kind whose logic table names them (`STATES_CAPSULE`; a table several kinds share lists them all in its doc), or the code that sets them (`STATES_KIRBY_INHALED`, `STATES_YOSHI_TONGUED`, `STATES_KIRBY_TONGUED`), the old name and the symbol as aliases |
| The logic table's last callback, the decompilation's `evt_unk`: `ItemLogic::evt_unk`, `it::evt_unk`, 62 `evt_unk` ports, 105 `EVT_UNK` address constants | `forget` (what it does: a fighter's unload runs it on every item, which forgets the fighter; 61 kinds' ports already had the name): `ItemLogic::forget`, `it::forget`, `FORGET` |
| 82 fields of the port's own structs named by offset: the stage items' and enemies' variables (`ArwingLaserVars::xDF4`, `TincleVars::x5c`, `HeihoVars::x54`), the enemies' common words (`zako::Common`, `CommonVars::x8`), `Laid::x24`, `Spawner::x14`, `Globals::x6d00` and `x6d08` | named from their docs and uses (`parent`, `float_height`, `food`, `normal`, `damage_behavior`, `unused`, `warp_star_last_anim`, `foods_spawned`); the docs keep the offsets, and the comparer's labels stay the original's offsets |
| 19 functions named after the bit they touch (`set_x1b`, `x1_3`, `flag_all_x3`, `special_x0`, `xdc0_has`) | named by what the bit does, from its readers (`set_random_push`, `turns_to_face`, `pause_all`, `metal_box_frames`, `script_flags_have`) |

The renames were scripted, one kind of rename per commit: the kinds by a map over every `ItKind::` use; the tables by the generator; the fields by renaming the definitions and letting rustc's errors place every use. The entries' names for kinds 158 and 159 and the registry's docs had the two coins swapped, and called Topi "Ottosea"; they are corrected.

**Left in `game::it`.** 154 constants named by offset into the dat files' attributes (`data::common::X2C` and its 36 siblings, `attr::X68`, the kinds' own, such as `linkboomerang`'s 19 and `seakchain`'s 17), which want each one's uses read; five setters of `Item`'s flags whose meaning the port doesn't show (`zako::set_x1c`, `clear_x1c` and `clear_x1e`, `lib2725::set_xd09`, `data`'s `x1_4`: nothing but the comparer reads `x1C`, `xD09` and `x1A`); 122 locals named by the field they hold; `VarsKind`'s variants and the `*Vars` structs' names, which follow the decompilation's union members and the generated structs (wave 6); and the kinds' module files (`kinds::hassam`, `kinds::lipstick`), left as the decompilation's file names (the enemies' thirteen renamed since, in [task 11's enemies](#task-11-the-enemies-done-2026-11-20-refactordedup-enemies-from-75bc50b4-round-two-2026-11-21-refactordedup-enemies-2-from-662858bf) and its [round two](#task-11-the-enemies-round-two)). The generated structs' fields (`Item::xDC8_word`) are wave 6's.

**Checks.**

- **The whole state, tick by tick** (task 4's 82 replays: every `items-*` and `stage-*` diff, the items-on CPU and Ice Climbers diffs, the coin matches and the benchmark's Vanilla replays). The goldens task 4's merge wrote were checked by the base's oracle (`510e5139`, its coverage line set): all 82, 959,092 ticks, every tick's state hash the same. The branch's oracle checked the same goldens: all 82, 959,092 ticks, the same. The coverage line was set to the branch's, since the comparer's sources changed only by the renames in `tools/capture/src/items.rs` (kind names and three fields); the state hash's own sources are unchanged, and the hash reads fields in order, not by name.
- `item_tables.py` regenerates `tables_gen.rs` exactly (after `rustfmt`) at each commit that touches it.
- The ledger's `--check`: 19,827 of 19,827. The sorted `#[doc(alias = "...")]` list is the same 18,080 lines before and after; 613 list-form aliases were added and none removed, every renamed kind's old name among them. `docs/port-ledger.csv` changed only by paths and line numbers, and `ledger/checks.txt` by the module's path (`forget::` for `evt_unk::`).

The survey's 616 and 152 counted each definition twice (the struct's line and `Default`'s). Each keeps its C name as a list-form `#[doc(alias("x1964"))]` on the generated field.

| Was | Now |
| --- | --- |
| `RENAME_FIELDS`, one map for every struct (empty) | `tools/structgen/names.py`'s `FIELD_NAMES`, by struct (C name to Rust name), read through `config.py`; structgen checks that every name it maps is a field of its struct and that no two fields end up with one name, writes the alias on each renamed field, and labels it in the comparer as the new name with the C name beside it (`min_hitlag (x1964)`, `cpu.behavior (x18)`) |
| The fields named by offset (`fp.x1964`, `fp.x221C_b6`, `fp.cpu.x18`, `fp.x8_spawnNum`) | named from the decompilation's half-names where it has one (`spawn_num`, `scale`, `kb_vel`, `ledge_cooldown`, `attack_id`, `jumps_used`), else from the ports' docs and uses (`min_hitlag`, `in_hitstun`, `hit_status`, `intangible_frames`, `hitboxes`, `throw_hitboxes`, `effect_queue`, `frames_since_z`, `main_overlay`, `partner`, `grab_kinds`, `grab_immune`, `cpu.behavior`, `cpu.destination`, `cpu.may_shield`, `cpu.hurt_ahead`); the map's comments say what the less obvious ones hold |
| Hand-written labels of fighter fields in the comparer (`{path}.x588`, `{path}.x20A4.shadow`, `x203C`, `fighter {a} x60C`) and the strings that match labels or state paths (`session.rs`'s playback-cache filter, `cosmetic_tests`' `DISPLAY_ONLY`) | the same form (`light (x588)`, `shadow (x20A4).shadow`, `second_dobjs (x203C)`, `effect_queue (x60C)`), and the new names |

**The rename is re-runnable.** `tools/structgen/rename_readers.py` regenerates `structs.rs` and `mirror_gen.rs` from the map (with `rustfmt`), then runs `cargo check --workspace --all-targets` round after round and renames each use rustc reports as a missing field of a renamed struct (`E0609`, `E0560`, `E0026`; a shorthand field keeps its binding, `new: old`), until none is left: rustc places each use by its type, so `Item::x18` or a local `x14` is untouched. It ends by listing lines that still name an old field after a `.` in code `cargo check` didn't build or in strings. Here it was run from the base's sources with the final map: 3,971 uses renamed in eight rounds (the first 3,891 in melee-sim, then its dependents as each built), no other error, and six lines listed, five of them strings fixed by hand (the labels and paths above) and one another struct's. Running it on another branch's tree (joints, rollback, modes, the fast renderer) is [porting's recipe](porting.md#names-and-the-ledger): bring in `tools/structgen` and run it; only uses of the renamed fields change, so the merge's conflicts are the branches' own. The commit touches 258 files: structgen's four, the generated two, 236 more in melee-sim, melee-render's four (afterimage, draw, magnify, material), melee-player's two tests, and the tools' readers (the comparer, the importer, leaf-check's `camera`, `effects`, `fighter` and `foxlike`, the bench's `ruleset_tests` and `cpu_sweep`, the oracle's session filter).

**Left.** Placeholder names for fields nothing reads, as earlier waves name them: `Fighter`'s padding and never-touched bytes (`pad_105` to `pad_107`, `pad_61e`, `pad_119f`, `pad_1674`, `pad_2044`, `pad_206e`, `pad_2102`, `pad_2103`, `pad_210d`, `pad_21fd`, `unused_2022`, `unused_2229_b2`, `unused_222a_b2`, `b3`, `b5` to `b7`, and `unused_221f_b7`, which only the post-frame flag copy reads), `CpuFighter`'s (`pad_c9`, `pad_ed`, `unused_fa_b0`, `unused_fb_b1` to `b7`, and `unread_24`, `unread_28`, which only the spawn writes). Some names rest on one reader and are best guesses (`CpuFighter`'s `attack_reach_0`/`1`, `stage_read_count`, `hold_choice`; `Fighter`'s `alt_metal`, a metal look of its own that nothing turns on, `blast_immune_state`, `reflect_kept`): the map can rename them again by the same script. The nested structs' fields (`fp.dmg.x1830_percent`, `fp.x2070`'s bits, `Fighter_x1614_t`'s) are other structs', wave 6's next steps (tasks 22 and 23). Functions named after a renamed field (`cmd_x221e_b1`, `x594_bit`, `init_x1930`) and comments citing offsets are unchanged: the comments' offsets are the aliases'.

**Checks.**

- **The whole state, tick by tick**, on the verify list's 330 replay diffs (task 21's goldens and scripts). The base's comparer coverage had moved, so the goldens, their coverage line set to the base's, were checked by the base's oracle (`8ae7b39e`): all 330, **3,552,272 ticks, every tick's state hash the same**, no stale read. The branch's oracle checked them with the coverage line set to the branch's (its comparer's sources changed only by the renames and the labels; `golden.rs`, `diff.rs`, `state_eq.rs` and `melee-derive` are unchanged, `mirror.rs` only by its test, and the hash reads fields in order, not by name): all 330, 3,552,272 ticks, the same.
- **The labels:** `game::mirror::label_tests::a_renamed_fields_label_gives_the_originals_name` (a renamed field of each struct and a bit, beside a field that kept its name). **Mutation:** the collision resolve's reset of `min_hitlag` to 0.5 (reverted) makes `fox-fox-fd`'s diff differ in its first tick, printed as `p_link 8 #0 fighter.min_hitlag (x1964): ours 0.5, theirs 0.0` for both fighters.

Names come from the decompilation's half-names (`xDD4_flightTimer` `flight_timer`, `x24_angle` `angle`), else from the ports' docs and uses, which phase one of the work found by placeholders: the script was first run with each field mapped to a unique placeholder (`w6_ip_xca0`), so every use of a field could be listed by name with its function and its decompilation lines; the final map was then run from the base's sources. The map's comments say what the less obvious ones hold.

**Placeholders.** A field nothing reads or writes is `unused_*` (123: most of the hookshot's and the green shell's, which the decompilation lays out but the code never touches; `Item_FtTrack`'s seven words) or the decompilation's padding `pad_*` (17); one written and never read is `unread_*` (32: `Item`'s `xA0`, `xC94` and `xC98`, a link's `x20`, and kinds' words their spawns clear, such as the sword's `x0`, `x50`, `x54` and the charged shots' `xE2C` to `xE34`). Kirby's inhaled item's variables (`itKirby2F23_ItemVars`) are read as the union's bytes by offset (`kinds::kirbyinhale`'s `off`), so their names (`to_mouth_x`, `pull_speed_x`, `hold_time`) come from that module's docs. Some names rest on one reader and are best guesses (`Item`'s `spawn_origin`, `met_fighter`, the flag bytes' themes, the jolts' `placed_frames`): the map can rename them again with the same script. Three fields the decompilation already calls `pad_*` with their offsets keep their names (rustdoc refuses an alias equal to the name).

**The script.** `rename_readers.py` was unchanged: it already renames every struct `FIELD_NAMES` holds, so `names.py`'s new maps (`ITEM`, `ITEM_FLAGS`, `ITEM_VARS` and the rest, with `UNUSED_ITEM_FIELDS`) bring a branch from before step 1 or step 2 up to both in one run. Run from the base's sources with the final map: 5,826 uses renamed in eleven rounds (melee-sim first, then its dependents as each built), no error left, and nine lines listed: two strings in the oracle's session filters that match item flag labels (`item.phys_flags (xDD1_flag)`, `item.status_flags (xDD0_flag)`), fixed by hand, and seven other structs'. The commit touches 223 files: structgen's map, the generated two, 202 more in melee-sim (179 in `game::it`, and `game::mirror`'s label test), melee-render's material setup, melee-player's record test, the comparer (`compare.rs`, `items.rs`), the oracle (`birdo_sweep.rs`, `session.rs`), the bench's `ruleset_tests`, nine of leaf-check's tests (`items`, `items_monsters`, `items_on`, `items_shoot`, `effects`, `kirby`, `samus_swing`, `stages`, `yoshi`) and `docs/port-ledger.csv`'s line numbers. The coordinator reruns the script over `refactor/dedup-held-items` at merge time (porting's recipe).

**Left.** Functions and locals named after a renamed field (`lib2725::set_xd09`, `zako::set_x1c`, `clear_x1e`, locals such as `x9c`) and comments citing offsets are unchanged: the comments' offsets are the aliases'. The nested structs other structs share (`HitCapsule` in `ItemHitbox`, `CollData`, `CommandInfo`, `_itECB`, `Struct207C`, `CameraBoxFlags`) and the port's own hand-written item structs (`ToolsVars`, which keeps its offset label) are task 23's or the port's.

<a id="fighter-and-item-owned"></a>**`Fighter` and `Item` owned** (2026-11-23, `refactor/own-fighter-item`, after step 0's merge). Sarah, asked whether to start on both tonight: "Yes, both". Each with the structs only it holds, in three commits, each checked on the 19-diff sample before the next:

| Commit | Structs (C name, Rust name), home |
| --- | --- |
| `4d076d2f` | the fighter's parts, `game::ft::types`: `Fighter_x221B` (`FighterStateFlags`), `Fighter_x8B0_t` (`PartAnim`), `Fighter_x1614_t` (`StaminaMarker`), `Fighter_x1670_t` (`LedgePoint`), `Fighter_x20B0_t` (`AfterimagePoints`), `Fighter_x5F4_arr` (`ModelVariant`), `Fighter_input` (`FighterInput`), `FtInputTimers`, `U8Vec2`, `IntVec2`, `itPickup` (`ItemPickup`), `FtPartsVis`, `DObjList`, `FighterHurtCapsule`, `Fighter_ReflectAttr` (`ReflectAttrs`), `Fighter_AbsorbAttr` (`AbsorbAttrs`); its damage, `dmg` (`FighterDamage`), with `lb_80014638_arg0_t` (`StepFeet`), `HitResult` and `Struct2074` (`AttackRecord`); its attributes, `ftCo_DatAttrs` (`CommonAttrs`), `ftCo_DatAttrs_xBC_t` (`EggAttrs`), `_SmashAttr` (`SmashAttrs`); the computer player's `CpuFighter` with Nana's input record `Fighter_x1A88_xFC_t` (`PartnerInput`); and `ColorOverlay` (`lb::colanim`, which items and the background flash hold too) and `LbShadow` (`lb::shadow`) |
| `bb50d294` | `Fighter` (425 fields), `game::ft::types::fighter` |
| `ea3a4091` | `Item` (180 fields) and its parts, `game::it::types`: `flag32` and `flag32_flags` (`ItemFlags`, `ItemFlagBits`), `Item_xDCC_flag` (`ItemStateFlags`), `CameraBoxFlags` and `CameraBoxFlags_x0` (`ItemHitFlags`, `ItemHitBits`: the decomp types the item's hit flags with the camera box's), `ItemHitbox`, `ItemModStruct` (`ItemOutline`), `xB6C_t` (`DynamicsPoint`), `lb_80014638_arg1_t` (`StepBox`) |

Every renamed type keeps its C name as a `#[doc(alias)]`; the fields keep theirs and their names' comments (from `names.py` and `names_step3.py`, whose maps for these structs went). The conversion was a script over the generated structs (scratch `own.py`: each field's `/// +0x..` offset an `#[at(..)]`, `bits(..)` for a bitfield, `union` where the generated mirror read bytes), then compiled, the layout check run, and each struct's compiler padding declared. `structs.rs` went from 13,537 lines to 9,228 and `mirror_gen.rs` from 29,081 to 19,347; the hand-written modules are 3,843 lines.

Left generated, and why: the structs the fighter and the item share with others (`HitCapsule` and `HitVictim`, which items' hitboxes hold too; `HurtCapsule`, which the items' counted hurtboxes hold; `CommandInfo`; `Struct207C`, `S32Vec2`, `Vec2`, `_GXColor`; `ft_800898B4_t`, which the stale-move table holds; `_itECB`, which `Item_FtTrack` holds), and the **per-kind variables**: the items' `*_ItemVars` (members of the hand-written `ItemVars` union) stay generated, since structgen's tracked-variable machinery (`it::Var`, which knows whether a new item has written a byte yet, and its stale-read report) is what makes them exact over a block's leftovers, and owning them would mean writing that by hand for about 90 structs no reader names by type; the fighters' `FighterVars` and `MotionVars` were already hand-written byte arrays (`ft::vars`).

Two changes to the derive's support, both in `game::mirror` and `melee-derive`: `Mirror` now covers `RawPtr`, `Kept<T>` (compared and laid out only where known, as `config.KEPT`'s fields were) and every handle kind the generated mirrors passed; and the padding is a run of bits, `pad(AT, BYTES)` or `pad(AT, bits(FIRST, COUNT))`, since `HitResult` ends its bitfield one bit into the byte at `0x4` and the compiler pads the other seven.

**Types.** Named, not retyped: the flag words were already one `bool` per bit with every bit a field (named or `unused_*`), which is what a flag set would give, leftovers included, without touching their readers; the kinds stay the game's integers as the registry's design has them (`Fighter::kind`, compared with `FighterKind`). An enum for `ground_or_air` (the decomp's `GroundOrAir`), the hottest candidate, would touch about 360 reads across every fighter and item module other branches are changing, and the speed rule wants each representation change measured, which the build's 3% placement artifact (below) would hide; it waits for a quiet tree and a clean bench. `CpuFighter`'s pointers into itself and the fighter's spliced variables keep their hand-written types.

**Heap image and snapshots:** `Fighter` and `Item` are `LAY_ROOTS`; their `LayOut` is the derive's `lay` now, the same writes in the same order as the generated one's (`Kept` fields laid only where known, as before), and `SPLICE`'s types inside them (`DynamicBoneSets`, `ActionTree`, `SecondTree`, `FighterVars`, `MotionVars`, `ItemDynamicBones`, `IgnoreId`, `ItemHurtboxes`, `Script` in the color overlay, `CpuFighter`'s self-pointers) are field types. `KEPT`'s `Item` words (`x8`, `x21` to `x23`) are `Kept` fields. No file in `game::heap` or the heap image's chunking changed (`perf/vanilla-speed`'s).

**Checks.**

- **The whole state, tick by tick**, on the 19-diff sample after each commit: 252,871 ticks the same each time. At the head (`ea3a4091`), all 330 of the verify list's replay diffs against step 0's goldens (coverage line set to the head's oracle): **3,552,272 ticks, every tick's state hash the same**.

- melee-sim's unit tests at `ea3a4091`: 188 passed and `items_audit_reaches_nothing_unaccounted` failed, on the new `it/types` modules missing from the items audit's module list (presentation data, which lists every `game::it` file); the list gained them after, with `layout_check::proof::a_pad_of_bits_covers_what_a_bitfield_leaves` (a bit pad passes where a bitfield's byte leaves bits over, and fails over the bitfield's own bit).
- On the worker at `994430fe` (this PC wound down for Sarah, 2026-11-23): melee-sim's unit tests (190 passed, 2 ignored); `--test session` at two threads filtered to `item`, `celebi`, `computer`, `bot`, `classic`, `adventure`, `single_player`, `all_star` (12: the brick block's item, Celebi's two Poké Balls and none without the records, the item switch's save, computer players at three levels, training's unload, the reach rounds' bot sessions, Adventure's game over, All-Star, the single-player setups and intros: all pass, 72 minutes); `--test ruleset` at one thread (all 12 pass, 39 minutes, none skipped); the workspace's `clippy --all-targets -D warnings`; `fmt`; the ledger's `--check`.
- **Speed:** the 3% step 0 measured is own-structs' build, not its code: `perf` puts most of it in `gekko_fp::paired::ps_madds0` (2,432 self samples at `390dbedd`, 3,169 at own-structs' tip, in about 32,000), whose source didn't change; the music-switchover branch saw the same symbol move, and forcing the helpers inline lost speed elsewhere, so it is code placement, with run-to-run spread near 3.5% that session. The coordinator judges speed on the PGO build after the merge. Base `390dbedd` against `ea3a4091` on the worker (`melee-bench native --runs 5`, three alternated rounds, exclusive), medians in frames/s: `fox-fox-fd` 2706, 2752, 2703 against 2620, 2665, 2663 (about 2% fewer); `fox-marth-ps` 2342, 2342, 2299 against 2240, 2282, 2275 (about 3% fewer). Its `perf` profile is the same story: `ps_madds0` 2,405 self samples at the base, 3,068 at the head, in about 31,000; no function of the owned structs (their clones, comparisons, mirrors or layouts) shows among the profile's symbols, and the rest of the top symbols are flat.
