### Task 5, the ruleset interface

**Done (2026-11-20, `refactor/ruleset-interface` from `fd77569a`, batch 8's `refactor/char-registry`).** Every place gameplay differs by ruleset asks `game::ruleset`, one function per choice grouped by the [hook point](#ruleset-hook-points) it serves, and `Rules`' fields are private to it, so the compiler holds that nothing else tests a rule ([porting](porting.md#rules)).

**The rulesets as data.** `Rules` (moved from `game::state`, which re-exports it, with `Ruleset`) keeps its shape: which of the Slippi ruleset's rules a match runs (the determinism fixes, UCF's version, neutral spawns, wobbling prevention, off-screen limits, the freeze glitch fix, the version of Slippi's codes, players' codes, the 1P C-stick rule), as a replay's code list or the session's ruleset turns them on. So the state, its hash, the comparer and saved states are unchanged. What each version does is a table the functions read, not state:

- `Fixes`, the determinism fixes by version: `VANILLA`, `SLIPPI_3_19`, `SLIPPI_3_16` (3.17's word for word, 3.15's too) and `SLIPPI_3_14` (3.16's without WhispyBlowDirFix). Their choices: Init Player and Stage Data, FreezeDeadUpFallPhysics, Final Destination's background (`FdBackground`: draws from the seed, skipped, keeps the seed), Pokémon Stadium's monitor, freeze and synchronous loads, WhispyBlowDirFix, NanaDeterminism (`NanaDeterminism`: leftovers, r30 cleared, r30 and r5), and melee-hd's own under Slippi's rules: the EFB copies kept on the GPU, the playback note (design call 9) and the Super Scope's stand-in.
- `UcfCodes` by version: `UCF_OFF`, `UCF_0_8` (dashback, shield drop, tumble), `UCF_0_84` (all eight).
- `Session` by the session's `Ruleset`: `VANILLA_SESSION`, `SLIPPI_SESSION` (the float mode, the card and developer setting, the general codes, Skip Memcard Prompt, live matches, DynamicsFix everywhere, the rules every match runs).

`the_tables_choose_as_the_versions_do` and `the_sessions_choose_as_the_rulesets_do` hold the tables to the branches they replaced; `rulesets_are_asked_through_the_module` fails on a `Ruleset` compared by name outside the module (the session format's decoding builds one and is allowed).

**What became calls** (counted by making the fields private at the base and taking the compiler's list; the [hook-point table](#ruleset-hook-points) names each site's function): 64 reads of `Rules`' fields at 60 sites in 35 files of melee-sim (2 more in `game::ucf`), 5 tests of the session's `Ruleset` in 4 files, 9 tests of playback's presence that decide a rule (`Game::runs_playback_codes`: the frame count, `CostumeBoundCheck`, `AllocSceneBuffer`, the recorder's virtual processes, the playback note's follower; `runs_sound_codes`; `dynamics_fix` and `copies_reach_memory`, moved in), and in the tools 11 (the corpus run's rules name, now `Rules::short_name`; the bench's per-version run, `Rules::with_fixes`; the comparer's SDI colors; the oracle's playback note and C-stick mutation, `with_c_stick_1p`; the power-on test). The survey's 21 playback sites at `f0fa4c81` also counted playback's own state (its frame reads, start seed, sound log and settling in `game::playback`, `vs_match`, `live` and `gm::scene`, the pad library's playback flag): those are the playback set's machinery, not a rule, and stay as they are. The players' codes keep their decoding in `game::player_codes` and are asked through the module like the rest.

**Task 6's seam.** The allocator model's on/off tests (41 in 27 files: `heap::image_on()` and `heap::with(|_| ()).is_some()`) test the lent model's thread-local state, not `Rules`, and weren't routed. Task 6 puts the model behind the ruleset: a choice in `Fixes` (or `Session`) and a `Game` function in `game::ruleset` that those sites call, the tripwire where a Slippi match reaches the model.

**Checks.**

- **The whole state, tick by tick** (task 2's way): the base's oracle (`fd77569a`) checked task 2's goldens of all 330 of the verify list's replay diffs (every character, the bench matches, Slippi 3.19, 3.16 and 3.17 replays with UCF 0.84 and 0.8, players' codes, the stages and items): 3,552,272 ticks, every tick's hash matched. The branch's oracle checked the same goldens: **3,552,272 ticks, every tick's state hash the same**. The goldens' coverage line was set to the branch's: the comparer's sources changed only by `tools/capture/src/compare.rs` reading the SDI colors code through the module; the state hash's own sources are unchanged. **The Slippi-ruleset sessions and live matches:** a scratch build of each oracle (not committed) logged the whole `Game`'s state hash after every tick of `melee-oracle session TRACE INPUTS --all --heap --after-handback` on `matches-a`, `matches-b`, `pause-fd`, `pause-far`, `slomo`, `player-fd`, `menu-tour`, `classic-cstick` and `vs-menus` (their live matches and past the handbacks included): 63,966 ticks, every hash the same before and after.

### Task 6, Vanilla's modelling behind the ruleset

**Done (2026-11-20, `refactor/vanilla-behind-ruleset` from `6a7835e5`).** Under the Slippi ruleset a match of its own, live or a `.slp`'s playback, runs none of Vanilla's memory modelling (the allocator model, the heap's image, the menus' layouts, r5 through the computer player's frame) but where Slippi Dolphin itself reads what the model gives, one test of the ruleset decides it, and a tripwire stops any path that still reaches the model.

**The one test.** The versions' tables (`Fixes`) carry a `Memory` choice: `Modelled` for Vanilla, Slippi 3.16 and 3.14 (whose NanaDeterminism clears r30 only: r5 can hold an item's or a stage's joints' address, which only the model gives), `WhatSlippiReads` for 3.19. `Game::models_memory` reads it: always true outside a match of its own (a session's menus and the matches they keep, Sudden Death, the single-player modes and the attract match, which run Vanilla's menus under either ruleset), and under 3.19 in a replay's playback or a live match only where the match started the model. Every step's loan asks it (`Game::lend_model`, at the 18 places a step lent the model; `Match::start` adds the settings' own choice). Where it's false the step lends nothing: the state's model stays `Heap`'s default, untouched. The 41 on/off tests stay as Vanilla's own distinctions (a session's image or none, a match's model on or off by its players: `heap::image_on`, and `heap::modelled` for the two `heap::with(|_| ())` probes), and a Slippi match's loan answers them all false.

**Where the model still runs under 3.19.** `Settings::from_replay` decides it from the rules (`Settings::playback_needs_model`, `Rules::playback_runs_model`), so a `.slp`'s playback and a live match decide alike: on with Samus in the match, on any port (Sarah, 2026-11-18: "Yes, model on"), off otherwise. The tools that compare a match's addresses with the original's turn it on (`Settings::keep_model`): the oracle's `diff`, `record` (unless `--heap off`) and `session` (its live matches, as before, unless `--no-live-model`). `melee-bench corpus` takes the rules' choice by default (`--heap rules`).

**The tripwire.** In a step that models no memory, `heap::with` and `heap::start_scene_lent` stop with the caller's place (`the allocator model reached at FILE:LINE in a match whose ruleset models no memory`, `#[track_caller]`). Two kinds of model call don't stop, since every match runs them and nothing comes back from them but addresses: `heap::mirror`, for what mirrors the original's allocations and frees (the pools' objects and limits, the scene's lists and buffers, an archive's blocks, the collision arrays, refraction's images, the dynamics links, the item tables' arrays, playback's buffers), and `heap::image`, for the heap image's bookkeeping and reads (claims, words laid, copy hints, a file's words), which find no image, as a replay's match with the model has none. The hooks the pools and classes call (`class_alloc`, `obj_alloc`, `mem_alloc` and the rest) are `mirror`'s. Finding the paths: a scratch build that logged each place `with` was reached instead of stopping, over `data/bench`, Sarah's 203 replays and the oracle's 211 benchmark replays under 3.19's rules (`ssbm-rs/local/bench/oracle-*`: every stage, the items, the Super Scope, Samus's grapple, the Ice Climbers, the monsters), named 17 places (five of them a pool's free, reached through its callers: the list nodes, FObjs, AObjs, particles and generators); each was a mirror or the image's bookkeeping (now `mirror` or `image`), and with the tripwire armed the same 420 replays run with no stop from it. Changed call sites, by file: `hsd::pool` (alloc, free, limit, a slot's note), `hsd::gobj` (4 image claims), `hsd::export::with_image`, `lb::figatree`, `lb::bgflash`, `lb::dynamics`, `lb::refract`, `mp::mod` (the collision arrays), `game::gr::setup` (a file's render mode word), `game::gr::pokemon_stadium` (the transformation buffer), `game::it::spawn` (the pick tables), `game::ft::anim_buf` (copy hints), `game::ft::data` (archives; a new animation buffer's fill), `game::disc` (archives), `game::playback` (playback's buffers), `game::vs_match` (the scene's lists and text arena), `hsd::robj` (the expressions' buffer), `hsd::mobj` (the model probe). `Lent::take` takes the test's answer (`tools/oracle/src/birdo_sweep.rs` asks `Game::lend_model` too).

**A new animation buffer in a match that models no memory** holds a fresh heap's zeros, as the model's playback layout says (`Heap::fresh`): the match is the Slippi ruleset's own, from playback's fresh heap. Without the model it used to be unknown.

**r5.** Where NanaDeterminism clears r5 (3.19's, `Rules::follows_r5`) the computer player's frame hands the DI a word that stops if read (since the Slippi ruleset's task 5), and now the floor search's caller doesn't work out the r5 a stage's excluded-floor check leaves (`excluded_floor_r5`). The rest of the bookkeeping is the r5 that functions return, which the floor search computes as part of its own loop: no work left to skip.

**What Slippi itself reads.** Every place a Slippi-ruleset match reads an address or a leftover the model provides, and how it's decided (Slippi Dolphin's behaviour first; the [audit of 2026-11-18](slippi-ruleset.md) and task 7's are the evidence, the 3.19 runs above and the state comparison below the check):

| What reads it | What it reads | Under 3.19 | Decision |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A computer player's DI with no knockback (`ftCo_800AC5A0`, Nana's too) | its `Fighter*` in r30 and the r5 its frame left (stack addresses, an item's address, Mushroom Kingdom's and Venom's joints array) | NanaDeterminism clears both | never reached: the DI reads 0; the frame hands it a word that stops if read |
| The Super Scope's one-shot charge (`it_80291DAC`, `it_80291D38`) | its GObj's address as a level | no code fixes it | a narrow stand-in (`sscope::ADDRESS_STAND_IN`): every heap address takes the same branches; held by melee-bench's `slippi_super_scope_stand_in_matches_the_model` |
| Samus's bounced and hanging grapple swing (`it_802B7160`) | bit 19 of her `ThrowN` joint's address | no code fixes it; the address is playback's layout's | the model on for any match with Samus (`Settings::playback_needs_model`), live or played back; without it the read stops (`SwingSlot::Unmodeled`) |
| Big Blue's Falcon Flyer road search (`grBigBlue_801E8D64`) | two joints' addresses as floats | no code fixes it | a stand-in (`flyer_stale_pos`): any arena address puts the search on the same road piece |
| Pokémon Stadium's transformation buffer | its block's address | files are read into it; nothing branches on it | the frontend's address stands in |
| An animation buffer read past its end | the next block's bytes | | stops by name |
| The menus' heap bytes (`game::leftovers`: Brinstar's timer, Fountain of Dreams' star, Nana's ring, the collision stamp, Rainbow Cruise, the spawner, links, the fan) and the layouts `vanilla_vs`, `from_menus` | the heap's image | the match starts from playback's fresh heap; `Init Player Data` and `Init Stage Data` zero the fighter and stage blocks | never reached: a match of its own has no image, and its leftover readers take a fresh heap's values; the layouts are `Settings::vs_with`'s, Vanilla's |
| A new animation buffer's contents | the heap's bytes there | playback's fresh heap | a fresh heap's zeros (above) |
| An item's hitbox joint word before its script writes it (`HitJObj`) | the block's last item's joint's address | | never read before written (a read stops) |
| An item's variables and hurtboxes over its block's last item (`it::Var`, `alloc_stale`) | the last item's words, pointers among them | Melee's own; no code clears them | reached alike, the slots' order the pools' own (not the model's); a pointer word's address is unknown without the model, and a read of it stops |
| The recorded post-frame state word and items' misc bytes | heap addresses the recorder copies | playback never reads them | written as 0 without the model, accepted (follow-up 8 of [the Slippi ruleset](slippi-ruleset.md)) |
| HSD's ID table's address keys and the stack descriptors' keys (`id::address_key`, `stack_key`) | addresses | | the comparer's alone: nothing looks them up |

**Snapshots** under the Slippi ruleset hold no model: `objs.heap` is `Heap`'s default in every Slippi match without Samus (no OS heap, pieces, pools, classes or image), and since task 7 there are no per-address slots. `melee-bench snapshot-match` (indicative, this PC, the base's build against the branch's): Sarah's 3.19 replay `Game_20260129T130802` 2.19 and 2.22 MB at ticks 5,000 and 10,000 before, 2.14 and 2.17 MB after (2.3% smaller, 26 fewer allocations, clone 134 and 139 µs against 133 and 136); the Ice Climbers' 3.19 replay `Game_20260913T224137` 2.63, 2.73 and 2.73 MB at 3,000, 6,000 and 9,000 before, 2.56, 2.66 and 2.66 MB after (2.6% smaller, 26 to 28 fewer allocations, clone 166 to 176 µs against 160 to 166). The live matches already ran without the model; this is the `.slp`'s playback.

**Checks.**

- **The verify list's replay diffs, tick by tick**: the branch's oracle checked task 5's goldens of all 330 (every character, the bench matches, Slippi 3.19, 3.16 and 3.17 replays, players' codes, the stages and items): **3,552,272 ticks, every tick's state hash the same**, no stale read. The oracle's `diff` keeps the model in every match (`Settings::keep_model`), so Slippi replays compare as before; the goldens' coverage line was set to the branch's, since the comparer's sources changed only by that line in `tools/oracle/src/diff.rs`.
- **The Slippi-ruleset sessions and live matches** (task 5's scratch hash log, the whole `Game` after every tick of `melee-oracle session TRACE INPUTS --all --heap --after-handback`): `matches-a`, `matches-b`, `pause-fd`, `pause-far`, `slomo`, `player-fd`, `menu-tour`, `classic-cstick` and `vs-menus`, 63,966 ticks, every hash task 5's. As the player runs them (`--no-live-model`), the six with live matches against the base: every tick outside the live matches the same (menus, handbacks, Sudden Death, results), and inside them only the new animation buffers' fill differs (above): a scratch build with the old fill gives the base's hashes on every tick of `pause-fd` and `player-fd`.
- **What leaves a Slippi match's state, field by field** (a scratch tool, not committed: each replay played twice in lockstep, with the model as the base played it and as the rules now choose, `state_diffs` of the whole `Game` after every tick, the model itself left out): the verify list's 117 Slippi replays. The 104 under 3.16's and 3.14's rules and the one 3.19 replay with Samus (`hf/link-dd-a6`'s `diamond-diamond`) don't differ on any tick. The 12 under 3.19 without Samus (Sarah's 2026-01 and 2026-05 replays, 646 to 17,466 ticks, each to its end) differ in 45 to 55 places, 67 in all, every one an address the model gives or follows from one: each pool's addresses (`addrs`: GObjs, processes, fighters and their parts, attributes, DObj lists and animation buffers, items, item links and bone tables, effects, the HSD classes, particles, generators, app-SRTs, shadows, grounds, stage start hooks), the blocks the model places (the collision arrays and islands, the dynamics and wind blocks, the camera boxes, the background flash, the magnifier's buffers, the name tags' user data, a stage's archives and enemy spawner, Fountain of Dreams' and Stadium's buffers), the class objects' parts (a joint's `scl`, a camera's inverse view matrix, a texture's TEV and palette, a material's block, a shape's envelopes, a shadow's image), `ft.addresses`, an item hitbox's joint word (`left_at`), HSD's ID table's values and its address keys (`objs.ids.map`), the list nodes of the lights and generators, whose slots the model's addresses order where the free lists part (`Pool::reused_at`), and the bytes of an item's variables and hurtboxes over its block's last item where that item held a pointer (unknown both ways: their known bits are the same). No fighter, item, stage, camera, seed or other gameplay field differs on any tick.
- **The `.slp` corpus under the Slippi ruleset** with the tripwire armed (`melee-bench corpus`, 3.19's rules, the model as the rules choose): `data/bench`, Sarah's 203 replays and the oracle's 211 benchmark replays, 420 in all: 389 to their ends, no stop from the model; the other 31 are Sarah's replays of 2026-09-15 and 16, whose characters (34 to 56) no port has, which stop starting the match as at the base.
- **The oracle**: `--test ruleset` 12 pass, `--test sync` 18 pass, `--test record` passes; the sessions above.
- melee-player's `record_tests`, `live_tests` and `power_tests` at two threads: 22 pass (`a_live_match_equals_its_playback`, both sides without the model now). melee-bench's `ruleset_tests` at two threads: 19 pass, 1 ignored, then the new `a_slippi_match_models_no_memory` (`ics-peach-ps` under 3.19's rules as the rules choose, the model's state `Heap`'s default after every tick, to the match's end).
- **`sync-test --rollback 7`, `--fp slippi`**, on three of Sarah's 3.19 replays: `Game_20260531T181904` (Samus, so the model on: 15,772 ticks checked), `Game_20260913T224137` (the Ice Climbers, the model off: 9,121) and `Game_20260129T214824` (Pokémon Stadium, the model off: 14,140), no difference. No Slippi replay with a Super Scope exists (Sarah's are without items); melee-bench's `slippi_super_scope_stand_in_matches_the_model` covers it.
- **Mutation**: a model call put back on a Slippi match's path (`vs_match`'s text arena, `heap::mem_alloc` back to `heap::with(|h| h.mem_alloc(0x4800))`) fails `a_slippi_match_models_no_memory` at the match's start: `the allocator model reached at crates/melee-sim/src/game/vs_match.rs:681:9 in a match whose ruleset models no memory`. melee-sim's `a_step_that_models_no_memory_stops_where_the_model_is_reached` holds the hooks silent and `with` stopping.
- **Speed (indicative, this PC)**: `MELEE_BENCH_FP=slippi melee-bench native --runs 5`, the base's build and the branch's alternating in three rounds, median of 15 runs: `Game_20260129T130802` 3,429 frames/s before, 3,538 after (+3.2%; runs 3,196 to 3,502 and 3,448 to 3,600), and with `MELEE_BENCH_HEAP=off` 3,491 and 3,549 (+1.7%); `Game_20260913T224137` 1,996 and 2,102 (+5.3%; 1,882 to 2,136 and 2,020 to 2,142), with the model off 2,109 and 2,104 (−0.2%). The default runs are the model's removal; with it off in both the hooks' cost is within the runs' spread. The worker's runner decides.
- melee-sim's unit tests, `ticks_and_restores_dont_allocate` at one thread, the ledger's `--check`, `fmt` and `clippy -D warnings`: melee-sim 175 pass, 2 ignored (`a_step_that_models_no_memory_stops_where_the_model_is_reached` new); the allocation test passes; the ledger 19,827 of 19,827 (only `docs/port-ledger.csv`'s line numbers moved); fmt and clippy clean.

### Task 24, the game modes' registry

**Done (2026-11-21, `refactor/mode-registry` from `refactor/rename-modes` at `c130a46b`).** Proposed by the second survey: a `GameMode` enum with the game's numbers and an entry per mode in place of the `GM_*` constants and `gm::machine`'s `states` and `mode_hooks`. Every game mode is one entry of `game::gm::registry::MODES`, at its number, and nothing else lists modes' states or hooks. A new mode's port writes its module and fills its entry ([porting](porting.md#game-modes)).

**Two numberings, both the game's.** The game numbers its modes (`GameModeKind`, `GM_*`: the routing's mode, the index of `gm_GetAllGameModes`' table, 45 of them, `GM_COUNT` none) and its scenes (`GameSceneKind`, `GS_*`: the index of `gm_GetAllGameScenes`' table, which holds each scene's entry, frame and exit functions, 45 of them) separately ([design call 2](#design-calls); Sarah, 2026-11-19: "two enums is fine"). A mode runs a list of mode states and each state names the scene it runs by its scene number, so one type can't hold both: VS mode is mode 2 and runs scenes 8, 9, 2, 3 and 5; Training is mode 0x1C and its match scene 4; the character select (scene 8) serves a dozen modes. So there are two enums, `GameMode` and `GameScene` (`#[repr(u8)]`, `ALL`, `from_id`, `id`, `PartialEq` with the number; `GameMode::of` stops by name on a number that's no mode, `GameMode::NONE` is `GM_COUNT`). The names are the ones players know where there is one (`ClassicEnding` for `GM_CLASSIC_GOVER`, `TrophyLottery` for `GM_TOY_LOTTERY`, `FixedCamera` for `GM_CAMERA_VS`, `Opening` for `GM_OPENING_MV`, `CharacterSelect` for `GS_CSS`, `CameraCardCheck` for `GS_CAMERA_VS`), each old name a list-form `#[doc(alias)]` on its variant (wave 3's convention). The state keeps the numbers (`routing`, `Phase`, `Game::game_mode`, `gr.game_mode`), so it, the comparer and saved states are unchanged.

**An entry** (`gm::registry::Mode`): its `kind`, `name`, `preloaded` (the original's `GameMode::preloaded`), the ports of its `on_load` and `on_unload` (`Hook`, `fn(&mut Running)`), its `states` (`ModeState`: id, preload class, `on_enter` and `on_exit` handlers, scene: its `gm_Mode_*_States`) and `start` (where its match scenes' start data lives, the files the frontend reads first: a `StartData`'s getters). The original's mode table also holds each mode's `on_init`; `modes_on_init` (`gm_801A4510`'s loop, inline in mode order) stays one function.

**What became reads of it**:

| Was | Now |
| --- | --- |
| 69 definitions of 42 `GM_*` constants in 20 files (`GM_ADVENTURE` six times) and 36 of 32 `GS_*` scene constants in 10, with their 586 and about 280 uses | `GameMode::*`, `GameScene::*` |
| Bare mode and scene numbers: `is_1p_mode`'s 13 and `is_training`'s, the menu's entry lists after a mode (`menu::entry_list`, 30 arms), the main menu's and the Special Melee and trophy lists' exits, the challenger checks' modes, the 1P results' and the Special Melee results' modes, the comparer's state lists (`tools/capture`), the cutscenes' scenes 0x11 to 0x1B in the mode lists and `vi` | the enums |
| `gm::machine::states` (23 lists) and its `one_player_states` (12), each state's handlers a `Hook` variant | each entry's `states`, the handlers the ports themselves |
| `mode_hooks` (preloaded, `on_load`, `on_unload` by mode) | the entry's `preloaded`, `on_load`, `on_unload` |
| `mode_hook`'s `Hook` dispatch (about 160 arms), and its diversion of VS mode's handlers to the Special Melee modes' own when VS mode's list ran for one | gone: each Special Melee mode has VS mode's states with its own handlers (`special_vs!`); Stamina Mode's two VS handlers its own the same way; Multi-Man's six their mode's (`multi!`) |
| `scene_start_data` and `attract::scene_enter_data` (two 10-arm matches on the mode) | the entry's `start` |
| The functions that set a mode taking a number (`change_game_mode_after_scene`, `set_pending_game_mode`, `menu_exit_to`, `challenger_check`, `one_player_clear` and the rest) | take a `GameMode` |

The conversion was scripted: the constants' definitions and imports removed and every path to one rewritten as the enum (`enums.py`), then compiled clean by hand where a `match` on a number needed `GameMode::of` or `from_id` or a call took the number (`.id()`); the lists moved by script with each `Hook` variant written as the port `mode_hook` ran for it, then the Special Melee modes' and Stamina Mode's handlers by hand. Outside `gm`, `ty`, `vi`, `mn` and `game::session` only names changed: `ft::cpu_move`'s one `== 28` (Training), `lb::cardgame`'s and `lb::dvd`'s `GM_*`, `it::pokemon`, `pl::tally`, `game::live`, `game::player_codes`, `game::vs_match`, `hud::debug_*`, the audits' stop texts (`menus_audit`, `single_player_audit`: the mode lists' stops gone, the scene machine's printing the enum, the unported handlers the registry's `u!`), the comparer (`tools/capture`), the oracle's hooks and melee-player's tests.

**Left.** The scenes: a new scene is still its arms in `gm::machine`'s `finish_entering` (entry and frame functions) and `end_scene` (exit), the original's `gm_GetAllGameScenes` table as two matches; a scene entry per `GameScene` would make them one list. The per-mode data selectors besides the start data (`match_exit_data`'s exit data, `results_scene`'s results data, `one_player`'s select data) are matches on `GameMode`, and `uses_vs_melee` lists the modes that take VS mode's data (`the_start_data_follows_vs_melee` holds the entries to it). `modes_on_init` stays one function.

**Checks.**

The base's (`c130a46b`) and the branch's oracles ran wave 5's 263 of the verify list's `diffsession-*` ([wave 5](#task-12-wave-5-the-modes-names): the 178 the last verify timed under two minutes and the quickest of each other family, every mode the menus reach among them: Classic, Adventure and All-Star runs, events, Multi-Man, the Home-Run Contest, Target Test, Training, the tournament, the trophies, the Special Melee modes, Camera Mode, the data screens, the unlocks, the endings, the debug menu and its modes): 979,007 ticks, every tick's hash the same, and every session ending as the verify expects against its trace (262 clean, `mutate-no-image` differing at tick 798 as it must).

The sorted `#[doc(alias = "...")]` list is the same 18,078 lines before and after (`gm_Mode_Opening_OnLoad`, `gm_801BF718` and `onExitMovie` moved from `mode_hook` to the ports they name); 99 list-form aliases were added and none removed. `docs/port-ledger.csv` changed only by line numbers and `gm_Mode_Vs_OnUnload`'s and `gm_Mode_Event_OnUnload`'s file (`gm/registry.rs`).

### Task 13, the camera boundary

**Done (2026-11-19, `refactor/camera-boundary` from `f2b6d007`).** `game::camera::CameraState`'s fields (the camera, `Camera`; its boxes; the second CObj; the default field of view; the debug cameras; playback's followed camera) are private to `game::camera`. Everything else reaches them through the module's functions and `CameraState`'s methods; the compiler holds it. The state's shape and field order, the comparer and saved states are unchanged.

**Who reached it before.** Measured by making the fields private at `f2b6d007` and counting the compiler's errors, so the list is exact: **213 reads and writes of the fields in 63 files** of melee-sim (its unit tests included): `cam` 135, `boxes` 57, `debug` 9, `followed` 8, `fov` 3, `cobj_copy` 1. By module:

| Module | Sites | What it reached |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `gr` (22 files) | 61 | the boxes of its stage objects (Castle, Onett, Fourside, Poké Floats, Stadium, Great Bay, Home-Run), the camera's flags and colour (setups, Big Blue, Flat Zone, Corneria's interest, Mute City's eye, the stages' layers) |
| `ft` (14 files) | 36 | the fighters' own boxes (spawn, death, respawn, ledge, barrel, update), the hands' cutscene scripts (`bosslib`, 17), the 1P zoom |
| `live` | 24 | every field: the handoffs' camera copies, playback's followed camera, the mode, the second CObj |
| `db` | 23 | the debug cameras' state (9), the camera's flags and colour, a followed fighter's box |
| `gm` (7 files) | 21 | `init`, layers, colour, modes, a box (the attract match), the C-stick rule's test |
| `render` | 15 | the hidden layers, the pass and layer, the shadows flag |
| `it` (6 files) | 10 | the items' boxes, the eye and interest |
| `mp_draw` | 9 | the zones' flag, the walk of the framed boxes |
| `export`, `disc` | 4 | the boxes' block, for the heap's image |
| `hud`, `vi`, `pl`, `playback`, `session`, `lb` | 10 | a box's bone, the colour, the width average, the settling's extents, the default field of view, a box's depth for a shadow |

Outside melee-sim: melee-render 3 (the magnifier's box bone, the camera's GObj, the erase colour), melee-player's `record_tests` 8, and the tools: leaf-check 59, the comparer and importer 2 blocks, the bench's ruleset tests 5. Beside those, 388 calls of the module's functions in 100 files, most handed `&mut g.camera.cam` or `&mut g.camera.boxes`. None of it went through `Running`'s methods: the camera module had none; its functions took the parts.

**What crosses the line now** (game code outside `game::camera`: melee-sim, melee-render, melee-player but their tests):

- **`CameraState`'s methods (77), about 190 calls:** the original's getters and setters (`Camera_SetBackgroundColor`, `Camera_80030A60` to `Camera_80030BA8`, the pass and layer, the modes), the boxes' allocation (`alloc_box`, `free_box`, `reset_box`), `init`, the cutscene script's setters, the follower's start and stop, and reads the module had no getter for (`mode`, `hidden_layers`, `draw_zones`, `cobj_copy`, `default_fov`, `quake_model`, `width_average`, `quake_frames`, `is_following`, `followed_transform`). 54 of the module's free functions over `&Camera`, `&mut CameraState` or `&mut CameraBoxes` became methods, unchanged inside; their callers were rewritten by script.
- **The subjects, 32 calls in 20 files:** a box (`CmSubject`) is its owner's record: a fighter, an item or a stage object writes where it is, its target extents, facing, state and ledge flags every tick, as in the original, and the camera eases and frames from them. Owners reach theirs by handle: `subject(b)`, `subject_mut(b)`. What the camera derives (the eased extents, the timer, the framed mark, the links) is the camera's: `fit_extents`, and `fit_horizontal_extents` for playback's settling, which wrote them directly.
- **The debug cameras' state, lent whole to `db`** (`debug_cameras_mut`, 7 calls): the develop tools' camera input (`dbcamera.c`) moves it and the debug camera modes read it; it is `db`'s data kept in the camera's state.
- **The module's functions over `Game` or `Running`, 111 calls:** `request_quake` (61), `snap`, the scripts' look-at and jump, the pause, free, training and clear setups, `ground_span`, `in_bounds`, the screen projections.
- **The raw parts, 4 calls in two files:** `raw_parts()` lays the state open whole for what reads it as the original's memory: the heap's image of the boxes' block (`export`) and the disc's check of whose heap holds it (`disc`). The oracle's comparer and importer, leaf-check and tests use it too (25 calls in all). `camera::boundary_tests::the_camera_is_reached_through_its_module` (since task 21 `game::boundary_tests`, for every subsystem behind its line) scans melee-sim's, melee-render's and melee-player's sources and fails on any other use outside a test module (mutation-tested: a `raw_parts()` read added to `gr::pokemon_stadium` fails it, naming the line).

Three pieces of the camera's own logic were being done outside it, and moved in: the live match's handoffs (`live` copied the whole `Camera` and picked fields: now `carried`, `take_carried`, `take_match`), playback's first-frame settling on the followed camera (`live` fitted the boxes and put them back: `settle_followed_boxes`), and the zones' debug drawing (`mp_draw` walked the boxes' list and asked `Camera_8002928C`, which counts automatic boxes' timers down: `for_each_framed`).

**What it cost.** `game/camera.rs` 3,852 lines to 4,172 (the new accessors and the raw parts about 190, the audit test 55, the rest the methods' `impl` blocks); 77 files changed, 1,284 lines in and 992 out; no behaviour changed, so no ruleset, oracle or golden change. The `#[doc(alias)]` attributes are the same 18,076 before and after (a sorted list diffed). The speed, below, is the same within this PC's noise: the methods inline to the field accesses they replace.

**What the pilot found.**

- The camera was already a module of functions: its line was crossed by data, not calls. Of the 213 field sites, 135 handed the `Camera` itself to the module's own getters and setters, which taking `&mut Camera` invited. Making the fields private and the functions methods removed those mechanically; the compiler listed each site.
- The real crossings are records the original shares by design: the camera subjects, written by their owners each tick, and the debug cameras' state, written by the develop tools. A clean line keeps them as records lent by handle, rather than wrapping every field in a setter; the camera's part of a subject can't be fenced off while `CmSubject` is a generated struct with public fields (the rename wave of the generated structs, task 12, could split it into the owner's part and the camera's).
- The oracle needs the whole state, as memory: one named door (`raw_parts`), audited, rather than public fields. The heap's image needs it too, which is Vanilla's machinery (task 6 puts it behind the ruleset).
- Logic about the camera had leaked into its readers where they needed several fields at once (the handoffs, the settling, the zones' walk). Those moved in; the readers now name what they do.

**The rule for the next subsystems** (task 13's, revised with what the second boundary, [collision](#task-21-the-collision-boundary), found).

1. **Behind the line:** the subsystem's state structs, with private fields, in the `Game` fields they have now (the state's shape and field order don't change, so the oracle and saved states don't). Private means private to the subsystem: where its files share its state, as `mp`'s do (`coll`, `search`, `island`, `joint` and the rest are impl blocks on one `Geometry`), the fields are `pub(in crate::mp)`. Its functions take the parts as before inside the module. A piece of its state another module holds stays there if moving it would change the state's shape (the update counter's stand-in before a stage loads, `gr.coll_generation`); the subsystem's methods cover the rest of it.
2. **What may cross, and how:**
   - methods on the state for what others ask or set, named for what they mean (`mode`, `hidden_layers`, `floor_bounds`, `joints_addr`), one per original getter or setter where there is one;
   - the subsystem's functions over `Game` or `Running` where it reads other state (the quake requests, the scripts);
   - records the subsystem keeps that others read (a collision line, vertex or island), lent read-only by id (`line`, `vertex`, `island`, `group_lines`), with a walk where readers walk a list (`floor_islands`, `first_floor_island`); writes stay the subsystem's methods;
   - records the original shares with their owners, in whichever side keeps them: a record the subsystem keeps and its owner writes (a camera subject) lent by handle (`subject`, `subject_mut`), the owner's fields named in the method's doc, the subsystem's derived fields behind methods; a record its owner keeps and the subsystem works on (an object's `CollData`, in its `Fighter`, `Item` or `Ground`) lent whole to the subsystem's functions for the call (`&mut CollData`), until the generated struct can be split (wave 6);
   - another system's data the original keeps in this one's state (the debug cameras for `db`), lent whole by one named method;
   - the raw parts, one door per state struct, always named `raw_parts` and `raw_parts_mut` (a struct of references to every field), for the oracle, leaf-check, tests and the heap's image only, and a blank constructor where the tools build the state from a capture (`Geometry::blank`); `game::boundary_tests` holds every subsystem to it.
3. **How to do it:** make the fields private first and let the compiler list every reader (the counted list goes in the docs; `cargo check --workspace --all-targets --keep-going` with the fixes in rounds, since a crate's errors hide its dependents'); turn the free functions over the parts that others call into methods by script; move logic that reads several of the fields at once from a reader into the module; give the tools the raw parts (rustc's diagnostics place each: a scratch fixer wrote `.raw_parts()` at each private-field read and made it `raw_parts_mut()` where the borrow checker asked). Add the subsystem to `game::boundary_tests`' table: its sources, its state structs (whose fields the audit holds private) and the files that may open its raw parts. Check with the whole state's per-tick hashes before and after on replays that reach it.
4. **What the camera and collision didn't test:** a subsystem whose state other systems' code is generic over (the fighters' `Fighter`, read through `Running`'s 8,000 methods) has callers in every module, and its records (the generated structs) are the line's content, not a detail. Such a split wants the generated structs taken over first (task 12's last wave). Collision's own record, `CollData`, is one: 404 lines of game code in 121 files name its fields (the fighters 210 in 62, the items 184 in 52), the owners' fields and collision's results side by side, so its line waits for task 23.

**Checks.**

- **The whole state, tick by tick** (task 3's way, the other way round): the base's oracle (`f2b6d007`) ran the full diff of 83 of the verify list's replays, each comparing every traced tick with no difference and no stale read, writing a per-tick golden; the branch's then checked each golden: **1,087,030 ticks, every tick's state hash the same.** The replays: the benchmark's five Vanilla ones and Sarah's Pokémon Stadium replay under Slippi's rules; Dream Land, Yoshi's Story and Fountain of Dreams (`ics-fox-dl-a7365e16`, `gnw-zelda-ys-4e9d13cb`, `roy-puff-fod`); every `stage-*` diff (23) and the stages' others (9 `oracle-cpu-stage-*`, 2 barrels, Poké Floats' replay, the castle's walkers, Corneria's and Venom's long, taunt and CPU matches, 8); Special Melee's 11 (the fixed camera, Super Sudden Death, Tiny, Giant, Slo-Mo and Lightning among them); the camera player code's (`pc-bebc5463`); the items on Pokémon Stadium, Yoshi's Story and Fountain of Dreams and Big Blue's platforms; the Ice Climbers' seven; the Super Scope's two, the grapples' two, the Goomba's and Octorok's; and the four-player match. The goldens' coverage line was set to the branch's, since the comparer's sources changed only by `tools/capture`'s reads of the camera through `raw_parts`; the state hash's own sources (`golden.rs`, `state_eq.rs`, `mirror*.rs`, `melee-derive`) are unchanged. **Mutation:** `CameraState::fit_extents` made a no-op (a spawning fighter's box keeps its reset extents) makes `fox-fox-fd`'s hash differ from the golden's in tick 1, its first, and the full diff names the camera (`camera.transform.interest: ours [5.05619e-7, 10.137911, 0.0], theirs [5.05619e-7, 17.218706, 0.0]`, 11 places).
- **Oracle:** `melee-oracle --test ruleset`: all 12 pass (the paused live matches, `past_the_handback_only_the_named_differ` and `past_the_handback_the_statics_compare` among them). `--test session`, 26 named for their cameras: `camera_mode_takes_and_saves_a_snapshot`, `camera_mode_in_japanese`, `special_melee_sessions` (the fixed camera), `target_test_from_the_menu`, `target_test_cleared`, `the_home_run_contest`, `multi_man_melee`, `all_star`, `single_player_intros`, `adventure_game_over`, `the_debug_cutscenes`, `the_debug_endings`, `master_hand_in_debug_vs`, `crazy_hand_in_debug_vs`, `giga_bowser_in_debug_vs` (the bosses' cutscene cameras), `icicle_mountain_climb_in_debug_vs`, `brinstar_escape_in_debug_vs`, `the_fzero_race_in_debug_vs`, `training_from_its_select`, `training_unloads_its_computer_players` (the training menu's camera), `the_debug_menus_go_back_laid_out`, `a_debug_level_opens_the_debug_menu` (the debug cameras), `an_idle_title_reaches_the_attract_match`, `the_attract_match_on_brinstar_reads_a_leftover`, `matches_from_the_menus_on_three_stages`, `a_tournament_match_on_peachs_castle_goes_back_laid_out`: all pass. `melee-oracle session` on `m6s-pause/pause-fd` with `--all --heap --after-handback`: "to its handback before tick 1757; past it 134 ticks compared to tick 1890, no difference but the named, 1 live matches in all".
- **Player tests:** melee-player's `record_tests`, all 15 pass, `a_live_match_on_the_consoles_counters_plays_back_the_same` and the follower's (`a_live_match_follows_playbacks_camera_until_they_meet`, `a_paused_live_match_follows_playbacks_camera`, `a_paused_live_match_follows_playbacks_camera_readers`, `the_players_live_match_counts_camera_reads_after_a_pause_only`, `a_live_match_equals_its_playback`, `a_slow_mo_live_match_equals_its_playback`) among them.
- `sync-test --rollback 7 --fp hardware`: `fox-marth-ps` (13,579 ticks checked) and `stage-corneria` (14,518), no difference.
- leaf-check's `--test camera` (39) and the two stage checks it touched (`ground_settings_and_queries`, `ground_background_color`); melee-sim's unit tests (164, the boundary audit and the follower's unit tests among them); `ticks_and_restores_dont_allocate` at one thread; the ledger's `--check` (19,827 of 19,827; only `docs/port-ledger.csv`'s line numbers moved); `fmt` and `clippy -D warnings`.

Within the runs' spread: the methods are the same field accesses. The worker's runner should take the deciding numbers ([design call 9](#design-calls)).

### Task 21, the collision boundary

**Done (2026-11-21, `refactor/collision-boundary` from `82e7ea27`, task 15's `refactor/rename-engine`).** `mp::Geometry`'s fields (the stage's vertices, lines and joints, the data's line groups, the joint list, the bounds, the update counter, the islands, the arrays' addresses, the render stamps, the debug ROM flag) and `mp::coll::CollStatics`' (mpcoll's walls, correction, floor filter, stale-read count and watchdog) are private to `mp` (`pub(in crate::mp)`, since `mp`'s files are impl blocks on the same state). Everything else reaches them through `mp`'s functions and methods; the compiler holds it, and `game::boundary_tests` holds the fields private and the raw parts to the heap's image. The state's shape and field order, the comparer and saved states are unchanged.

**Who reached it before.** Measured by making the fields private at `82e7ea27` and counting the compiler's errors over the workspace (`--all-targets`, in rounds, since a crate's errors hide its dependents'), so the list is exact: **218 sites in 30 files**, 74 of them in 17 files of game code (melee-sim's library; none in melee-render, none in melee-player's but its tests). `Geometry` 170, `CollStatics` 48. The survey's 111 lines naming `game.geometry` in 52 files were mostly calls of `mp`'s methods already (`geometry.as_ref().expect(..)` then `probe_floor`, `island_of_line`, `enable_joint`, ...): `mp` was a module of methods on its state from the start, so only these reached past them.

| Where | Sites | Files | What it reached |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `game::ft` | 31 | 9 | the computer players' walks of the floors' islands (`islands`, 27 in `cpu_ai`, `cpu_attack`, `cpu_decide`, `cpu_move`, `cpu_recover`, `cpu_vs`), the joints' array's address for the excluded-floor r5 (`cpu`, 2), the line count for the platforms' filter (`coll`), the enabled floors' bounds (`common`) |
| `game::gr` | 15 | 2 | the update counter (`ground`'s bump and read, the load's carry), the joints' stamps and the render stamps (`Ground_UpdateMapColl`, 8), the debug ROM flag at the load, the joints' model joints unbound when a stage object's model is freed (`setup`) |
| `game::export` | 12 | 1 | the vertices, lines, line groups, islands and joints and their arrays' addresses, for the heap's image |
| `game::live` | 7 | 1 | the update counter playback hands a live match; the statics the console's last wall check left, carried into a live match (6) |
| `game::mp_draw` | 5 | 1 | the lines and vertices of `mpLib_DrawMatchingLines` and `mpLib_80059554` (the debug drawing) |
| `game::sfx` | 2 | 1 | the enabled floors' bounds (the crowd's gasps as fighters fall and recover) |
| `game::disc`, `game::it` | 2 | 2 | the joints' array's address (whose heap holds it), the hookshot's island ends |
| melee-player's `record_tests` | 4 | 1 | playback's counters copied into a live match's state |
| leaf-check's `mplib` and `mpcoll` | 109 | 6 | the geometry and statics they set up, mutate and compare with ssbm-rs's (5 statics built field by field) |
| the comparer and importer (`tools/capture`) | 28 | 4 | the geometry and statics compared and imported (the geometry and the statics built field by field) |
| the oracle | 3 | 2 | the statics' stale-read count |

**What crosses the line now** (game code outside `mp`):

- **New methods, 15:** `line_count`, `lines` and `group_lines` (the debug drawing's walks, read-only), `floor_bounds`, `generation`, `bump_generation`, `set_generation`, `set_debug_rom`, `joints_addr`, `island`, `first_floor_island` and `floor_islands` (the computer players' walks, read-only), `joint_stamp` and `set_joint_stamp`, `unbind_jobjs`; on `CollStatics`, `carry_from` and `stale_reads` (the oracle's). About 45 calls in game code.
- **The records `mp` keeps, read-only by id:** a line, a vertex, an island (`line`, `vertex`, `island`, which were there for the line and vertex). Nothing outside `mp` writes one; `Joint` isn't lent at all.
- **The records an owner keeps:** an object's `CollData` (`Fighter::coll_data`, `Item::x378_itemColl`, a stage object's `coll_data`) is its owner's, and `mp`'s passes take it whole for the call (`&mut CollData`), as before. 404 lines of game code in 121 files name its fields (the fighters 210 in 62, the items 184 in 52); it is a generated struct with the owner's fields and collision's results side by side, so its split waits for wave 6 (task 23), as the survey planned.
- **The raw parts, 2 calls in one file:** `Geometry::raw_parts()` lays the geometry open whole for the heap's image (`export`'s `coll_arrays` and `coll_joints`). The comparer and importer, leaf-check and tests use it, `raw_parts_mut` and `CollStatics`' pair too, and the importer builds a geometry from `Geometry::blank()` (about 110 calls in all, most of them leaf-check's).

Four pieces of collision's own logic were being done outside it, and moved in: `Ground_UpdateMapColl`'s read of a joint's stamp, which resolves a stamp the load left over the GPU's pixels from the heap's image or stops (`joint_stamp`, in `gr::ground` before); the stamp's write and the render stamp's clearing (`set_joint_stamp`); the unbinding of the joints a freed model drove (`unbind_jobjs`, from `gr::setup`); and the live match's taking of the console's statics but its own floor filter, stale-read count and watchdog (`carry_from`, from `live::Carried::put`).

**The audit, generalized.** The camera's `the_camera_is_reached_through_its_module` became `game::boundary_tests`, one table of subsystems (each one's sources, state structs and the files that may open its raw parts): `subsystems_are_reached_through_their_modules` scans melee-sim's, melee-render's and melee-player's sources for `raw_parts` outside a subsystem, its readers and test code, and `the_subsystems_states_keep_their_fields_private` fails on a state struct's field made `pub` again (the camera's audit didn't check that: making a field public would have let a reader past the line with no error). Mutation-tested: a `raw_parts()` read of the geometry added to `gr::ground::generation` fails the first (`melee-sim/src/game/gr/ground.rs:71: let _ = game.geometry.as_ref().map(|g| g.raw_parts().joints_addr);`), and `Geometry::islands` made `pub` fails the second (`collision (task 21): melee-sim/src/mp/mod.rs: Geometry: pub islands: Islands,`).

**What it cost.** `mp` 7,960 lines to 8,285 (the methods and the blank constructor about 150, the raw parts and their structs about 175); the audit 138 lines (the camera's 65 gone); 35 files changed, 746 lines in and 349 out, 108 of leaf-check's reads rewritten by a scratch fixer over rustc's diagnostics. No behaviour changed, so no ruleset, oracle or golden change. The `#[doc(alias)]` attributes are the same 18,851 before and after, both forms (a sorted list diffed).

**What generalized from the camera, and what didn't.**

- Generalized: the compiler's list as the exact count, private fields with methods named for what they mean, logic moved in where a reader needed several fields at once, one raw-parts door for the tools and the heap's image, and a source audit.
- Collision was already behind its functions. The camera's line was crossed by data handed to its own getters; collision's by reads of what it derives (the islands, the floors' bounds, the counter) and by tools. No free function needed turning into a method.
- Its shared records sit on the other side. The camera keeps its subjects and lends them to their owners by handle; collision's `CollData` lives in its owners and is lent to `mp` for a pass, so "by handle" turned into "whole, per call". Collision's own records (lines, vertices, islands) are lent read-only by id instead.
- A raw-parts struct copies its scalar fields, so a write through `raw_parts()` (`p.raw_parts().ecb_tiny = true`) compiles and is lost: leaf-check's two setters did that after the fixer's pass, and its `mpcoll` case caught it. Writes go through `raw_parts_mut()`, whose fields are references.
- Private had to mean private to a module tree (`pub(in crate::mp)`), not to a file.
- The tools build the state, not only read it: the importer and leaf-check filled `Geometry` and `CollStatics` with struct literals, which private fields refuse. A struct of references can't be built, so the door needed a blank constructor (`Geometry::blank`, `CollStatics::default`) and its `_mut` pair.
- The doors share one name, so a text audit can't tell whose raw parts a line opens: the readers' lists act as a union (`disc` may open the camera's but not the geometry's, which only the type would tell). A typed door wasn't worth it: the readers are two files.
- A piece of collision's state lives in another module: the update counter's stand-in before a stage loads (`gr.coll_generation`), which `gr::ground::generation` picks between. Moving it into `mp` would change the state's shape, so it stays; the methods cover the loaded counter.

**Checks.**

- **The whole state, tick by tick** (task 15's way): task 15's goldens of the verify list's 330 replay diffs (every character, the bench matches, Slippi 3.19, 3.16 and 3.17 replays, players' codes, the 32 stage replays (`stage-*` and `oracle-cpu-stage-*`, Icicle Mountain, Brinstar, Rainbow Cruise, Big Blue, Poké Floats, Mute City, Corneria and Venom among them) and the items), checked by the base's oracle built at `82e7ea27`: all 330, **3,552,272 ticks, every tick's state hash the same**, no stale read. The branch's oracle, with the goldens' coverage line set to the branch's (its comparer's sources changed only by `tools/capture`'s reads through the raw parts; `golden.rs`, `state_eq.rs`, `mirror*.rs` and `melee-derive` are unchanged, and the hash reads fields in order, not by name): all 330, 3,552,272 ticks, the same.
- **Oracle:** `melee-oracle --test ruleset` at one thread: all 12 pass. `--test session`, 12 named for collision (stage hazards, walls, ledges and climbs): `the_attract_match_on_brinstar_reads_a_leftover`, `rainbow_cruises_blocks_hold_the_heaps_leftovers`, `matches_from_the_menus_on_three_stages`, `the_hookshot_bounces_back_off_a_wall`, `the_fzero_race_in_debug_vs`, `race_in_debug_vs`, `brinstar_escape_in_debug_vs`, `icicle_mountain_climb_in_debug_vs`, `single_player_intros` (Adventure's run: the Underground Maze, Brinstar's escape, Icicle Mountain, the F-Zero race among its stages), `mute_citys_terrain_edit_reaches_the_heaps_image`, `the_home_run_contest`, `target_test_from_the_menu`: all pass. `--test single_player`'s `adventures_like_like_ko_and_ledge_run_clean` (the Underground Maze, its ledge and an item crawling to a platform's edge) and `adventures_red_shell_stopped_on_a_slope_runs_clean`: both pass.
- `sync-test --rollback 7`: `fox-marth-ps` (`--fp hardware`, 13,579 ticks checked), `stage-corneria` (14,518), `stage-icicle-mountain` (28,918) and Sarah's 3.19 Pokémon Stadium replay `Game_20260129T214824` (`--fp slippi`, 14,140): no difference.
- leaf-check's `lbcollision` (7), `mpcoll` (1, every stage's collision cases), `mplib` (1), `stage` (62), `fighter` (38) and `stages` (177, 26 minutes): all pass. `mpcoll` failed on the first run: two of its setters wrote mpcoll's statics through `raw_parts()`, a struct of copies for the scalar fields, so the writes were lost silently (`ecb_tiny` stayed false on `GrTe.dat`'s right-wall case); they write through `raw_parts_mut()` now.
- melee-sim's unit tests (176 pass, 2 ignored; the two boundary audits among them), `ticks_and_restores_dont_allocate` at one thread, the ledger's `--check` (19,827 of 19,827; only `docs/port-ledger.csv`'s line numbers moved), `fmt` and `clippy -D warnings`.
- **Speed (indicative, this PC with other agents' work on it):** `melee-bench native --runs 5 fox-fox-fd fox-marth-ps`, base (`82e7ea27`) and branch alternating in three rounds, median of the 15 runs: `fox-fox-fd` 3,327 frames/s before, 3,327 after (runs 3,226–3,607 and 3,248–3,428); `fox-marth-ps` 2,778 before, 2,814 after (2,732–3,075 and 2,728–2,895). Within the runs' spread: the methods are the same field accesses, `#[inline]` across the codegen units. The worker's exclusive runner was taken (the player's build round running, Pokémon Stadium's allocations queued behind it), so the deciding numbers are the coordinator's to take there ([design call 9](#design-calls)).

### Task 32, the HUD's boundary and its structs

**Done (2026-11-24, `refactor/hud-boundary` from `2389110c`, batch 16's base).** Task 13's rule for the HUD, and [design call 3's second half](#design-call-3s-second-half-the-owned-structs) for its structs. The state's shape, field order and hash are unchanged.

**The structs.** The HUD's generated structs are hand-written in `game::hud::types` behind `#[derive(Mirror)]` and the layout check, on structgen's `OWNED` list: 22 of them, 154 fields (the survey's 16 counted those code names; the six held only inside them came along). The address- and C-styled names are Rust names now, each keeping its C name as a `#[doc(alias)]`: `HudIndex` is `DamageDisplays` (of `DamageDisplay`, with `DamageDisplayFlags`), `ScInfStcModels` `KoMarks` (of `KoMarkSlot`), `ifTime_data` `HudTimer`, `IfStockUserData` `StockUserData` (with `StockSteal`), `ifMagnify` `Magnifier` (of `MagnifierBubble`, with `BubbleState`), and a display's `HUD_parent_entity` is `parent`; `Announcement`, `HudLayout`, `HudStocks`, `JoinerTally` and the rest kept wave 6's names. Their names left `names_step3.py`, the comments with them becoming the fields' docs. The layout check asked for four runs of the compiler's padding to be declared (`MagnifierBubble`, `DamageDisplay`, `OffscreenArrows`, `ScoreDisplay`). No type was improved past the generated one's: the flag bytes are already a bool per bit, and none of the HUD's numbers are C enums. `StaticModelDesc`, which the title screen holds too, stays generated. `game::structs` goes from 177 generated structs to 155 (9,228 lines to 8,432), `mirror_gen.rs` from 19,347 lines to 17,863; `game::hud::types` is 647.

**The boundary.** `hud::Hud`'s 23 fields are private to `game::hud` (`pub(in crate::game::hud)`). What crossed the line, by the compiler's list over the workspace (`--all-targets`):

| Where | Lines | What it reached | Now |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `db` | 17 | the debug text screens | `Hud::devtext`, `devtext_mut` |
| the toys (`ty::collection`, `debug`, `debug_view`, `gallery`) | 15 | the debug text screens | the same |
| `export` | 3 | the debug menus' pool and a name tag's user data address, for the heap's image | `devtext`, `nametag_user_data_addr` |
| `state` | 1 | a name tag's user data address (the user data's lookup) | `nametag_user_data_addr` |
| `live` | 3 places | the off-screen arrows' colors and players a live match carries; the arrows' and announcements' values the menus' session takes back from a live match | `Hud::carried` and `take_carried` (a `hud::Carried`), `Hud::take_match`: the logic moved in, as the camera's did |
| the comparer and importer (`tools/capture`'s `hud.rs`) | about 30 | every field | `Hud::raw_parts`, `raw_parts_mut` |
| leaf-check's `hud` | 16 | the layout, the KO marks, the timer, the announcements, the stocks, the hazard sign, the scores, the debug text | `raw_parts`, `devtext`, `devtext_mut` |

Nothing in melee-render or melee-player reached it. `game::boundary_tests` has the HUD as its third subsystem (its sources `game/hud/`, its state `Hud`, no game code allowed its raw parts). Mutation-tested: `let _ = *self.game.hud.raw_parts().hidden;` added to `db`'s `db_setup_cpu` fails `subsystems_are_reached_through_their_modules` (`melee-sim/src/game/db.rs:433: ...`), and `Hud::hidden` made `pub` fails `the_subsystems_states_keep_their_fields_private` (`the HUD (task 32): melee-sim/src/game/hud/mod.rs: Hud: pub hidden: bool,`). The magnifier's state (`Game::magnify`, a `Magnifier`) stays a field of `Game`: the display reads it (`melee-render`'s `hsd::magnify`), and the task's row named `Hud`'s fields.

**Checks.**

- **The whole state, tick by tick**: batch 16's goldens (`v5-b16`) of the verify list's 330 replay diffs, their coverage line set to the branch's oracle (the comparer's sources changed): all 330, **3,552,272 ticks, every tick's state hash the same**.
- **A mutation per owned struct, reported under its label**: a scratch oracle whose damage display process wrote one wrong value into the struct an environment variable named, each run on `fox-fox-fd` (first difference in tick 1 for each): `magnify.player[0].state.ignore_offscreen` (`BubbleState`), `magnify.player[0].gobj` (`MagnifierBubble`), `magnify.pad_ec (pad)[0]` (`Magnifier`), `hud status.players[0].flags.unused_b7 (unk1)` (`DamageDisplayFlags`), `hud status.players[0].old_damage` (`DamageDisplay`), `hud status.damage_mark_shapeanims` (`DamageDisplays`), `hud announcement 0.flags (x12).unused_3 (x3)` (`AnnouncementFlags`), `hud announcement 0.unread_4 (x4)` (`Announcement`), `hud KO marks.slots[5].gobj2` (`KoMarkSlot`), `hud KO marks.scene_models` (`KoMarks`), `hud time.pad31[0]` (`HudTimer`), `hud all.player_pos_2 (x84)[1]` (`HudLayout`), `hud stock.player[0].stocks` (`PlayerStocks`), `hud stock.counter_model (x4)` (`HudStocks`), `hud stock user 0.steal[1].end` (`StockSteal`), `hud stock user 0.anim[6]` (`StockUserData`), `hud stock 1774.crowd_count (x108)` (`JoinerTally`), `hud stock 1acc.costumes (x83)[132]` (`CharsLeftTally`), `hud arrows.pad_34` (`OffscreenArrows`), `hud coins.unused_3 (x3)` (`CoinCounter`), `hud scores.entries (x8)[5].score (xC)` (`ScoreDisplay`), `hud scores.pad_x4[3]` (`ScoreDisplays`). Three first tries showed something else first: the damage mark's model cleared stopped the match's start (the HUD reads it), the stock icons' models cleared changed the render chain first, and a swap of two model lists ran once per display and undid itself.
- The layout check (74 owned structs) and its proofs, and the label tests: pass.
- `melee-oracle --test session` at two threads, the 20 that reach the HUD: `a_session_plays_its_match_from_the_selects`, `the_rules_reach_the_match`, `a_name_from_the_character_select`, `a_team_battle_with_handicaps`, `computer_players_at_three_levels`, `a_vs_coin_match_on_yoshis_story`, `a_debug_level_opens_the_debug_menu`, `the_debug_menus_go_back_laid_out`, `the_yoshiki_test_page`, `the_debug_only_code`, `master_hand_in_debug_vs` and `icicle_mountain_climb_in_debug_vs` (the debug pause), `target_test_from_the_menu`, `the_home_run_contest`, `multi_man_melee`, `all_star`, `training_from_its_select`, `the_trophy_collection_in_each_layout`, `a_tournament_tie_runs_over_the_stage_selects_preview`, `taken_up_match_plays_on_with_the_reads` (a live match's handoff): all pass (`multi_man_melee_won` ignored, as by default; 64 minutes). `--test single_player`: `sarahs_all_star_clear_runs_clean_to_its_card_after`, `classic_clear_runs_clean_to_its_card_after`, `adventures_like_like_ko_and_ledge_run_clean`, `the_events_run_clean_to_their_cards_after`: all pass.
- leaf-check's `hud` (83): all pass. melee-sim's unit tests (205 passed, 2 ignored), `ticks_and_restores_dont_allocate` at one thread, the ledger's `--check` (19,827 of 19,827; only `docs/port-ledger.csv`'s line numbers moved), `fmt` and `clippy --all-targets -D warnings` on the workspace.
- **Speed (indicative, this PC with other agents' work on it):** `melee-bench native --runs 5 fox-fox-fd fox-marth-ps`, base (`2389110c`) and branch alternating in three rounds, median of the 15 runs: `fox-fox-fd` 3,820 frames/s before, 3,907 after (runs 3,661 to 4,175 and 3,588 to 4,061); `fox-marth-ps` 3,233 before, 3,233 after (2,911 to 3,394 and 3,075 to 3,348). Within the runs' spread: the HUD's code is unchanged but for the accessors, the same field reads. The deciding numbers are the coordinator's to take on the worker ([design call 9](#design-calls)).

### Task 33, part 2: the containers and the power-ups

**Done (2026-11-24, `refactor/dedup-tail-2` from `2b378559`, batch 16's merge).** Two more item families share one implementation each, the item pairs' way ([design call 7](#design-calls)): a new module per family holds the shared functions, each carrying every original's `#[doc(alias)]`, over a descriptor per kind that names what differs; each kind's `run_pred` and `run_event` keep its own addresses and call the shared function with its descriptor (`COLL4 => container::drops(r, gobj, &OPENING)`). Each item's file keeps its addresses, its `run` tables, its data and what only it does; the fighters' and stages' callers (`wstar::descend`, `rabbitc::scale_ears`, `crate_box::spawn_floating`, `kusudama::spawn_at`) are unchanged. The same calls in the same order: where two originals put the same writes in another order, it's a setting or a callback, never a changed path.

- **The containers** (`it::kinds::container`, `itcapsule.c`, `itbox.c`, `ittaru.c`, `itkusudama.c`): the do-nothing callbacks, the falls' gravity and the thrown spin, the rest, the fall's landing and the skid, the pickup, the throw and the drop, the smoke, the blast's lifetime, the stop (`it_8027D0B8` and `fn_80286480`), the fall (`it_8028655C`, `it_80287D0C`), and for the three that open, the hit that breaks one, the damage that breaks one, the thrown flight and the drop that smash one, the burst empty (`it_80286AA4`, `it_802885C8`, `it_8028AC74`), the opening (`it_80286BA0`, `it_802886C4`, `it_8028AD44`), the frames until it's gone, the breaking's splinters, the weights' draw (`it_80286340` and `it_8028A114`, the same function twice) and the forget, over a `Container` (the Capsule, the Crate and the Party Ball: `capsule::CAPSULE`, `crate_box::CRATE`, `kusudama::PARTY_BALL`) and an `Opening` (the Crate, the Barrel and the Party Ball: `crate_box::OPENING`, `taru::OPENING`, `kusudama::OPENING`). The three's variables share their first two words (`x0` opened, `x4` the frames left), read through a struct of references as the shells' are (`container::Vars`).
- **The power-ups** (`it::kinds::powerup`, `itmetalb.c`, `itrabbitc.c`, `itspycloak.c`, `itwstar.c`): the spawn, the stop (`it_802953FC`, `it_80295138`, `it_80295C68`, `it_8029455C`), the rest, the fall (`it_80295498`, `it_802951C0`, `it_80295D04`, `it_80294624`) and its landing, the gravity, the pickup and the drop, the throw's vanishing, the skid and the forget, over a `PowerUp` per item (`metalb::METAL_BOX`, `rabbitc::BUNNY_HOOD`, `spycloak::CLOAKING_DEVICE`, `wstar::WARP_STAR`). The Cloaking Device keeps no code of its own; the Metal Box only its break.

**What differed**, besides the addresses, each a setting:

| Family | Setting | Values |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Containers | `fell`, `landed` | the Capsule's fall is the state alone (`it_8027D148`), the Crate's stops it being picked up (shared with the Barrel), the Party Ball's stands it upright; the Party Ball's stop clears its speed before it's picked up (the inline `Item_StopAndEnterState`, `container::stop`) and stands it upright (`it_8028A190`) |
| | `tilts`, `held`, `thrown`, `dropped` | the Crate tilts with the floor at rest; held, thrown and dropped in 2, 3 and 4, the Party Ball in 4, 5 and 6 |
| | `let_go_disables_pickup` | all but the Capsule |
| Opening | `breaks`, `smashes` | the Crate and the Barrel break (splinters, then open or burst); the Party Ball opens or bursts (`it_80289B50`), chimed when smashed |
| | `damage_limit`, `drop_limit`, `settles` | the attributes' `x14` and `x1C` (the Crate), `xC` and `x34` (the Barrel, read by its own damage and landings), `x20` and `x2C` (the Party Ball); a soft drop's stop: the Crate's, the Barrel's `Item_StopAndEnterState`, the Party Ball's tipped upright (`it_80289910`) |
| | `has_items` | the Crate's weights `x0` to `xC`, the Barrel's `x0` in `x0 + x4`, the Party Ball's `x4`, `x0`, `x8` and `xC` |
| | `burst_state`, `burst_wind` | 6, 6 and 7; the wind's blast for the Crate and the Barrel |
| | `open_sfx`, `spills`, `open_frames`, `open_state` | 0xF6, 0xFB, 0xF8; the Crate's items by its weights (`it_80286248`) and the Barrel's one item, each with a small quake before it's hidden, nothing for the Party Ball (it rains later); 40, 40, 85 frames; states 7, 7, 8 |
| Power-ups | `clears_hurt`, `on_spawn` | the Metal Box and the Warp Star clear their hurt flag's bit 7; the Warp Star sets its ride's scales to 1 |
| | `tilts` | the Metal Box and the Cloaking Device tilt with the floor at rest |
| | `on_pickup`, `on_drop` | the Bunny Hood's shine hidden and shown (`it_802950D4`'s calls); the Warp Star's glow on bone 3 and its effects gone |

**Left as each item's own, and why.** The Capsule's landing check and explosion (coins, a draw for the bomb, its own order of the blast's steps and its wind at 60 degrees), its hits (they open it unless it's exploding, and say whether it's gone) and its shield callbacks; the Crate's tumble while a Kongo Jungle barrel carries it, its spin, its float and follow, its upright landing off a slope and its items' drop by weights; the Barrel's roll, turn, lying collision, bounces, throw (its pose reset), let-go with its hits kept, rolling states and its damage (a lying one knocked rolling, a rolling one pushed, before the break shared with the others); the Party Ball's tilt and turn to the front, its rise, hang and cheer, its rain and its opening's draw. The Warp Star's ride differs too much to share: its pick of a ride animation, its sizing to the rider, its box, its descent with its attack's flags and id, its crash (let go of with its attack kept, hidden where the rider lands) and the crash's hitboxes; its rest spins it. The Metal Box's break (turning its breaker metal) and the Bunny Hood's parts fitted to its wearer are their own. The Warp Star's forget stays in `forget`'s `FORGET_ONLY`, as the Capsule's does. No Melee defect found.

**The numbers.**

| | |
| --- | --- |
| Lines | the containers' four files 2,234 to 2,182 with the new `container.rs` (459 lines, about a quarter of them the aliases); the power-ups' four 855 to 738 with `powerup.rs` (194). Code lines (blanks and comments left out, the survey's count): 1,694 to 1,604 and 572 to 493. The near-copied lines (the survey's 100-token scan over each family's files) fall from 443 to 233 and from 326 to 167: what's left is the address lists, the `run` tables and the descriptors. 1,281 added and 1,342 removed in `crates` and `tools`, leaf-check's tables included |
| Aliases | the same 18,078 `#[doc(alias = "...")]`, 4,105 list-form attributes and 83 pending ports (sorted lists diffed); `ledger --check` 19,827 of 19,827, only `docs/port-ledger.csv`'s locations moved |
| Oracle | batch 16's goldens (`v5-b16`) of the verify list's 330 replay diffs: all 330, **3,552,272 ticks, every tick's state hash the same**. The 46 among them whose Slippi item updates name a family's kind (408,017 ticks): the Capsule in 9 (opened, thrown in 3, dropped in 4, exploding in 5, skidding in 6), the Crate in 32 (all its states: thrown, dropped, burst empty in 6, open in 7, floating with Kongo Jungle's barrels in 8 in `items-box-kongo-jungle`), the Barrel in 33 (thrown, rolling in 5, burst in 6, open in 7), the Party Ball in 28 (all its states, risen, swung open and raining in 8), the Warp Star in 5 (ridden in 2, 3 and 5 in `items-powerups-fd` and `oracle-cpu-items-captain-ys`), the Bunny Hood in 14 (picked up in 9), the Metal Box in 8 (picked up in 2), the Cloaking Device in 7 (picked up in 7). No replay throws a power-up (state 3) or skids one (state 4); the Barrel's state 4 isn't reached by a replay either |
| Mutation | a probe build (never committed) whose shared `still`, of both families, lifts an item of the kind an environment variable names by 0.25 each call, switched on for one kind at a time on the goldens: the Capsule `items-ported-fd` tick 1029, the Crate 3523, the Barrel 1623, the Party Ball 1140, the Warp Star `items-powerups-fd` 511, the Bunny Hood 3813, the Metal Box 5983, the Cloaking Device 2266, each its kind's first frame out; switched off, both replays match their goldens. On Training's item session (`tr-items`, the item menu dropping each item) the same probe fails each power-up: the Warp Star at tick 3537, the Metal Box 3569, the Bunny Hood 3601, the Cloaking Device 3973; switched on for a container it compares clean (6,776 ticks): Training's menu drops none of them. Of the other sessions in the subset, only the debug item session reaches a container: switched on for the Barrel, `zelda-items` fails at tick 4489; switched on for the Capsule, the Crate or the Party Ball, `zelda-items` (7,049 ticks), `coin-ys` (9,275), `un-held` (13,162) and `un-reset` (9,045) all compare clean, as does the Barrel in the last three. **No session of the subset reaches the Capsule, the Crate or the Party Ball**; the replays above do, each kind in all its states |
| Sessions | `melee-oracle --test session` at two threads, the four whose matches drop or spawn items: `training_from_its_select` (`tr-items`, Training's item menu dropping every item), `training_unloads_its_computer_players` (items dropped among joining players), `the_debug_only_code` (`zelda-items`, the Barrel), `a_vs_coin_match_on_yoshis_story`: all pass (53 minutes); by the probe, `tr-items` reaches the four power-ups and `zelda-items` the Barrel |
