### Task 27, the stage boundary

**Done (2026-11-24, `refactor/stage-boundary` from `2389110c`, batch 16's base).** `StageInfo`'s 49 fields (owned since [own-structs](#design-call-3s-second-half-the-owned-structs)) and `gr::Globals`' 76 are private to `game::gr` (`pub(in crate::game::gr)`, since `gr`'s 59 files share them). Everything else reaches them through accessors and named operations on the two structs and `gr`'s functions; the compiler holds it, and `game::boundary_tests` holds the fields private and the raw parts to the heap's image. The state's shape and field order, the comparer's mirror (`#[derive(Mirror)]`, generated beside the struct) and saved states are unchanged.

**Who reached it before.** Measured by making the fields private at `2389110c` and counting the compiler's errors over the workspace (`--all-targets`, in seven rounds, since a crate's errors hide its dependents'), so the list is exact: **396 sites in 82 files**, `StageInfo` 123, `Globals` 273. The survey's estimate (165 lines in 64 files outside `gr`, the method calls among them) counted game code only; the tools' importer is most of the rest.

| Where | Sites | Files | What it reached |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `game::ft` | 53 | 24 | the stage's kind (the computer players' stage cases, Flat Zone's depth, the terrain's sounds, Yoshi's egg), the camera's settings and blast zones (`ftCo_800D3158` and the fighters' bounds, the fixed zoom), the winds (8 blasts), the devices' registry (the bury, wind and collision devices), the collision callbacks' stage parameters (`StageMaps`: Yoshi's Island, Great Bay, Onett, Mushroom Kingdom, Big Blue, Mute City's track, the dynamic areas, the map objects), the line-touch hook, the barrel, the game mode |
| `game::camera` | 25 | 1 | the camera's settings (24) and the acid's level, the quake models |
| `game::gm` | 23 | 10 | the stage's kind, the targets left and their count (the results, the clears), a bonus stage's end (`Ground_801C1D84`), the entry points' switches, a special stage's respawn zone and the camera's offsets it moves, the stage's parameters, the game mode |
| `game::it` | 20 | 14 | the winds (9 blasts), the enemy spawner's slots (the enemies' destroy and KO), the stage's item descriptions and scripts, the item counts (`Ground_801C2AD8`), the stage kind |
| `game::export`, `disc` | 12 | 2 | the start hooks, the spawner's slots, the stage's render leftovers and collision data, Stadium's screens and the reflection, the winds: the heap's image, and whose heap holds the spawner's slots |
| `game::render` | 9 | 1 | the switches, the guide points and the fog (`Ground_801C4FAC`, `Ground_801C1E2C`) |
| `game::live` | 7 | 1 | the handoff's update counter and stamp, the start points, the switches |
| `lb` | 7 | 2 | the winds and their level (the swing chains), the shadows' intensity |
| `game::vi`, `vs_match`, `session`, `state`, `hud`, `db`, `slippi`, `player_codes` | 20 | 13 | the start points the intros place, the game mode, Stadium's files and buffer, the stage kind, the defaults, Stadium's screens and the reflection freed, the targets, the parameters, the player camera code's values |
| `game::mirror` (its tests) | 4 | 1 | the comparer's labels test |
| melee-render, melee-player | 12 | 3 | the magnifier's parameters, Stadium's screens and the reflection (5); its frame test (2); `record_tests`' counters (5) |
| the importer and comparer (`tools/capture`) | 169 | 4 | every field, imported and compared |
| leaf-check, the bench's tests, the oracle, render-check | 36 | 8 | the setups and checks of the stage's leaves, the wind's leaves, the cars' state, the defaults, the stage's kind |

**What crosses the line now** (game code outside `gr`):

- **Accessors, 31** (15 on `StageInfo`, 16 on `Globals`), named for what they read: the stage's kind (`ground_kind_id`, beside `ground_kind`, which panics on a number outside the game's list; the readers compared the number, so the number stays), the camera's settings (`camera_settings`, lent read-only as a record: `StageCameraInfo`'s own fields stay public), the blast zones, the switches (`bits`), `acid_level`, `has_quake_models`, `touch_line_callback`, `map_gobj`, `barrel`, `targets_left`, `target_count`, `route_zone`, `bgm`, `item_counts`; on `Globals` the winds and their level (`winds`, `winds_mut`, `wind_level`: `lb`'s pool, which the original keeps beside the stage's, lent whole), the devices' registry (read-only), `start_points`, `game_mode`, `stage_kind`, `stage_article`, `yaku_script`, `ground_param`, a stage's `yakumono(GrKind)` for the collision callbacks, `mute_city_track_live`, `dynamic_attrs`, `stadium_screen`, `izumi_reflection`, `coll_stamp`. They replace 184 of the game code's sites (melee-render's 5 among them).
- **Named writes, 12:** `set_camera_offset` (a special stage's respawn), `set_camera_motion` (the player camera code, `C2 801C0A50`), `carry_bits_from` (the live match's handoff), `set_start_point`, `set_game_mode`, `set_stage_kind`, `set_defaults`, `set_stadium_file`, `set_stadium_buffer`, `set_coll_stamp`, `set_wind_level`, and `gr::ground::set_generation` beside `generation`.
- **The raw parts, 8 calls in two files:** `StageInfo::raw_parts()` and `Globals::raw_parts()` (`gr::parts`, written from the structs by a scratch script) lay the state open whole for the heap's image (`export`: the start hooks, the spawner's slots, the render leftovers, the collision data) and the disc's check of whose heap holds the spawner's slots (`disc`). The importer, leaf-check and tests use them and the `_mut` pair (about 200 calls). **Every part is a reference**, unlike the geometry's (which copies its scalars): a write through `raw_parts()` doesn't compile, where the collision boundary's lost two writes silently; reads of a scalar take a `*`.

Six pieces of the stage's own logic were being done outside it, and moved in: the stage's fog (`Ground_801C4FAC` and `Ground_801C1E2C`, `render` to `gr::display`), the item counts (`Ground_801C2AD8`, `it::spawn` to `StageInfo::item_counts`), the spawner's halves of the enemies' destroy and KO callbacks (`grZakoGenerator_801CAC14` and `801CACB8`, `it::zako` to `gr::zako`'s `trophy_coin_left`, `enemy_destroyed`, `enemy_kod`; the aliases stay on the item callbacks that inline them), a bonus stage's end (`Ground_801C1D84`, `bonus_stage_ended`), the player camera code's nine writes and the update counter's handoff (`live`'s match on the geometry). The `#[doc(alias)]` attributes are the same 22,396 before and after (a sorted list diffed); none was added.

**What it cost.** `gr` 1,039 lines more (the raw parts 571, the accessors and operations about 320, the moved fog 99, the spawner's halves 47); 89 files changed, 1,642 lines in and 688 out, most of the tools' 205 rewritten by a scratch fixer over rustc's diagnostics (`*X.raw_parts_mut().f = v` for a whole field, the `_mut` door where the line writes or borrows mutably). No behaviour changed, so no ruleset, oracle or golden change.

**What generalized, and what didn't.**

- Generalized: the compiler's list, private-to-the-tree fields, accessors named for meaning, logic moved in where a reader needed several fields, one door for the tools, the audit's table (one more row).
- The stage's line was crossed by data much as the camera's was, but by readers rather than by the module's own getters: most of the 179 game-code sites read one value (the kind, a camera setting, a count), so a getter each, not methods over `Running`, was the whole change. The stage's state also holds three other systems' data the original keeps in `gr`'s globals (the joints' library's winds, `ftdevice.c`'s registry, the enemy spawner the items' callbacks free), and the camera's rule for the debug cameras fits them: lent whole by one named method, or the logic moved in where the reader wrote it.
- References for every part close the collision boundary's lost-write trap at the cost of `*` on scalar reads in the tools.
- `StageInfo`'s records (the camera's settings, the blast zones, the switches) are lent read-only with public fields: a reader that wants a setting names it, as the original's `Stage_GetCam*` inlines did. Fencing those too would be a getter per setting (24 for the camera's) for no reader that writes them.
- Not covered: the stage objects' `Ground` and the grounds' pool (`game.grounds`), which the fighters' and items' collision callbacks and the camera's subjects reach by id; a third line, if wanted.

**Checks.**

- **The whole state, tick by tick:** the goldens of the verify list's 330 replay diffs (`v5-b16`, as checked equal at batch 16's base: every character, the bench matches, the 32 stage replays, Slippi 3.19, 3.16 and 3.17, players' codes, the items), checked by the branch's oracle with the goldens' coverage line set to the branch's (its comparer's sources changed only by `tools/capture`'s reads through the raw parts; `golden.rs`, `state_eq.rs`, `mirror_gen.rs` and `melee-derive` are unchanged, `mirror.rs` only in its labels test, and the hash reads fields in order, not by name): **all 330, 3,552,272 ticks, every tick's state hash the same**.
- **The audit:** `game::boundary_tests` has the stage's row (its sources `game/gr/`, its states `StageInfo` and `Globals`, its readers `export` and `disc`). Mutation-tested, both at once: a raw-parts read of the stage's kind in `ft::phys` fails `subsystems_are_reached_through_their_modules` (`melee-sim/src/game/ft/phys.rs:33: let grkind = *self.game.stage_info.raw_parts().grkind;`), and `Globals::winds` made `pub` fails `the_subsystems_states_keep_their_fields_private` (`the stage (task 27): melee-sim/src/game/gr/mod.rs: Globals: pub winds: crate::lb::wind::Winds,`).
- **Oracle:** `--test ruleset` at one thread: all 12 pass (the live matches' handoffs among them). `--test session`, 12 named for the stages: `matches_from_the_menus_on_three_stages` (Corneria, Kongo Jungle, Pokémon Stadium), `a_team_battle_with_handicaps`, `the_attract_match_on_brinstar_reads_a_leftover` (Brinstar's bubbles, Pokémon Stadium from the selects' preloads), `an_idle_movie_plays_the_attract_match`, `the_unlock_sessions_end_with_their_cards` (Hyrule Temple), `brinstar_escape_in_debug_vs` (the pause), `all_star` (Pokémon Stadium's screen, Corneria's intro), `the_switches_left_as_they_come_in` (Corneria's hull), `camera_mode_takes_and_saves_a_snapshot` (the pause camera), `special_melee_sessions`, `a_second_match_sets_its_spawner_up_again`, `single_player_intros` (the intros' start points, Adventure's enemies): all pass.
- `sync-test --rollback 7`: `fox-marth-ps` (`--fp hardware`, 13,579 ticks checked), `stage-corneria` (14,518) and Sarah's 3.19 Pokémon Stadium replay `Game_20260129T214824` (`--fp slippi`, 14,140): no difference.
- leaf-check: `stages` (177, 38 minutes), `stage` (62), `camera` (39), `mplib` (1), `fighter` (38, the winds' leaves), `display` (26, `Ground_801C1E2C`), `fog` (1), `items_monsters` (2, the enemies' destroy and KO), `cpu_vs` (`cpu_vs_frame`, and `cpu_vs_changed` in four shard processes, 2 h 49 min to 3 h each): all pass. melee-player's `record_tests` (17); melee-sim's unit tests (205 pass, 2 ignored; the three subsystems' audits among them); `ticks_and_restores_dont_allocate` at one thread; the ledger's `--check` (19,827 of 19,827, regenerated at the tip with no change); `fmt` and `clippy -D warnings`.
- **Speed: owed.** The worker's exclusive runner was queued behind other agents' long jobs (an exclusive request waits for every earlier one, and its runtime budget counts the wait: the first request, `c185d6ea`, timed out in the queue), and this PC was then needed, so no number yet, indicative or deciding. The worker holds both builds (`release`, `-j 3`): `~/Projects/melee-work/wt/stage-boundary/bench-base` (`2389110c`) and `bench-branch` (`08986bf0`, the boundary itself; the later commits change no code a match runs), and `wbench.sh` (the handoff's) runs them alternating in three rounds, `melee-bench native --runs 3` and Sarah's 3.19 replay. The methods are the same field reads and inline (`#[inline]` on every accessor, as task 5 found it needed across codegen units).

### Task 10, the Rollout pilot: done (2026-11-18, `refactor/dedup-rollout` from `aa79a9b8`)

Jigglypuff's Rollout and Kirby's copy of it are one implementation in `ft::purin::special_n`: each shared function takes a `Rollout` (Jigglypuff's `PURIN` beside it, Kirby's `KIRBY` in `ft::kirby::special_purin`) and carries both originals' `#[doc(alias)]`. Kirby's file keeps his 59 addresses, his `Rollout` and his callback table; his init's copy dispatch and leaf-check's two by-name calls (`*_8013DA24`/`*_801010D4`, `*_8013DD54`/`*_8010140C`) pass the descriptor, so no one-line wrappers were needed.

**What differed.** Once the names were normalized (`kb_pr_` to `purin_`, `kb_attr_` to `purin_attr_`), the two files differed only in: the attributes' place (Kirby's `specialn_pr_*` are Jigglypuff's `ftPurinAttributes` offsets plus 0x280, every one of the 38), the `u` word that keeps the model's scale (x4, x8C), the 17 states, five hook addresses (Kirby's death and damage hooks are his generic cleanups, a defect already in [Melee's bugs](melee-bugs.md)), and two branches of Kirby's own: off the ground or landing mid-charge he sets the roll angle before turning `TopN` side-on (`roll_before_top`), and his bounce's landing dust takes the roll's way and angle (`dust_from_roll`, also in Melee's bugs). The squash tables, the ECB, the flags and every operation were the same.

**The numbers.**

| | |
| --- | --- |
| Lines | the two files 2,974 to 1,740; with `purin/mod.rs`' attribute reader folded into `char_attr_*` and the callers, 1,244 fewer (639 added, 1,883 removed) |
| Aliases | the same 18,076 `#[doc(alias)]` attributes before and after (a sorted list diffed); `ledger --check` 19,827 of 19,827, only the CSV's locations moved |
| Time | about an hour of reading and diffing, 40 minutes of editing, then builds and checks: 5 hours of wall time in all, 4 of them the corpus's oracle runs (16.8 hours of job time, four at a time) |
| Oracle | every corpus replay and session with Jigglypuff or Kirby (below) compares with no difference; `sync-test --rollback 7` clean on a Jigglypuff replay (`Game_20260524T180613`, 17,460 ticks) and Kirby's (`oracle-kirby-jigglypuff-fd`, 12,483 ticks) |
| Mutation | the shared `rollout_init` writing -2 for `mv` x4's -1 fails Jigglypuff's `Game_20260524T180613` at tick 3020 and Kirby's `oracle-kirby-jigglypuff-fd` at tick 333, both on `fighter.mv[7]` |
| Speed (indicative, this PC at about 94% busy with other agents) | `melee-bench native`, three alternating rounds of five runs, median of the 15: `Game_20260524T180613` (`MELEE_BENCH_FP=slippi`) 2,623 frames/s before, 2,625 after; `oracle-kirby-jigglypuff-fd` 3,588 before, 3,539 after (−1.4%, inside the runs' spread of 3,455 to 3,715) |

**The corpus, and how it was found.** The verify's replay diffs (`verify-par.sh`'s job list, 329 replays) by their Slippi Game Start's characters (external ids 4 and 15): 51 have Kirby or Jigglypuff (34 with Kirby: his 22 copy CPU matches, six Slippi replays, two catch and four CPU matches; 18 with Jigglypuff, one of them the Kirby–Jigglypuff copy match). The verify's sessions (535 jobs on 533 traces) by the fighter kinds in their traces' fighter regions: 75 traces, 76 session jobs. The trace scan over-counts (some sessions' fighter regions hold other data at that offset), which only adds sessions to run. Each replay ran as a full diff (`main`'s goldens were stale at tick 1 for these), writing a scratch golden; the build from before the change then checked all 52 of those goldens, so the whole state hashes the same before and after at every tick, not only what the comparer reads.

**What was tricky.**

- Finding what reaches the move. Of the corpus's Jigglypuff replays, the two the mutation was first tried on (`roy-puff-fod`, the CPU `oracle-cpu-puff-fod`) pass with it: the mutated word is never seen there; the replay that does, `Game_20260524T180613` (the ledger's check for `ftPr_SpecialS_8013D658`), is only a sync job in the verify, though its trace is in `v5-mixed`. Its diff was added here; the verify should run it as a diff.
- Every `Running` method shares one namespace, so the copies had prefixed private helpers (`kb_pr_mvf` beside `mvf`); the shared code keeps one set (`ro_*`).
- The ledger records each alias's line, so moving aliases rewrites `docs/port-ledger.csv` (123 rows) with no change in status.

**The recipe for the next family.**

1. Diff the pair with the copy's prefixes normalized (`sed` the names), and list each difference by kind: an attribute base (often one offset for all), `u` and `mv` offsets, states, hook addresses, and real branches.
2. Put a descriptor struct in the original's module with a `const` per user, the shared functions taking `(gobj, &Descriptor)`; give each real branch a named field with its reason, and point any Melee's-bugs entry at it.
3. Move every alias of both originals onto the shared items; keep each user's own `run` table (the addresses differ) and pass the descriptor from callers that name a function, rather than keeping wrappers. Diff the sorted alias list before and after, and run `ledger --check`.
4. Find the corpus from the replays' Game Start characters and the sessions' trace fighter kinds, then prove with one mutation in the shared code (a word every use writes) that a replay of each user actually reaches it.
5. Run the corpus with the new build writing scratch goldens (`--write-golden --golden FILE`), check them with the old build, run the sessions in full, sync-test one replay of each user, and bench before and after in alternating rounds.

### Task 11, Kirby's other copies: 16 done, 2 left as they are (round one 2026-11-18, `refactor/dedup-kirby` from `909b868c`; [round two](#task-11-round-two) 2026-11-19, `refactor/dedup-kirby-2` from `4770a3ca`; [Master Hand and Crazy Hand](#task-11-master-hand-and-crazy-hand) 2026-11-20, `refactor/dedup-hands` from `fd77569a`)

The recipe above, one character at a time, closest copy first: Luigi, Mario (with Dr. Mario), Pikachu (with Pichu), Captain Falcon (with Ganondorf), Donkey Kong, Samus, Zelda. Each original's `special_n.rs` holds the move over a descriptor (`Fireball` twice, `ThunderJolt`, `FalconPunch`, `GiantPunch`, `ChargeShot`, `NayrusLove`) with the character's `const` beside it; Kirby's `ft/kirby/special_<char>.rs` keeps only his addresses, his `KIRBY` and his `run` table. Where the original asks the fighter's kind (Mario or Dr. Mario, Pikachu or Pichu, Captain Falcon or Ganondorf) and Kirby's copy asks his copy's (`u` `HAT_KIND`), the descriptor says which (`ft::kirby::KindFrom`, `Running::special_kind`).

**What differed**, besides the states, the item and effect kinds, the joints and the hooks' addresses, which every pair has:

| Copy | Kirby's attributes | Named branches |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Luigi | none read | none |
| Mario, Dr. Mario | none read; the pill's colours in his `u` x68, x6C | his copy-less kind throws nothing (Mario's own kind never is Kirby's, so the test is shared) |
| Pikachu, Pichu | a block per copy, Pikachu's layout (x1C0, x1DC) | none (the aerial throw's unset landing lag for another copy, in Melee's bugs, stays a stop) |
| Captain Falcon, Ganondorf | Captain Falcon's layout at x1F8 | `wind`: Kirby's Warlock Punch blows no wind |
| Donkey Kong | Donkey Kong's layout 0x164 on; the swings in `u` xBC | `on_any_damage`: his copy sets (and clears) no any-damage hook |
| Samus | Samus's layout 0x150 on; `u` 0xA4 on | `fire_offset` (his fire's position taken with a zero offset), `effects_off_always` (his charge's effects destroyed unguarded, a new entry in Melee's bugs) |
| Zelda | Zelda's layout 0x2A4 on, the reflection 0x37C on | `hooks_on_change`: changing between the ground and the air sets the hitlag hooks at once where Zelda's arms the effect's accessory again |

**The numbers.**

| | |
| --- | --- |
| Lines (the copy's file and the original's `special_n.rs`) | Luigi 285 to 220 (−65), Mario 407 to 324 (−83), Pikachu 422 to 308 (−114), Captain Falcon 447 to 344 (−103), Donkey Kong 995 to 759 (−236), Samus 932 to 667 (−265), Zelda 440 to 335 (−105); with callers and the attribute constants folded, 960 fewer in all (1,420 added, 2,380 removed in `crates` and `tools`) |
| Aliases | the same 18,078 `#[doc(alias)]` attributes after every commit (a sorted list diffed); `ledger --check` 19,827 of 19,827 each time, only the CSV's locations moved |
| Oracle | the base build (`909b868c`) ran the full diff of every verify replay with Kirby or a touched character (external ids 0, 1, 4, 7, 8, 13, 16, 18, 19, 22, 24, 25: 205 replays, and 30 others) and wrote per-tick whole-state goldens; every commit's build then checked the replays with Kirby or its character (52 to 80 each, 450 checks) and the final build all 205: every tick's state hash equal. Kirby's 87 session jobs (the pilot's list): the 52 diffs compare with no difference and 34 of the 35 session-syncs roll back clean; `rec-kirby-throw`'s shortcut stops at tick 508 on the heap's free lists, as it does with the base build (a known difference, not this change's). The trace scan can't tell Mario's kind (0) from zeros, so sessions weren't picked for the other characters: their code is covered by the replays' per-tick hashes. `sync-test --rollback 7` clean on the seven Kirby copy matches (`oracle-kirby-{luigi-bf,mario-fd,pikachu-bf,captain-fd,donkey-bf,zelda-bf}`, 11,565 to 16,272 ticks, and `hf-kirby-samus-fod`, 19,865) |
| Mutation | one write in each shared function, switched on for one user at a time: both users of every pair fail, Kirby's in his copy match and the original's in its own replay (Luigi `dk-luigi-ys` tick 200 and `oracle-kirby-luigi-bf` 2760; Mario `pc-7452c0d4` (Mario against Bowser) 150 and `kirby-mario-fd` 714; Pikachu `mewtwo-pikachu-fod` 3154 and `kirby-pikachu-bf` 833; Captain Falcon `ganon-captain-fd` 519 and `kirby-captain-fd` 704; Donkey Kong `dk-falco-ys` 3649 and `kirby-donkey-bf` 369; Samus `dk-samus-bf` 97 and `hf-kirby-samus-fod` 8771; Zelda `gnw-zelda-ys` 10875 and `kirby-zelda-bf` 4345), so the corpus reaches every copy |
| Leaf-check | Kirby, Luigi, Mario, Dr. Mario, Pikachu, Pichu, Captain Falcon, Ganondorf, Donkey Kong, Samus, Zelda: all pass (Luigi's needs `LUIGI_CALLS`: its harness reads a relative path and otherwise checks none) |
| Speed (indicative, this PC busy with other agents) | `melee-bench native`, three alternating rounds of five runs, median of the 15: `oracle-kirby-mario-fd` 4,776 frames/s before, 4,535 after; `oracle-kirby-zelda-bf` 3,917 before, 4,281 after; the runs spread 3,342 to 5,819, so both differences are noise (`kirby-zelda-bf`'s bench reports "5 of 5 runs differ" before and after alike) |

**Left after round one:** Mewtwo, Ness, Peach, Bowser, Marth and Roy, Sheik, Link and Young Link, the Ice Climbers, Mr. Game & Watch, Yoshi, Fox and Falco, not reached in that run (the corpus's base goldens took most of the day). [Round two](#task-11-round-two) took all but Yoshi and Fox and Falco.

#### Task 11, round two

The same recipe over the 11 copies left, one commit each, closest first: Mewtwo, Ness, Peach, Bowser (with Giga Bowser), Marth (with Roy), Sheik, Link (with Young Link), the Ice Climbers and Mr. Game & Watch share their originals' specials now (`ShadowBall`, `PkFlash`, `Toad`, `FireBreath`, `ShieldBreaker`, `Needles`, `Bow`, `IceShot`, `Chef`); Yoshi's and Fox's and Falco's stay Kirby's own (below). Where a copy has two originals' states (Marth's and Roy's, Link's and Young Link's), the descriptor holds a pair picked by the user's kind (`KindFrom`); a collision callback that told its states apart by its own address takes the phase instead (Mewtwo's and Bowser's `Phase`). A dispatch function of the table needs no new attribute: each copy's `run` keeps its own.

**What differed**, besides the states, attributes, `u` words, joints, item kinds, effects, sounds and the hooks' addresses (Kirby's generic cleanups, `ftKb_Init_800EE74C` and `_800EE7B8`, for every copy):

| Copy | Kirby's attributes, `u` | Named branches |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Mewtwo | Mewtwo's layout 0x384 on; `u` 0x94 on | `on_death3`: no third hook; `effects_off_always`: his charge's effects destroyed unguarded (a new entry in [Melee's bugs](melee-bugs.md#kirby-mewtwos-copy-destroys-his-effects-whether-or-not-the-charges-are-out), Samus's copy's defect again) |
| Ness | Ness's layout 0x1A0 on; `u` 0x68 on | `clears_hooks`: the flash let go of, forgotten or reset leaves his damage hooks |
| Peach | Peach's layout 0xE4 on, the bubble's 0x340 on; `u` 0xB8 on | `held_item_spec`: Toad isn't his special item; `clears_hooks` |
| Bowser, Giga Bowser | Bowser's layout 0x114 on; `u` 0x84 on; a point on his mouth | `sfx_by_kind`: Bowser's sounds for both copies; `air_start_lands_in_loop` and `giga_states` carry the two Melee bugs already recorded ([the landing](melee-bugs.md#kirby-bowsers-copy-lands-from-the-aerial-breaths-start-into-its-loop), [the refill](melee-bugs.md#kirby-giga-bowsers-copy-refills-its-breath-while-breathing)); his first angle group -1 is a setting |
| Marth, Roy | Marth's layout, Marth's at 0x35C and Roy's at 0x370 | `keep` (his sword through every change of state), `wind`: none; `one_flash`: one flash callback for both swings, its effect by whether he's on the ground; `sword`: the copy's sword in his hand at the draw |
| Sheik | Sheik's layout 0x290 on; `u` 0xB4 on | `clears_hooks`: the cancels, the throws' changes and the dropped needles leave his hooks |
| Link, Young Link | Link's layout, Link's at 0x268 and Young Link's at 0x27C; `u` 0x64 on; bones, not parts | `checks_out`: the words cleared without Link's check and boomerang query; `draw_from_zeros`: the draw's points a table of zeros (a new entry in [Melee's bugs](melee-bugs.md#kirby-links-and-young-links-copies-draw-the-bow-from-a-table-of-zeros)); `sword_parts`: no sword model to put back |
| Ice Climbers | their layout 0x398 on; `u` the ice 0xC0 on, the hop 0xA4 on | `clears_hooks`; `hammer`: the copy's hammer in his hand |
| Mr. Game & Watch | his layout 0x3CC on; `u` 0xC0 on | none: his pan (an item Mr. Game & Watch's Chef has no counterpart of) and its five functions stay Kirby's; his callbacks and `keep` (without `SKIP_MODEL`) are settings. The loop takes its frame as `ftGw_SpecialN_Loop` does |

**Left as they are, and why.** Yoshi's copy plays his hat's tongue animations (the copy data's dynamics) at six points of the move, sets his cleanups at every change where Yoshi sets a damage hook only for an item caught, keeps no item flag, catches an item through its own tongue path (`catch_kirby`), lays its egg from his own attributes and sends a victim into his own egg states (`ft::co::yoshi_egg`'s Kirby layer): a named branch at nearly every function. Fox's and Falco's copy holds the blaster at another point and joint, scales it differently, zeroes his speeds at the draw, keeps his hooks where Fox's clear them, picks the shot's kind and sounds by the copy with a return for others, and answers the blaster item's queries itself (the throws mapped in), while Fox's code also fires the throws' shots: more branches than shared lines (18% near-copy). Both would need flags everywhere; each stays as Kirby's own port.

**The numbers.**

| | |
| --- | --- |
| Lines (the copy's file and the original's `special_n.rs`) | Mewtwo 1,375 to 949 (−426), Ness 661 to 503 (−158), Peach 946 to 699 (−247), Bowser 845 to 663 (−182), Marth 1,020 to 701 (−319), Sheik 920 to 676 (−244), Link 989 to 742 (−247), the Ice Climbers 442 to 373 (−69), Mr. Game & Watch 570 to 431 (−139); 2,031 fewer in the pairs, 2,001 in all (3,042 added, 5,043 removed in `crates` and `tools`) |
| Aliases | the same 18,078 `#[doc(alias)]` attributes after every commit (a sorted list diffed); `ledger --check` 19,827 of 19,827 each time, only the CSV's locations moved |
| Oracle | the base build (`4770a3ca`) ran the full diff of every verify replay with Kirby or a touched character (296 of the 330, every one but 34 without any of them) and wrote per-tick whole-state goldens (12.8 hours of job time, no difference); each commit's build then checked the replays with Kirby or its characters (47 to 100 each, 590 checks) and the final build all 296: every tick's state hash equal. Kirby's 87 session jobs (the pilot's list): the 52 diffs compare with no difference and 34 of the 35 session-syncs roll back clean; `rec-kirby-throw`'s shortcut stops at tick 508 on the heap's free lists, with the base build too (as in round one). The touched characters' other sessions (about 240 traces by the trace scan) weren't run: their code is covered by the replays' per-tick hashes. `sync-test --rollback 7 --fp hardware` clean on eleven Kirby copy matches (`oracle-kirby-{mewtwo-bf,ness-fd,peach-fd,bowser-fd,marth-fd,roy-bf,zelda-bf,link-bf,younglink-fd,ics-fd,gnw-bf}`, 9,454 to 19,544 ticks each) |
| Mutation | one write in each shared function, switched on for one user at a time: both users of every pair fail, Kirby's in his copy match and the original's in its own replay (Mewtwo `oracle-cpu-mewtwo-dl` tick 391 and `kirby-mewtwo-bf` 1206; Ness `oracle-cpu-approach-ness-fd` 912 and `kirby-ness-fd` 323; Peach `oracle-cpu-peach-fod` 4880 and `kirby-peach-fd` 1073; Bowser `oracle-cpu-bowser-ps` 462 and `kirby-bowser-fd` 768; Marth `fox-marth-fd` 1999, Roy `oracle-cpu-roy-fd` 5173, `kirby-marth-fd` 260 and `kirby-roy-bf` 1657; Sheik `oracle-cpu-sheik-ys` 99 and `kirby-zelda-bf` (his Sheik copy) 9659; Link `oracle-cpu-link-dl` 1039, Young Link `oracle-cpu-ylink-ys` 97, `kirby-link-bf` 329 and `kirby-younglink-fd` 674; the Ice Climbers `oracle-cpu-ics-fd-interp` 90 and `kirby-ics-fd` 1348; Mr. Game & Watch `hf-gnw-falco-fd-7b66bba4` 1552 and `kirby-gnw-bf` 1680), so the corpus reaches every copy. The first Marth write (in the charge) reached no replay of either: CPUs and players tap the move, so it moved to the draw |
| Leaf-check | Kirby, Mewtwo, Ness, Peach, Bowser, Marth, Roy, Sheik, Link, Young Link, the Ice Climbers (`nana`), Mr. Game & Watch: all pass |

The command, for the worker: `melee-bench native --dir DIR --runs 5 kirby-mewtwo-bf kirby-marth-fd`, the two replays copied from `local/bench/oracle-kirby3/` |

### Task 11, the teleports

**Done (2026-11-20, `refactor/dedup-teleports` from `fd77569a`).** Zelda's Farore's Wind, Sheik's Vanish and Mewtwo's Teleport are one implementation in `ft::zelda::special_hi` over a `Teleport` (Zelda's `ZELDA` beside it, Sheik's `SHEIK` and Mewtwo's `MEWTWO` in their own files with their own effects and `run` tables); Zelda's and Sheik's down specials, the two sides of the transformation, are one in `ft::zelda::special_lw` over a `Transform` (Sheik's `SHEIK` in her file). Each shared function carries all its originals' `#[doc(alias)]`; nothing outside the five files named a function of them (leaf-check dispatches by address), so no one-line wrappers were kept. One commit each: `aae331fb` (the teleport), `5b482246` (the transformation).

**What differed.** All three teleports have six states in the same order (wind-up, travel, reappearance, on the ground then in the air), one attribute layout at a different place in each character's attributes (Zelda's from x38, Mewtwo's from x40, Sheik's from x28: she has no first speed divisor and keeps her hop in the second's place), the same `mv` words and the same transition flags (Sheik's written as numbers, `0xC4C508E` and `0xC4C508A`, equal to Zelda's named ones). Beyond the states, attributes and accessories' addresses, the named settings:

| Setting | Whose | What |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `windup: Windup::Hop` | Sheik | her wind-up clears its variables before the state change and sets no accessory, hops in the air (where the others are stopped, or divide their speeds down), and drifts while it falls (the others slow without drift) |
| `wind` | Zelda | a wind at the wind-up's start (`lb_800119DC` at part 4) |
| `vanish_at_travel` | Sheik | the travel's start sets her explosion's accessory |
| `travel_sfx` | Mewtwo | a sound as the travel starts |
| `end_to_air_cb` | Sheik | leaving the ground as she reappears sets the reappearance's accessory again |
| `appear_part`, `appear_gfx`, `appear_pos_first` | all | the reappearance's effect: at the hip (Mewtwo's waist), none for Mewtwo (he takes the position and makes nothing), Zelda's position taken before the effect's test |

The wind-up's effects stay each character's: Zelda's on her top joint by ground or air, Mewtwo's at his waist, Sheik's explosion (the flash item, its effect and sound). The transformation differs in nothing but settings: the states, the attributes' place (Zelda's x70, Sheik's x60), the part the first half's effect follows (Zelda's 104, Sheik's 43), its three accessories and the other side's second half.

**A Melee bug found:** the wind-up's changes between the ground and the air set Sheik's explosion as the accessory (a copy of Zelda's, which sets her wind-up's effect there), so her Vanish explodes when its wind-up lands or leaves the ground, then again when the travel starts ([Melee's bugs](melee-bugs.md#sheik-vanish-explodes-early-when-the-wind-up-changes-between-the-ground-and-the-air)): three corpus replays do it.

**The numbers.**

| | |
| --- | --- |
| Lines | the three up specials 1,828 to 1,077 (−751), the two down specials 469 to 360 (−109); with the three characters' attribute and `mv` constants the move no longer names, 939 fewer in all (795 added, 1,734 removed in `crates`) |
| Aliases | the same 18,078 `#[doc(alias)]` attributes after each commit (a sorted list diffed); `ledger --check` 19,827 of 19,827 each time, only the CSV's locations moved |
| Oracle | the verify's 65 replay diffs with Zelda, Sheik or Mewtwo (their Slippi Game Start's external ids 18, 19, 10): the base build (`fd77569a`) checked the char-registry round's goldens of all 65 (written at the same commit) with no difference, so they are the base's per-tick whole-state hashes; each commit's build then checked all 65: 760,265 ticks, every tick's state hash equal. Sessions: the verify's session jobs whose traces have a Zelda, Sheik or Mewtwo fighter kind (the Kirby round's trace scan: 33 session diffs and the 30 session-syncs on their inputs): all 33 diffs compare with no difference (from 1,486 to 55,022 ticks), and the 17 shorter session-syncs roll back clean (33 jobs ran on the worker, the 17 whose traces or inputs it lacks on this PC); the 13 longer session-syncs (29 to 195 minutes each, 17.4 hours of job time by the verify's durations) weren't run: `sync-test` covers rollback on each character's replay |
| Mutation | one write in each shared move, switched on for one user at a time (the travel's frames one more; the second half from one frame later): Zelda's teleport fails 9 of her 17 replays (first `gnw-zelda-ys-4e9d13cb` at tick 677), Sheik's 35 of 37 (`oracle-cpu-stage-brinstar` at 264), Mewtwo's 13 of 17 (`mewtwo-pikachu-fod-aaf066d1` at 103), Zelda's transformation 12 of 17 (`items-zelda-flower2-fd` at 438), Sheik's 25 of 37 (`items-zelda-flower2-fd` at 444): the corpus reaches every user of both |
| `sync-test --rollback 7 --fp hardware` | clean on `items-zelda-flower2-fd` (Zelda's teleport and both transformations, 7,318 ticks), `oracle-cpu-stage-brinstar` (Sheik, 10,918) and `oracle-cpu-l1-gnw-l5-mewtwo-fod` (Mewtwo, 10,918) |
| Leaf-check | `zelda` (both tests, the transformation's captures too), `sheik`, `mewtwo`: all pass |
| Speed (indicative, this PC with other agents' work on it) | `melee-bench native --runs 5`, three alternating rounds of the base and final builds, median of the 15: `cpu-l1-gnw-l5-mewtwo-fod` 2,231 frames/s before, 2,232 after; `cpu-stage-brinstar` (Sheik) 1,381 before, 1,375 after (−0.4%); within the runs' spread (2,171 to 2,324 and 1,337 to 1,440): no change. The command, for the worker: `melee-bench native --dir DIR --runs 5 cpu-l1-gnw-l5-mewtwo-fod cpu-stage-brinstar`, the two replays copied from `local/bench/oracle-m4/`; the Mewtwo match's bench reports its runs differing from the replay with the base build too |

**Left:** nothing of the family. The three wind-up effects and Sheik's explosion are each character's own code, not copies.

### Task 11, the enemies: done (2026-11-20, `refactor/dedup-enemies` from `75bc50b4`; [round two](#task-11-the-enemies-round-two) 2026-11-21, `refactor/dedup-enemies-2` from `662858bf`)

The Goomba, ReDead, Octorok, Topi, Polar Bear and Like Like share one implementation of every part where they were near-copies: being hit, beaten and flung, the knockback's start, falling, flight and the do-nothing callbacks, the looping animations, the turn's step and the walk's velocity. Each is a function in `it::zako` (the enemies' common code, `itzako.c`) over a `zako::Enemy`, a `const` beside each enemy (`GOOMBA`, `REDEAD`, `OCTOROK`, `TOPI`, `POLAR_BEAR`, `LIKE_LIKE`), and carries every original's alias; each enemy's `run_event` and `run_pred` keep its own addresses and call the shared function with its settings (`DMG_RECEIVED => zako::damaged(r, gobj, &OCTOROK)`, `ANIM6 => zako::looped(r, gobj, 6, change::ANIM_UPDATE)`). The enemies' modules are named after the enemies now (`kinds::goomba`, `redead`, `octorok`, `topi`, `like_like`, `polar_bear`), each keeping its decompilation name (`oldkuri`, `leadead`, `octarock`, `oldottosea`, `likelike`, `whitebea`) as a list-form alias on its `mod` line.

**What's shared, and how each enemy differs** (besides its states, flags and addresses, which every function takes):

| Shared (`it::zako`) | Originals | Named settings and branches |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `damaged` | the damage callbacks of the Goomba (`it_2725_Logic0_DmgReceived`), Octorok (`it_802E4B00`), Topi (`it_2725_Logic8_DmgReceived`), Polar Bear (`it_802E3884`) and Like Like (`it_2725_Logic5_DmgReceived`) | `on_hit` (the Like Like spits out what it swallowed), `keeps_facing` (all but the Goomba keep the way they faced for the knockback), `thrown` (the state a hit beats it in), `on_beaten` (Topi lets its Freezie go), `knocked` (its own knockback) |
| `defeat`, `fling_away` (the inline `Item_ZakoDefeat` and its draw) | all six; the ReDead's own damage callback (`it_802E8CD8`, too different to share: it lets go, dodges, shrugs knockbacks off) calls them | `stompable` (the Goomba's stomp cleared), `clears_hits` (all but the ReDead) |
| `fling`, `fling_to_screen` | `it_802D82C4`, `it_802EA2A0`, `it_802E57D4`, `it_802E3400`, `it_802E48B4`, `it_802DC310`; `it_802D839C`, `it_802EA334`, `it_802E58A0`, `it_802E3528`, `it_802E4980`, `it_802DC3DC` | `flung_checks_blast_zones` (Topi), `flung_keeps_facing` (the ReDead), `flung_upright` (the Goomba) |
| `knock_back` | the start of the Octorok's, ReDead's (both), Topi's and the Polar Bear's knockbacks and the Goomba's inline one | `Knock::AtLeast` (0.2 as a double for the Goomba, as a single for the ReDead and Octorok), `Free` (Topi), `Fifth` (the Polar Bear) |
| `fall_physics`, `flight_physics`, `knocked_physics`, `still`, `answers_false`, `looped`, `knocked_anim`, `knocked_coll`, `flight_lands`, `bounce` | 120 callbacks of the six (`*_UnkMotionN_Phys`, `_Anim`, `_Coll`) that were each a few lines of the same code | the state and flags, the landing and falling callbacks |
| `turning`, `walk_velocity` | the ReDead's, Topi's and the Polar Bear's turns; the Goomba's, ReDead's and Polar Bear's walks | the timer's word |

The attributes' readers (`attr`, `health`, `walk_speed`), the variables' words (`var`), the collision's facing, the stop and `Item_InitZakoCollision` are shared helpers in `zako` too.

**The "Old" copies.** The executable's logic tables give the Old Goomba (kind 208) the Goomba's callbacks and the Old Topi (216) Topi's (`STATES_GOOMBA` and `STATES_TOPI` name both kinds), and the Old ReDead and Old Octorok (210, 211) have none: the original is already one implementation over two kinds' data (each kind's attributes, read through its article), and melee-hd's registry already ran both kinds through one module. Nothing changed there.

**Left as they are, and why.** The walks themselves, the stands and the attacks differ in more than settings: the Goomba walks a round each way and is stomped; the ReDead lunges, grabs and holds a fighter, dodges and lies down; the Octorok hops by its bone and spits stones; Topi pushes a Freezie and charges; the Polar Bear jumps and speeds Icicle Mountain's climb; the Like Like clings to ceilings, lunges, swallows and chews. Their thrown, dropped and picked-up callbacks are three lines each over different variables. The Koopas, the Shy Guys and the stages' other enemies weren't in this family; [round two](#task-11-the-enemies-round-two) put them on the same `zako` functions. `zako::set_x1c` and its siblings keep their names (task 12's wave 3: nothing but the comparer reads those bits).

**A defect found.** Topi's and the Polar Bear's knockbacks put them in the air before asking the knockback whether it points off the floor, which `it_8027B798` answers only for an item on the ground, so both always land their knockbacks on the ground and the Polar Bear's airborne branch never runs: [Melee's bugs](melee-bugs.md#topis-and-the-polar-bears-knockbacks-never-leave-the-ground). The shared `knock_back` keeps it (`Knock::Free`, `Knock::Fifth`).

**The numbers.**

| | |
| --- | --- |
| Lines | the six modules and `zako.rs` 6,289 to 5,541 (−748): the modules 5,394 to 4,143, `zako.rs` 895 to 1,398; 742 fewer in all (946 added, 1,688 removed in `crates` and `tools`) |
| Aliases | the same 18,078 `#[doc(alias = "...")]` attributes and the same pending ports (`// Ports X; aliased once a check reaches it`) before and after (sorted lists diffed); `ledger --check` 19,827 of 19,827 after each commit, only `docs/port-ledger.csv`'s locations moved |
| Whole state | the base (`75bc50b4`) ran the full diff of 97 of the verify's replays (task 4's 82, every `items-*`, `stage-*`, items-on CPU and Ice Climbers diff, the coin matches and the benchmark's Vanilla replays; the containers' monsters, `monsters-*`, 11; the grabs that catch them, `catch-grab-*`, 4), each clean, writing per-tick goldens: 1,070,527 ticks. Both commits' builds checked every golden: every tick's state hash the same |
| Sessions | 37 sessions from the verify's milestone 6 list in full, `--all --heap`, with the branch's build: the Mushroom Kingdom route (`mk-*`, 5), Icicle Mountain's climb (`im-*`, 5), the Underground Maze (`mz-*`, 8), the Adventure runs and debug routes through them (`m6-adventure2`, `m6-meikyu`, `m6-stages-1p` three, `m6i-adv-*` four, `m6ad*` four, `m6u-adv-*` three, `m6pb-im-polar`, `m6ac-mk-slope9`, `sel1p-adventure`): 279,972 ticks, each its expected tick count with no difference. Sessions have no golden mode, so they compare against ssbm-rs's traces |
| Oracle tests | `--test single_player`: the seven Adventure runs (`adventures_*`) and `each_mode_runs_to_its_first_stop`, 8 passed; `--test session`: `adventure_game_over`, `icicle_mountain_climb_in_debug_vs`, `single_player_setups`, `single_player_intros`, `a_brick_blocks_item_pops_out_at_random`, `the_debug_menus_go_back_laid_out`, 6 passed |
| Rollback | `sync-test --rollback 7 --fp hardware`: `monsters-goomba-fd`, `monsters-redead-fd`, `monsters-octorok-ps`, `monsters-ottosea-freezie-fd` (7,318 ticks checked each); `session-sync --rollback 7 --shortcut`: `m6ad4-ll-edge` (the Like Like, 2,068 ticks), `im-bears` (the Polar Bear, 3,770), `mk-enemies` (the route's Goombas, 6,227), `mz-capture` (the maze's ReDeads and Like Likes, 2,747): no difference |
| Mutation | a build with the shared `fall_physics` setting a falling enemy's vertical speed to 0.5 and `add_hit_damage` adding 1, switched on per kind: each enemy's check fails, the Goomba `monsters-goomba-fd` tick 1330 and `mk-enemies` 1357, the ReDead `monsters-redead-fd` 877, the Octorok `monsters-octorok-bf` 1205, Topi `monsters-ottosea-fd` 1018 and `im-look` 1640, the Like Like `ll-edge` 2381, the Polar Bear `im-bears` 2079. Switched on for every kind, 31 of the 37 sessions fail (the six that pass have no enemy: `sel1p-adventure`, `m6-stages-1p-b`, `-c`, `m6ad-adv-giga-vh`, `m6u-adv-gw`, `m6u-adv-ylink`). **The Old Goomba and Old Topi (208, 216) are reached by nothing**: switched on for them alone, all 37 sessions and the 15 enemy replays compare clean (the route's Goombas are kind 43, the climb's Topi 46). Their code is the Goomba's and Topi's, which the corpus reaches |
| Leaf-check and tests | leaf-check's `items_monsters` (2, from the monsters' and grabs' captures), `items_on` (5) and `items` in three shards (585): all pass; melee-sim's unit tests (171, 2 ignored); `ticks_and_restores_dont_allocate` at one thread; `fmt` and `clippy -D warnings` |

Pichu runs Pikachu's code already |

The name entry's two variant tables differ (the katakana's last two keys aren't the hiragana's) |

#### Task 11, the stage pairs

**Done (2026-11-20, `refactor/dedup-stages` from `510e5139`), one commit a pair.** The recipe above over the survey's three stage families: Corneria and Venom (`grcorneria.c`, `grvenom.c`), Kongo Jungle and Kongo Jungle N64 (`grkongo.c`, `groldkongo.c`), and the race and the Underground Maze (`grpushon.c`, `grshrineroute.c`; the survey's third pair, `gr/pushon` and `gr/shrineroute`). Each shared part is one Rust item in one of its stages' modules over a descriptor, the other stage's constant beside its own code, both originals' `#[doc(alias)]` on each shared function; each stage's `run` keeps its addresses and passes its constant.

| Pair | Shared, over | Each stage's settings and named branches | Left as it is, and why |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Corneria and Venom | the Arwings in `gr::corneria` over `Arwings` (`corneria::ARWINGS`, `venom::ARWINGS`): the flight path's setup and process, the path's sounds (`path_sound_near`, inline in Venom's), the wings' setup and process, the flying Arwings' setups and process, the blast zones' checks, and the spawns' tail (`send_arwing`, `pick_group`, `demo_type`) | the statics' slots, parameters' offsets, maps, frames, tables (types' and rolls' animations, group maps, joints, parts), the close types' range (1 to 9, 1 to 7), the far Arwing's pinned path joint (4, 5), the wing's depth (10, 50), the blast zones' margin and front, sounds; `PathScale` (Corneria scales every path's x, Venom a close path's z), `rides_ship` (Corneria's paths ride the Great Fox's height), `FarPath` (Corneria's far paths side on with their own sounds, lowered with the sinking Great Fox; Venom's face on with the close sounds), `close_turn`, `close_fire` (only Corneria's close Arwings fire straight ahead), and the far shot's decision, window and side (`fires`, `far_window`, `same_side`). Venom's flying Arwing of no type now takes the original's own `Ground_DisableStarFoxArwingGObjs` (the same as Corneria's) where its port stopped; no Venom type reaches it | the Great Fox: Corneria's drifts, sinks and fires its guns, Venom's flies the canyon by its animation's cues, no near-copied lines; each stage's `spawn_arwings` (Corneria's types by the city's end, Venom's by the Great Fox's held groups) and `path_pos` (Corneria's seen from the side by the drift, at least 100 high; Venom's its joint 5). The team's talk was already one copy |
| Kongo Jungle and Kongo Jungle N64 | the barrel in `gr::kongo_jungle` over `Barrel` (`kongo_jungle::BARREL`, `kongo_jungle_64::BARREL`): the setup, the turn and swing (`turn_and_swing`, the processes' first part), the directions, the fighter catch, release and drop | the variables' two layouts behind one view (`BarrelView`: Kongo Jungle N64's has its shot timer after `xCE`, the rest four bytes on), the parameters' offsets (Kongo Jungle N64's stop and interval are Kongo Jungle's run and pause, its shot's bounds the hold's), `catch_anim` (Kongo Jungle N64's barrel plays its catch's animation) | what each barrel holds and its shot, the end of each process: Kongo Jungle's catches Klap Traps and shoots at once, then waits for none near; Kongo Jungle N64's plays the shot's animation and shoots ten ticks later. The river, the Klap Traps, the bridge and the events are Kongo Jungle's alone (Kongo Jungle N64 has no water), the birds Kongo Jungle N64's. `ft`'s callers keep one-line calls (`barrel_catch`, `barrel_release`, `barrel_drop`) |
| the race and the Underground Maze | the lights in `gr::race_to_the_finish` over `Lights` (`race_to_the_finish::LIGHTS`, `underground_maze::LIGHTS`): the lights' object's setup, the added lights' loader, and the lights' process | how the fighters' lights are found, the stage's table of lights (its own `set_up_lights`), the two added lights' descriptors, how many show (5, 6) and the follower's height (100, 15) | everything else: the course and its goals, the maze, its symbols, portal and arenas share no code |

The brief named the third family "Mushroom Kingdom II's routes"; the survey's family, and the one near-copied, is the race and the maze (`kinokoroute`, Adventure's Mushroom Kingdom, is in no pair of its scan).

#### Task 11, Master Hand and Crazy Hand

**Done (2026-11-20, `refactor/dedup-hands` from `fd77569a`).** The original's Crazy Hand is Master Hand's code copied over his own attributes, states and functions (his states keep Master Hand's numbers and the decomp's `ftMh_MS_*` names, which name Master Hand's moves, not his). The code both hands run is now one implementation in `ft::masterhand`, each function taking the hand's settings (`masterhand::Hand`, each hand's `HAND` in his `mod.rs`) and carrying both originals' `#[doc(alias)]`. Each hand keeps his addresses, his attribute and variable offsets, his `run` tables (whose arms pass his `HAND`) and his own moves. Callers that named one hand's function pass the descriptor instead (`co::enter`, `co::catch`, `co::stamina`, `create`, `resolve`, `camera` and the registry's hooks; `Hand::of` takes a fighter kind to its hand). Four commits, one group each:

| Group | Shared | Settings and named branches |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Waits, glides, entrance, common callbacks | the attribute readers (`Hand::f32`, `i32`, `point`); the start, the waits, the attack table's draw, the glides, the return and the attack timer; the entrance, its camera script and the waist point; the empty, input, air-motion and return-on-end callbacks, and entering a state from its first frame (Crazy Hand's trivial state entries are `hand_enter` arms) | his place, glide and timer attributes, wait and glide states, his table (attacks, rows, next rows), its starts and his human button map (`start_attack`, `human_attack`, each hand's own), the entrance's state, script, waist, roar and facing. Named: `cries_waiting` (Crazy Hand cries on the script's flag while waiting and clears it as a wait starts), `tags_from_wait` (Master Hand's timer joins Crazy Hand's waiting tag attack), `fewer_rows_alone` (Crazy Hand draws from four rows once Master Hand is beaten), `return_mark` (the return marks Master Hand as in his second glide and Crazy Hand as in his first, so Crazy Hand's return keeps the glide's frame) |
| The grab | the reach and its chase, the grab, the hand closing on a catch or on nothing, the miss, letting go, the squeeze, the two throws, and the victim's caught, squeezed, waiting, cut and thrown states | `Grab`: the nine states, the reach, chase, squeeze and let-go attributes, the grab box's size (128 and 0x100) and callbacks, the victim's four states |
| Creation, defeat, tag ends | `OnLoad` and `LoadSpecialAttrs`; the defeat's fall, cheers and physics; the tag attack halves' animation; bosslib's beaten and out checks (`ftBossLib_8015C2E0` and `8015C31C`, `8015C358` and `8015C3A0`) | his articles, first attack, laser and hum words (a union laid out differently for each) and partner; `Beaten` (states, attributes, cheer words, the cry's sound); `TagCry` (Master Hand cries only in the clap, Crazy Hand in all but the crush, as each original tests) |
| The attacks | Master Hand's punch and Crazy Hand's walk (`Hold`), the flat slap and the drill (`Window`), the drill and the falling crush (`Countdown`), the poke and the rising crush (`Chases`), the finger beams and the poke (`Beams`), the jet and the stomp (`Pass`), the ram and the crush and the dive and the shrinking stomp (`Ram`), the disappearance and the back crush (the vanishing wind-up) | each move's states and attributes, the growth's words. Named: `Beams::partners_lasers`, Crazy Hand's poke making Master Hand's laser, which stops by name without Master Hand ([Melee's bugs](melee-bugs.md#crazy-hand-the-poke-makes-master-hands-lasers)) |

**Left as they are, and why.** Master Hand's sweep and Crazy Hand's slide have one shape but differ in their sound, the stage end they stop past (the left, the right, compared the other way) and their physics (the glide alone, the glide with the air motion): three branches in two short functions. The slaps, Master Hand's walk with its shot and his finger gun, and Crazy Hand's rise and bombs have no counterpart. The attack tables, their starts and the human button maps are each hand's own data. Master Hand's tag starts begin Crazy Hand's halves, and Crazy Hand's tag wait has no counterpart. The dispatch helpers stay each hand's: the single-player audit names Master Hand's stop text. Most of what the near-copy scan still pairs is each hand's `mod.rs`: his addresses and attribute offsets, and the two `HAND` settings.

**The numbers.**

| | |
| --- | --- |
| Lines | the hands' modules and `bosslib.rs` 6,205 to 5,307 (−898): Master Hand's 2,980 to 3,566 (the shared code), Crazy Hand's 2,780 to 1,304; in `crates`, 1,760 added and 2,658 removed. Near-copied lines (100-token windows) 1,251 and 1,163 to 556 and 492, nearly all of them `mod.rs`' address and attribute tables |

| Oracle | no corpus replay has a hand. The verify's sessions whose traces hold a Master Hand or Crazy Hand fighter (the trace scan's kinds 27 and 28) are 30 session jobs: Classic's clear, Event 50 (three), the hands' debug VS sessions, the bosses, the human Master Hand of the name entry glitch (his attacks and three 15-minute matches), Classic's Very Hard clear with Crazy Hand and its Easy run, the unlocks by Classic, the event-8 humans' grabs and bullets, and `mh-mash`. The base build (`fd77569a`), with a scratch per-tick hash of the whole `Game` in `melee-oracle session` (never committed), ran all 30, each "no difference" at its expected tick count: 440,703 ticks hashed, a hand in play in every session. Each commit's build matched those hashes tick for tick on 16 of them (the hands' debug VS sessions, two Events 50, the grabs, bullets and `mh-mash`, the human attacks, the bosses), and the final build on all 30, every tick equal and every session "no difference" |

### Task 11, the hookshot and the grapple beam: done (2026-11-20, `refactor/dedup-grapple` from `510e5139`)

Link's and Young Link's hookshot and Samus's grapple beam are one tether in two parts, each carrying both originals' `#[doc(alias)]`:

- **The item** (`it::kinds::tether`, `itlinkhookshot.c` and `itsamusgrapple.c`): its nine states (folded, shot, bouncing back, falling, drawn in, drawn in after a catch, latched, pulling, hanging), the spawn and put-away, the transitions, the fighter's three hooks, the scale (`it_802A2428`) and the forget (the decomp's `evt_unk`), over a `Tether` per item (`linkhookshot::HOOKSHOT`, `samusgrapple::GRAPPLE`): its variables' shared fields, its fighter's word, states and hand, its attributes' offsets, its links' functions and its named branches. Each item's file keeps its addresses, its `run` tables, its attributes and lengths, its links (`make_links`) and their physics.
- **The grabs** (`ft::samus::tether`, `ftCo_0D8E.c`, `ftCo_0D95.c`, `ftCo_AirCatch.c`): the grab's and dash grab's tether frames (`Running::catch_tether`: `fn_800D8EC8`, `fn_800D9228`, `fn_800D9558`, `fn_800D9930`), the air grab's (both parts of `ftCo_AirCatch_Anim`), the put-away off the ground (`fn_800D949C`, `fn_800D9C64`) and on a hit's start, over a `TetherGrab` per character (`link::catch::LINK`, `CLINK`, `samus::tether::SAMUS`): its frames, item kind, reach and hand, the beam's sparks, the hookshot's sounds. `ft::co::catch` calls the shared functions.

**What differed**, besides the addresses, states (Link's air grab is `0x168`, Samus's `0x165`), attributes' offsets and hands (his thumb by its part, her `ThrowN` by its bone index), each a setting:

| Setting | Hookshot | Grapple beam |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The hand's point (`ahead`) | 6 units along the hand's joint (`it_804D6D48`) | the joint's position |
| `sequence`, `on_spawn`, `on_hang` | none | the button sequence: its spawn's ground or air beam (`x16`), Z lengthening the hang |
| `animate`, `after_links` | none | every model animates after each line-up; the beam's own animations |
| `bounces_off_surfaces` | goes on past floors and ceilings | bounces off them too (the floor's normal) |
| `air_retract_doubles` | no | drawn in twice as fast off the ground |
| `line_up_held`, `lines_up_on_hang` | its own line-up while latched, pulling and hanging (`it_802A7384`); lines up as it starts to hang | the usual one; none as it starts to hang |
| `hang_frames`, `landing_lag` | his attributes' copy `xB8`, `xB4` | the character's own `xD0`; her copy's `xCC` |
| `stale_ignore_id` | no | the spawn's `x40` is a stack word ([Melee's bugs](melee-bugs.md#samus-the-grapple-beams-ignore-id-from-the-stack)) |
| The grabs' `reach`, `sparks`, `sfx` | 8, none, Link's or Young Link's | 2, 6 on the grab and 4 on the dash grab, none |

**Left as each item's own, and why.** The links' physics (the lead, the bounce back, the fall from the hand, the reel, the pay-out, the climb, the hold, the follow, and their sag and chase helpers) differ at nearly every step: the hookshot's links fall at a constant gravity, meet the stage through their own collision (`it_802A40D0`: an island's edge, a nudge off walls, the landing clink) and keep within one length of each other, the distance's sum fused (the file's inline copies of `it_802A3C98`); the beam's swing at random (an RNG draw per link), keep between two lengths, call `it_802A3C98` unfused, and its lead and bounce take the button sequence's swing hitbox and homing. The return codes and the last link's placement differ too. Sharing their walks would put a branch on nearly every line, so the shared states call them through the `Tether`. `make_links` stays each item's (the lengths' layout, two alternating link models against three animated ones, the collision's sizes), as do the two `Lengths` (seven words against nine) and the hookshot's spinning part (`itLinkhookshot_UnkMotion8_Anim`).

**The numbers.**

| | |
| --- | --- |
| Lines | the two items' files, `ft/link/catch.rs` and `ft/samus/tether.rs`, 4,211 to 3,938 with the new `it/kinds/tether.rs` (908 lines, half of them the descriptor's and the states' docs); 292 fewer in `crates` and `tools` (1,495 added, 1,787 removed) |
| Aliases | the same 18,078 `#[doc(alias)]` attributes after each commit (a sorted list diffed); `ledger --check` 19,827 of 19,827 each time, only the CSV's locations moved |
| Oracle | the base build (`510e5139`) ran the full diff of every verify replay with Link, Young Link or Samus (72 of the 330 by their Game Start's characters: 732,664 ticks, no difference) and wrote per-tick whole-state goldens (the Kirby rounds' were stale: the comparer's coverage changed since); each commit's build then checked all 72: every tick's state hash equal |
| Reach | a probe build (env-gated, never committed) over the 72, replays reaching each part for Link, Young Link and Samus: the grab's shot 14, 10 and 19; the air grab's 6, 4 and 7; the latch 6, 3 and 6; the hang 5, 3 and 1; the pull 6, 3 and 6; the fall 11, 6 and 16; the draw-in after a catch 12, 9 and 15; the bounce back only Samus's (2: the hookshot never bounced off a wall in the corpus; reached since, below) |
| Bounce back | recorded after the task (2026-11-20, `test/hookshot-walls`, [the oracle](oracle.md#sessions)): `hookshot-walls/link-front` and `yl-step`, Link and Young Link on Corneria's front deck grabbing toward the upper deck's step, bounce back off it in state 2 (`fn_802A3110`) three and four times (standing grabs from 21 to 39 units, Young Link's 20 to 24, nearer and the grab's stage check puts the hookshot away; and a dash grab); Young Link's Target Test start is too narrow for it. Both compare clean (3,790 and 4,625 ticks) and roll back clean; the bounce left unturned in `tether::shot` fails both and Samus's `grapple-samus-bf` and `stage-hyrule-temple` (`the_hookshot_bounces_back_off_a_wall`) |
| Mutation | one write in the shared code switched on for one fighter kind at a time fails that character's replay: the grab's shot (`tether::shoot`) Link `items-pokeball6-ps` tick 344, Young Link `stage-brinstar` 1513, Samus `items-pokeball4-fd` 2096; the air grab's shot Link `ics-link-bf-2a40237f-rf` 5034, Young Link `ylink-fox-dl` 2353, Samus `own-samus-fd` 494; the latch (`tether::latched`) Link 8167, Young Link 2357, Samus `dk-samus-bf-9da5c633` 3982; the draw-in after a catch Link 349, Young Link `stage-rainbow-cruise` 14923, Samus 2100 |
| Sessions | 20 of the verify's 222 session jobs the trace scan tags with the three (the trace scan over-counts; picked for the tether: the hookshot's air-grab and down-air sessions, the grab sessions, the ditto, the unlocks and events with Young Link, Classic and All-Star, the long recordings), on the final build, 15 on the worker and 5 here (their data isn't on the worker): the 15 session diffs (`hookshot-air-grab`, `link-dair`, `allstar-clear`, `2026-10-06_12-04-48`, `un-grab`, `tr-ditto`, `tr-cpu`, `tie-tie4-stock`, `rr-match4b`, `m6u-adv-ylink`, `unlock-ylink`, `ev34`, `ev-b`, `ev20`, `m6u-tr-combo10`; 3,660 to 55,022 ticks) compare with no difference; the session-syncs `rec-link-dair`, `un-grab`, `m6u-adv-ylink` and `ev34` roll back clean, and `hookshot-air-grab`'s shortcut stops at tick 3518 on the heap's classes, as the verify's `session-expect.txt` expects |
| Sync | `sync-test --rollback 7`, no difference: Link `ics-link-bf-2a40237f-rf` (14,199 ticks), Young Link `ylink-fox-dl` (10,969), Samus `own-samus-fd` (1,918) and `dk-samus-bf-9da5c633` (11,849) |
| Leaf-check | `link`, `young_link`, `samus`, `samus_swing`, `ftcommon`, `common_stops`: all pass |
| Speed (indicative, this PC with other agents' oracle runs on it) | `melee-bench native --runs 5`, three alternating rounds of the base (`510e5139`) and final builds, median of the 15: `grapple-samus-bf` 4,199 frames/s before, 4,189 after (runs 4,017–4,594 and 4,083–4,609); `cpu-l1-puff-l5-link-fod` 2,089 before, 2,065 after (2,047–2,139 and 2,026–2,100; its runs differ from the replay before and after alike). Within the spread. For the worker: `melee-bench native --dir DIR --runs 5 grapple-samus-bf cpu-l1-puff-l5-link-fod`, the two copied from `local/bench/oracle-m4-grapple/` and `local/bench/oracle-m4/` |
| After main's batch 10 (merged at `65e04cb3`: wave 3's `forget` and item-kind names) | the comparer's coverage had moved, so `65e04cb3`'s build wrote fresh goldens of the 72 (full diffs, 732,664 ticks, no difference) and the merged build matched all 72 every tick; `sync-test --rollback 7` clean on `ics-link-bf-2a40237f-rf`, `ylink-fox-dl` and `dk-samus-bf-9da5c633`; leaf-check `link`, `young_link`, `samus`, `samus_swing` pass |

### Task 11, the item pairs: done (2026-11-20, `refactor/dedup-item-pairs` from `75bc50b4`)

Lugia and Ho-oh, the green and red shells, and Samus's Charge Shot and Mewtwo's Shadow Ball (with Kirby's copies of both) each share one implementation now, the tether's way ([design call 7](#design-calls)): a new module per pair holds the shared functions, each carrying both originals' `#[doc(alias)]`, over a descriptor per item that names what differs. Each item's file keeps its addresses, its `run` tables, its data and what only it does; the fighters' callers keep calling each item's one-line `spawn`, `fire` and `remove`.

- **The birds** (`it::kinds::legendary`, `itlugia.c` and `ithouou.c`): out of the ball, the pose, the climb off the top of the screen, the return towards the camera, the sink's countdown, the attack's end, and their shots' set-out, life and stage hits, over a `Legendary` per bird (`lugia::LUGIA`, `houou::HO_OH`).
- **The shells** (`it::kinds::shell`, `itgshell.c` and `itrshell.c`): the dust trail, the roll, the fall, the landings, the pickup, throw and drop, the skid, the reflection, the shield bounce, the landings a bounce draws and the hit delay, over a `Shell` per shell (`gshell::GREEN`, `rshell::RED`) whose `vars` gives the variables' shared fields (`shell::Vars`, the same offsets in `itGShell_ItemVars` and `itRShell_ItemVars`).
- **The charged shots** (`it::kinds::chargedshot`, `itsamuschargeshot.c` and `itmewtwoshadowball.c`): the ball made in the hand and grown with the charge, the firing, the flight and its stage hits, the sparkles' count, the end, the removal, the reflection and the shield bounce, over a `ChargedShot` per shot (`samuschargeshot::CHARGE_SHOT`, `mewtwoshadowball::SHADOW_BALL`). Their variables are the same from `x4` to `x2C` but typed apart (the Charge Shot's three floats are the Shadow Ball's `Vec3` at `x4`), so the shared code reads them through a trait (`chargedshot::Vars`) both generated structs implement.

**What differed**, besides the addresses, each a setting:

| Pair | Setting | First | Second |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Lugia, Ho-oh | `climb` | its climb in `xE50.x` (`0x7C`) | `vel_accum` (`0x70`) |
| | `second_script_word` | one word (fire) | two (the effects, the fire), both cleared at the spawn |
| | `take_off_speed` | climbs from its attributes' `x4`, upward | from a standstill |
| | `sink`, `sink_ended` | the speed that brings it to rest where it came out; the sink's animation again (it fires at rest, in its physics) | its attributes' speed; the fire called when the animation ends |
| Green, red shell | `trail`, `roll` | dust every `x30` frames, roll at `x20` | `x44`, `x38` |
| | `hit_delay_on_throw` | none (started as it slides) | `x3C` on a throw or a drop |
| | `skid` | state 9 | state 7 |
| | `Vars::popped` | the pop-up bit (`xDEC` bit 3), cleared on a pickup and a skid | none |
| Charge Shot, Shadow Ball | `top` | the charge's state at most the eighth | up to the charge's levels |
| | `full_sparkles` | Samus's or Kirby's sparkles when full | none (its test's result dropped, [Melee's bugs](melee-bugs.md#mewtwo-the-fired-balls-dropped-comparison)) |
| | `wobbles` | no | the speed times the wobble's size (`x50`), the wobble's frame reset and the model's child back to size on firing; set back in the hands by the maker's scale |
| | `turns` | turns to face the way it flies back or bounces | keeps its facing |
| | `released`, `hold` | `ftSs_SpecialN_801291F0` and Samus's queries | `ftMt_SpecialN_SetNULL` and Mewtwo's |

**Left as each item's own, and why.** The birds' attacks (Lugia's three counts, its aim's search and the Aeroblasts' depth factors; Ho-oh's effects and the Sacred Fire's line search and ride) and their sinks' physics differ outright. The green shell's slide, pop-ups and owner grace and the red shell's chase, its push table, bounces and hit count share no more than the hits' outlines: their states, flags and speeds differ at every step, and the two `anim` countdowns run in different orders. The Charge Shot's glow in the hand, and the Shadow Ball's wobble, burst and the forward throw's ball, are one shot's alone. The Koopas' shells (`zgshell`, `zrshell`) near-copy the green shell's (186 of their 1,119 lines) but stay out: they're enemy items over the enemies' common variables (`zako::bytes`, not the shells' struct), have their own states (waking, the red one's pose), and are `refactor/dedup-enemies`' area. The Charge Shot's damage now converts as the Shadow Ball's did (`ppc::cvt_fp2unsigned`); its own conversion differed only for a NaN, which no charge reaches.

**The numbers.**

| | |
| --- | --- |
| Lines | the pairs' files with their new module: the birds 1,003 to 894 (−109; code lines, comments and blanks left out, 790 to 691), the shells 1,304 to 1,296 (−8; 1,092 to 1,060), the charged shots 1,287 to 1,346 (+59; 1,102 to 1,097: the variables' trait is 130 lines); 1,861 added and 1,840 removed in `crates` and `tools`, leaf-check's tables included. The near-copied logic is gone (the 100-token scan finds 60 of Ho-oh's 213 lines in Lugia's, 105 of the green shell's 451 in the red one's, 66 of the Shadow Ball's 445 in the Charge Shot's: the address lists, `run` tables and the shells' hit outlines), but the descriptors' and the shared code's docs take back most of the lines |
| Aliases | the same 18,078 `#[doc(alias = "...")]` attributes after each commit (a sorted list diffed); the list-form `evt_unk` the two birds' `forget` carried is one now (442 list-form aliases, from 443); `ledger --check` 19,827 of 19,827 each time, only the CSV's locations moved |

Lugia is in one replay and one session (`m6u-ev37-pichu`) of the corpus |

### Task 23, wave 6, step 3: the other generated structs

**Done (2026-11-22, `refactor/wave6-rest` from `0449dc76`, task 22's tip), its end checks in part ([checks](#task-23-checks)).** Sarah: "agreed, i am generally team rename" and (2026-11-20) "sure, i said rename freely"; [design call 11](#design-calls) for the comparer's labels. Steps 1 and 2's way of working: the map in structgen, the rename by `rename_readers.py`, every name checked by a phase that first renamed each target field to a unique placeholder (`w6_camera_x2ba`), so its uses could be listed by name with their functions and the decompilation's lines. 418 fields in 24 structs renamed, in the brief's order, six commits, each keeping its C name as a list-form `#[doc(alias("x2BA"))]`; the survey's "2,700 fields in 158 structs" counted each definition twice and every struct `game::structs` holds, named or not: 1,030 offset-named fields in 104 structs were left after step 2. Two continuations named the other 612 in thirteen commits, and 27 of the structs' types (the address-named ones of the save, the modes and the HUD, and the fighter's records): every field `game::structs` names by offset now has a name but the decomp's own paddings ([left](#task-23-left)).
