The decomp's own `pad_*` names with their offsets are kept (`Camera::pad_2C6`, `pad_39B`; rustdoc refuses an alias equal to the name). The last continuation's: `unused_*` for `VsSceneState`'s `unk_F`, `VsSceneFighter`'s `x5`, `PlayerInitData`'s bits `xD_b5` to `b7` (the record's export and the 1P left-list copy read them as bytes, nothing by name), `VsModeData`'s `unk_0x3` to `7`, `DebugPause`'s `unk_38_1`, `Announcement`'s `x13` and its flags' `x3` to `x7`, `IfDamageFlags`' last bit, the coin counter's `x3`, `SpawnItem`'s `x3E`, `x46_flag`, `x47_flag`, `LbShadow`'s bits 6 and 7; `unread_*` for `PlayerInitData`'s `xC_b3` (copied to a VS fighter flag nothing reads), `VsSceneFighter`'s `x9` (the platform's character), `Announcement`'s `x4`, the coin counter's `x0_b0`, the characters-left tally's `x108` (its process's GObj), `SwingLink`'s `unk_6C` and `unk_78`, `DynamicsData`'s `unk_94`, `SpawnItem`'s `x45_flag`. `NameTagData`'s `padding_x1A2` became `pad_1a3` (a name read past its end reaches it). Kept as the decomp's: `VsSceneState::pad_8C`, `GmStats::pad_2`, `TrophyNotices::pad_500`, `ifTime_data::pad31`, `OffscreenArrows::pad_34`, `ScoreDisplays::pad_x4`, the flash's `pad1` and `pad_01`.

**Best guesses**, resting on one reader: the hitbox's `item_hit_alt`, `fighter_hit_alt`, `shield_hit_alt` (an item box's hit logged as another kind), `meets_bubble`, `meets_reflector`; the item flag `keeps_pokeball_reflected`; the camera's `debug_switch_0` and `1` (a setter and a getter each, nothing else); `dmg`'s `hit_frames`, `dot_frames`, `source_attack`, `source_class`; the rules' `alt_go`, `long_countdown`, `exact_start_frame`, `keeps_rumble`, `goes_on`, `run_kind`; `MatchPlayerData`'s `air_time`, `ground_time`, `timed_stat_1` to `4` (they read the player's words past the stale-move table, `pl_StaleMoveTableExt_t`, which no struct describes); `_StaticPlayer`'s `kirby_init_flag`; the continuation's `plAttackStats::strikes_count` (the attack flags' `count_x1A0`, set on every attack but the grabs and throws), `EventData`'s `proc_counter` and `proc_state` (each event's process's own), `TrophyRecords::walk_distance`; the last continuation's `VsSceneState::photo_mode` (camera mode's shot sets it, the pause's quit reads it), `GameData::demo_count` (the attract loop's and the how-to's rounds share it), `GameRules`' `gallery_song` (the sound test sets it, the gallery plays it), `IfDamageFlags::heal_anim`, `PlayerInitData::hp_explodes` (the hands' stamina display), `OffscreenArrows::silent` (the rules' `arrows_flag`), `LbShadow::off_unset` (read with the others, set by nothing), `VsSceneFighter`'s `respawn_sub` and `respawn_flags` (read, never written), `SwingLink`'s `return_rate` and `ease_axis`. The map can rename them again by the same script (a field already renamed is renamed by hand: the script renames from C names).

**The script.** `rename_readers.py` gained one thing: rustc names a renamed type by its Rust name (`ControllerMap`), the map by its C name (`controller_map`), so it maps one to the other (`config.RENAME_TYPES`); nothing step 3 renamed needed it until the continuation's `a4e1fd8f`, which taught the script to rename types too: a renamed type's uses no longer build (E0412 a type, E0422 a struct expression or pattern, E0433 a path, E0425 a value, E0432 an import, E0531 a tuple pattern), and the old name inside the error's span becomes the new one, round after round as for fields; the type keeps its C name as a `#[doc(alias)]`, and step 3's type names are `names_step3.TYPE_NAMES`, which `config.RENAME_TYPES` takes. Its first run renamed every use of the six types (imports, paths, constructors, closures' parameter types) with no error left. Step 3's map is its own file, `tools/structgen/names_step3.py` (a dict per struct, in `GROUPS` by commit), which `names.py` reads into `FIELD_NAMES`, so the script brings a branch from before step 1, 2 or 3 up to all three in one run. Each commit was the script's run over the one before (5, 5, 6, 5, 4 and 2 rounds; 449, 470, 710, 486, 495 and 75 uses), and the whole was run again from the base's sources with the final map ([checks](#task-23-checks)). Strings it can't see were fixed by hand: the ruleset test's labels (`camera.zoom_step (x2BA)`, `fighter.coll_data.map_generation (x38)`) and the roster's audit text for the race's watched point; the lines it lists as still naming an old field after a `.` are all other structs' (`gr::vars`' hand-written labels, the capture tool's labels of statics). The last continuation fixed one bug: rustc spells a file's path two ways across a workspace's targets (`tools\oracle\src\boot.rs` and `tools/oracle/src/boot.rs`), and the script rewrote such a file once per spelling, the second time over stale spans (an assertion stopped it: the oracle's `boot.rs` was the first file both a library and a binary target reached with a step 3 field); it now keys files by a normalized path. Its runs: 3, 9 and 6 rounds (15 after the stop, 349 and 819 uses), 21 types with no error left. Hand edits: the oracle session's playback filter (`vs.state.fighters[..].ckind (x0)`, `flags (x4)`) and leftover filter (`flash.overlay (x4).`, `.desc.ease_axis (unk_54):`), the cosmetic test's hat paths (`desc.joint_pos`, `spin_axis`, `spin`); `boot.rs`'s `gm_80479D58` lines keep their C names as label strings.

<a id="task-23-left"></a>**Left:** no offset-named field but the decomp's own paddings, 20 in 17 structs (`inv.py`'s inventory: names matching `x<hex>`, `unk*`, `pad*`, `b0`-style bits without an alias): `Fighter::pad_port`, `_StaticPlayer::pad_port`, `Camera::pad_2C6`, `pad_39B`, `VsSceneState::pad_8C`, three item vars' paddings (the yo-yo's, the Thunder Jolt's, the Ice Climbers' string's), `ifTime_data::pad31`, `OffscreenArrows::pad_34`, `ScoreDisplays::pad_x4`, the flash's `pad1` and `pad_01`, `TrophyNotices::pad_500`, `ChallengerRecords::padding`, `GmStats::pad_2`, `FighterData::padding_0x32`, `GmSaveData`'s `pad_5`, `pad_2D5`, `padding_trophy_flags`. Before the continuations, 605 offset-named fields were left in 76 structs; they named 68 in `72c8086b`, 87 in `2eb870fc`, 101 in `4ca06f45`, 98 in `a4e1fd8f`, 89 in `555b7a77`, 84 in `765ad63e` and 85 in `fef6a0ee`.

**Types still named by offset or address:** 23 nested structs whose decomp names are their parent's field's (`Fighter_x8B0_t`, `Fighter_x1614_t`, `Fighter_x1670_t`, `Fighter_x1A88_xFC_t`, `Fighter_x20B0_t`, `Fighter_x221B`, `Fighter_x5F4_arr`, `ftCo_DatAttrs_xBC_t`, `ft_800898B4_t`, `lb_80014638_arg0_t`, `arg1_t`, `xB6C_t`, `Item_xDCC_flag`, `CameraBoxFlags_x0`, `Camera_x2D0`, `Ground_x10_flags`, `StageInfo_unk8C`, `pl_x5EC_t` and `_x10`, `pl_x8C0_t` and `_x4`, `pl_x8D8_t` and `_x4`) and `db_ItemAndPokemonMenu_t`. Their fields are named; the script renames them by `names_step3.TYPE_NAMES` like the 27 it did, if wanted.

Functions and locals named after a renamed field (`set_x39a_b0`, `x39a_b0`, `gr::ground::set_b1`, `b1`, the `vs_x*` aliases) and comments citing offsets are unchanged: the comments' offsets are the aliases'. The address-named types of the save, the modes and the HUD are renamed (27, each keeping its C name as an alias: `gmm_x0` is `GameData`, `un_804A1F10_t` `OffscreenArrows`); the nested ones named after their parent's offset above are the decomp's and unchanged. Two type aliases older than step 3 stay (`hud::small::Scores`, `Arrows`).

**Design call 3's second half** (taking the structs over by hand, once every field has a name). What step 3 found, for that decision:

- **Would pay now:** `StageInfo` (with `StageInfo_unk8C`, `StageCameraInfo`, `GroundParam`) and `Camera`: every field named, their readers are each one subsystem's (`game::gr`, `game::camera`, task 13's boundary), and the generator's work on them is the mirror and the defaults alone. Hand-written, the camera's unions (`script_interest`, `script_eye`, `script_frame` are byte arrays read through `union_word`/`union_vec`) and `StageInfo`'s `item_frequencies` pair (`xA0` and `xA4_pad`, one array the C splits) could become the types they are. `CollData` too, now that task 21 has it behind `mp`: its split (task 21's "after 23") can be done by hand on named fields.
- **Would pay after its last nested struct:** `Fighter` (its own fields all named; left: `state_flags`' bits (`Fighter_x221B`), `ColorOverlay`, `Fighter_x8B0_t`, `x1614`, `x1A88_xFC`, `x20B0`, the reflect and absorb attributes, `FtPartsVis`, and in `dmg` `ft_800898B4_t`, `Struct2074` and the pickup bounds `lb_80014638_arg0_t`/`arg1_t`): 93 fields in 13 structs, of which `72c8086b` named 68 and `2eb870fc` the last 25 (with the data structs `Fighter` reaches: the smash charge, the common attributes, the bones). `Item` is the same, its last ones `ColorOverlay`'s and the step-on box's. Both now have every field named, nested structs included: by this measure they would pay now, though they are the largest (task 18's 400 fields, task 22's) and the most read.
- **Wouldn't pay yet (as found before the last continuation):** the modes' and the save's structs (`EventData`, `GmSaveData`, `VsSceneState`, the `gmm_*` and `gm_*` ones: 194 fields, each mode's scratch read by name in one module) and the HUD's (`un_*`, `ifStock_*`, `Element_*`: 90 fields named after addresses, the types themselves nameless). They need names first, and their types renamed with them (`config.RENAME_TYPES`), which is a larger step than fields: most are read in one module each, so naming them together with taking them over (task 24's mode registry for the modes) is cheaper than two passes. The player's stats (`StaleMoveTable`, `plActionStats`) are a table of counters the results screen and the records read by index; naming them is mechanical once the results' stat getters are named. (The continuation named the save's and the event mode's, 98 fields, and six types with them: the script now renames types, so a mode's structs can be named before task 24 takes them over, and taking them over then starts from readable code.)
- **Since the last continuation:** the modes', the HUD's and the rest's are named too (258 fields, 21 types), so every struct `game::structs` holds now reads by name, and the call no longer waits on names anywhere. What's left to weigh is each struct's own: the modes' are each read in one module (`gm::vs`, `gm::scene`, `gm::main_lib`), the natural owners of a hand-written struct, and task 24's registry already gives each mode an entry to hang its state on; the HUD's are `game::hud`'s alone; `ItemCounts`, `SwingLink` and `SpawnItem` are the item and dynamics libraries'. None of them holds a union or a split array the generator models awkwardly, which is what made `Camera` and `StageInfo` worth taking over first.
- **What the generator still earns** on a struct taken over by hand: the comparer's mirror (`mirror_gen.rs`, 31,000 lines), its labels with both names, the heap image's layout (`LayOut`) and the importer. A struct taken over keeps a hand-written mirror or a generated one from a hand-written description; the second keeps the labels' C names in one place.

### Design call 3's second half: the owned structs

**Done for `CollData`, `Camera` and `StageInfo` (with `GroundParam` and `Ground`) (2026-11-22, `refactor/own-structs` from `390dbedd`, task 23's first part); its owed checks run at the merge with `refactor/wave6-rest`, [below](#step-0-the-merge-with-own-structs).** Sarah, shown three ways to keep the comparer's mirror right once a struct is written by hand ((A) a derive macro whose field attributes carry each field's address, with a test checking every hand-written struct against the decomp's layout; (B) structgen kept for the mirror with per-field converters; (C) hand-written import and compare): "yes, do the derive macro instead and own the code".

**The macro.** `#[derive(melee_derive::Mirror)]` (`crates/melee-derive/src/mirror.rs`) makes a hand-written struct's `game::mirror::Mirror` (import from ssbm-rs's memory, compare with it) and, with `lay`, its `LayOut` for the heap's image, from attributes:

- `#[mirror(c = "Ground", size = 0x204, lay, pad(0x12, 2))]` on the struct: the decomp's name of the C struct (none for a group of fields with no C struct of its own, such as `StageInfo`'s `GuidePoints`), its size, and each run of the compiler's padding, which nothing compares.
- `#[at(0x50)]` on each field, in increasing order: a value of any `Mirror` type at that offset. `Mirror` covers the C scalars (`u8` to `i32`, `f32`), arrays of any `Mirror` type (labels `path[i]`), handles (`Option<GObjId>`, `Option<JObjId>`, `Option<StartHookId>`: `game::mirror::Handle`, as the generated code passes `Cx` the handle's kind), `Option<CodeRef>`, `Option<DataRef>`, the hand-written mirrors (`X34Flags`, `EcbSource`, `GroundUnion`, `StageCameras`) and the generated structs (`Vec2`, `_GXColor`, `ColorOverlay`), so an owned struct holds generated ones and the generated ones hold owned ones by name.
- `#[at(0x341, bits(1, 2))]`: a bitfield of the byte at that offset (bits counted from the top, as the compiler allocates them), of a `BitField` type (`bool`, `u8`, a `c_enum!`).
- `#[at(0x344, union)]`: a C union kept as its bytes (`[u8; N]`), compared and laid out whole.
- The label: `path.name`, with the decomp's name beside it when the field's `#[doc(alias("x50"))]` gives one (`fighter.coll_data.weight (x50)`), so the comparer's output reads as the generated mirrors' did ([design call 11](#design-calls)); a group's fields nest (`stage_info.guides.front_far (x13C)`, `stage_info.watch_2 (x718).point`).
- A const check fails the build when a field runs past the struct's size or into the field before it.

`c_enum!` (`game::mirror`) declares a C enum as a Rust enum: the decomp's values as variants and `Other(raw)` for any other value the game stores, read, compared, laid out and hashed as the integer it is.

**The layout check.** `game::mirror::layout_check::owned_structs_match_the_decomps_layout` reads ssbm-rs's `typegen/types.json` (as structgen does; `SSBM_RS_LOCAL`) and checks every owned struct against its C struct: the same size; fields and declared padding covering every bit once (a gap names the C fields no Rust field holds, an overlap the field); each field starting and ending on C field boundaries, so it holds whole C fields (one, a run of bitfields, a group); a field of an owned struct's type where the C struct holds one of that name; padding only over bytes no C field names. Groups with no C struct are checked field by field against the struct around them. It also checks that its list of owned structs is structgen's `OWNED` list. Proved by `layout_check::proof`: a right layout passes, and a field moved onto its neighbour's bytes, a field left out, a field of the wrong size and a struct of the wrong size each fail with the message naming it; and a field read at the wrong address (two bytes late) fails a compare against a known memory image (`t.c: -2 -14866`) where the right struct compares clean.

**The rule for leftovers.** Compared state stays exact, leftovers included, so an owned field holds every bit pattern the game can store there: an enum through `c_enum!`'s `Other`; a flag byte as its bitfields (every bit a field, named or `unused_*`/`unread_*`) or as a hand-written type that keeps the bits it doesn't name (`X35Flags::rest`, `GroundX11Flags::rest`); a C union as its bytes, with typed views over them (`game::camera`'s `union_word`, `union_vec`, `union_f32`) rather than typed members, unless a written proof with a test shows one member is ever live. The sync test's hash reads the fields in order, so an owned struct keeps the original's field order and each type hashes as the generated one did (`c_enum!` hashes its integer); a sub-struct adds nothing to the hash, an array adds its length, which is why `GroundParam`'s nine bubble colors stay nine fields.

**What was converted** (three commits, each checked before the next):

| Commit | Structs (C name, Rust name, home) | Types improved |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | `CollData`, `_ftECB` (`Ecb`), `SurfaceData` (`Surface`): `mp::coll` | none: `env_flags` keeps the original's `i32` and the collision's constants, which its 175 readers test with masks (a flag-set type would only respell them, in the fighters' and items' code other branches change); task 21's split of it is still to come |
| 2 | `Camera`, `CameraTransformState`, `CameraQuake`, `Camera_x2D0` (`OrbitLimits`): `game::camera::types` | the camera's mode (`CameraType`) is `CameraMode`, and `game::camera`'s `STANDARD` to `DEBUG_FREE` are its values; a quake's kind (`CmQuakeKind`) is `QuakeKind` |
| 3 | `StageInfo`, `StageCameraInfo`, `StageBlastZone`, `StageInfo_unk8C` (`StageBits`), `GroundParam`, `Ground`, `Ground_x10_flags` (`GroundFlags`): `game::gr::types` | the guide points are a group (`guides.front_near` for `guide_front_near`), and the two watched points' boxes (`watch`, `watch_2`: `half_width`, `half_height`, `point`); `grkind` stays the `i32` that compares with `GrKind` (the enum has no variant for a value outside the game's list) |

The 14 structs left structgen's output through its new `OWNED` list in `tools/structgen/config.py` (C name to Rust name and path): it generates neither them nor their mirrors, still walks them for what they hold, and `game::structs` re-exports them (`pub use`), so readers that name `game::structs::CollData` still build; new code imports them from their homes. Their names left `names_step3.py` (structgen refuses a `FIELD_NAMES` entry for an owned struct), their comments became the fields' docs, and `rename_readers.py` keeps working for the generated rest. `structs.rs` went from 14,115 lines to 12,913 and `mirror_gen.rs` from 31,300 to 28,994.

Vanilla's heap image and the snapshot see the same bytes as before: `CollData`'s layout (`LAY_ROOTS` reaches it through `Fighter`, `Item` and `ItemLink`) is the derive's `lay` now, the same writes in the same order as the generated one's; `Ground`'s stays `game::export::lay_ground`, by hand as before; `Camera` and `StageInfo` are statics the image doesn't lay out; `SPLICE`'s hand-written types inside them (`X34Flags`, `X35Flags`, `StageCameras`, `GroundX11Flags`) are field types now; none of the 14 is in `EXPORT_ROOTS` or `KEPT`.

**How to convert the next struct.**

1. Write it in its subsystem's module, field by field from the generated one (`game::structs`), in the same order: `#[derive(melee_derive::CloneFrom, Debug, Default, PartialEq, melee_derive::StateEq, melee_derive::Mirror)]`, `#[mirror(c = "...", size = ...)]` (`lay` if `LAY_ROOTS` reaches it, `pad(..)` for the padding the layout check reports), `#[at(..)]` on each field, and the generated field's `#[doc(alias(..))]` kept, which gives the label. Move its comments from `names.py` or `names_step3.py` onto the fields and drop its map there. Arrays over 32 elements need a hand-written `Default`.
2. Add it to `OWNED` in `tools/structgen/config.py` and to `layout_check::owned`, regenerate (`python tools/structgen/structgen.py $SSBM_RS_LOCAL/typegen/types.json crates/melee-sim/src/game`, then `cargo fmt --all`), and run the layout check.
3. Improve the types within the rule for leftovers (`c_enum!` for a C enum, a group for related fields, a hand-written type that keeps the bits it doesn't name), and keep each one's hash as the generated type's.
4. Check: the layout check, the label test, the whole state's per-tick hashes on the verify list's replay diffs, and a mutation (a field read from its neighbour's address) that the comparer reports under the new label.

**Checks.**

- **The whole state, tick by tick**, on the verify list's 330 replay diffs (task 23's goldens from `390dbedd`, their coverage line set to each oracle's: the comparer's sources changed by the derive, the hand-written mirrors and the label test): a sample of 19 (the bench's, a cape shield, a shield break, the camera, the match rules, items on, Jungle Japes' and Kongo Jungle's barrels, Onett, Mushroom Kingdom, Peach's Castle, Corneria, the item box, Kirby's swallow, stamina, the fixed camera, the special mix) after each of the three commits, 252,871 ticks the same each time; then all 330 at `2e46a4ee`, **3,552,272 ticks, every tick's state hash the same**, no stale read.
- **Mutations the comparer catches by the new label** (one oracle with all four, `fox-fox-fd`'s first tick): `CollData`'s weight read from `ledge_snap_x`'s word (`fighter.coll_data.weight (x50): ours 75.0, theirs 11.0`), the camera's `nearz` from `farz`'s (`camera.nearz: ours 1.0, theirs 30000.0`), `StageInfo`'s `grkind` from `flags`' (`stage_info.grkind: ours 37, theirs 0`), a stage object's `map_id` from `unread_0`'s (`ground.map_id: ours 1, theirs 0`, and each of the swapped fields' partner).
- **The layout check and its proof:** `owned_structs_match_the_decomps_layout` passes for the 14 structs; `the_check_passes_a_right_layout_and_fails_each_mutation` and `a_field_read_at_the_wrong_address_fails_the_compare` as above; the label test (`step_3s_renamed_fields_label_gives_the_originals_name`) holds the owned structs' labels, groups among them.
- **A bug the first full run found:** the handles' kind came from the macro's whole path (`crate :: hsd :: gobj :: GObjId`), which the goldens and the oracle's diffs passed (its importer and comparer fall back for a kind they don't match) but leaf-check's collision `Cx` stops on: `lbcollision` passed and `mpcoll`, `stage`, `camera`, `fighter` and `stages` failed at their first collision import. Fixed in `88f53d8c` (the kind is the type's name, as the generated mirrors pass it), with a test holding the three kinds.
- At `2e46a4ee`: `fmt`; the ledger's `--check` (19,827 of 19,827; only `docs/port-ledger.csv`'s line numbers moved); melee-sim's unit tests (188 passed, 2 ignored); `ticks_and_restores_dont_allocate` at one thread; `sync-test --rollback 7 --fp hardware` on `fox-marth-ps` (13,579 ticks) and `items-pokeball1-fd` (7,318): no difference; `--test session` filtered to `stage`, `camera`, `pause`, `magnif`, `brinstar`, `rainbow`, `hookshot`, `icicle`, `mute_city`: 13 of 16 pass, and `a_tournament_tie_runs_over_the_stage_selects_preview`, `icicle_mountain_climb_in_debug_vs` and `rainbow_cruises_blocks_hold_the_heaps_leftovers` failed, before the kind fix (their output wasn't kept; to rerun at `88f53d8c`). At `88f53d8c`: `--test ruleset` at one thread (all 12 pass); `clippy -D warnings` on melee-sim and melee-derive, all targets (the workspace's run at `2e46a4ee` stopped only on the derive's `int_plus_one`).

### Task 25, the tethers left: done (2026-11-22, `refactor/dedup-tethers` from `71653b16`)

Sheik's chain, the Ice Climbers' string and Ness's yo-yo share one implementation now, the item pairs' way ([design call 7](#design-calls)): a new `it::kinds::strand` holds the shared functions, each carrying the originals' `#[doc(alias)]`, over a `Strand` per item (`seakchain::CHAIN`, `climbersstring::STRING`, `nessyoyo::YOYO`) that names what differs. The three are closer to each other than to the hookshot and the grapple beam ([task 11](#task-11-the-hookshot-and-the-grapple-beam-done-2026-11-20-refactordedup-grapple-from-510e5139)): no fighter hooks run them, their item states and accessory callbacks drive them, and their links are made, walked and lined up alike, where the grabs' tethers make theirs from derived lengths, fall at a constant gravity or swing at random, and line up through `link::line_up_models`' scaled orientation. So they share among themselves, and with the grabs' tethers only the links' module and `Item_RetractChain` (`link::retract_chain`, moved there from the grapple beam's file, whose `wind_in_chain` calls it).

- **Shared** (`itseakchain.c`, `itclimbersstring.c`, `itnessyoyo.c`): the spawn at the hand (`itSeakChain_Spawn`, `it_802C27D4`, `it_802BE9D8`), the links' making (`it_802BAF2C`, `it_802C248C`, `it_802BE65C`), the release and removal (`it_802BB20C`, `it_802C2750`, `it_802BE958`), the end once the fighter leaves the move (the chain's and the string's animations), the tip's model at the hand (`it_802BCA30`, `it_802C33B8`), the tip sent out (the throw's, the pay-out's and the spin-out's starts), the pay-out walk (`it_802C2EC4`, `it_802BF28C`), the line-up (`it_802BCB88`, `it_802C3520`, `it_802BFAFC`), the forget (the chain's and the string's), and two walks every state's frame runs with its own step: `walk_out`, the links following the tip out one by one, and `trail`, the links behind a link pulled toward the hand (the chain's `it_802BBB0C`, the string's `it_802C2CA8` and `it_802C2DB0`, the yo-yo's `it_802BF180` and its drag's tail).
- **Each item's own**: its addresses, `run` tables and attributes; the chain's links' collision and clank (`it_802BB938`), its throw's and bounce's falls (`it_802BBD64`, `it_802BBED0`) and its swing (`it_802BC080`); the string's slack fall (`it_802C30E8`), Nana's hand's point its tip holds (Popo's `x2240`), its reel speed and its states' showing and hiding; the yo-yo's smash and facing, its spin (`it_802BE5D8`), its charge's drag along the ground (`it_802BF4A0`) and its links' collision (`it_802BF030`), and its model at the hand scaled with Ness (`it_802BF900`).

**What differed**, besides the addresses, each a setting:

| Setting | Chain | String | Yo-yo |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `vars` (tip, hand, fighter, tip's model) | `x0`, `x4`, `x8`, none | `x4`, `x8`, `xC`, `x14` | `x8`, `xC`, `x10`, `x18` |
| `part`, `flagged` | her `L3rdNa`, sets `x44`'s top bit | his `L4thNb`, clears it | his `R2ndNa`, sets it |
| `at_item`, `tip_starts_out`, `coll` | at the origin, in, a square of 1 for every link | at the item, out, none | at the item, out, 1 and twice his height for the yo-yo |
| `ahead` | 0.1 along the hand's joint | the joint's point | the joint's point |
| `states`, `let_go`, `forgets_fighter` | her side special's; she lets go; forgets her | the up special's; Popo; forgets him | the smashes'; Ness; keeps him |
| `length`, `copies` | the link length | the link length, the matrix copied out and back (`PSMTXCopy`) | his height, times the size but for the yo-yo |
| `tip_folds_in` | no | the knot goes back in at the hand | no |
| `on_link`, `tip_faces` | her hitboxes at each third and the tip | none | his hitbox at the yo-yo, which faces its way |

**Left as each item's own, and why.** Each walk's per-link step differs throughout: the chain's links meet the stage through their own collision with a clank and fall faster down the chain on the bounce (a factor per link), the string's fall with no collision and stop at a limit of links, the yo-yo's drag runs the airborne pass on the link behind ([Melee's bugs](melee-bugs.md#ness-the-yo-yos-drag-runs-the-link-behinds-collision)) and a second pass from above the hand. So the shared walks take each step from its item. The chain's swing (the stick's history down the chain, 230 lines) has no counterpart. The yo-yo's model at the hand concats an offset and a scale by Ness's height where the other two copy the hand's matrix, so it keeps its own and shares only the placing. The `forget`s of the yo-yo and the hitlag callbacks (already one pair, which other items call) stay as they were.

**Melee's bugs.** The chain's clank switch read before it's written keeps its entry (`seakchain.rs`, `link_coll_clank`); the chain's swing with no link out keeps its row, its line moved. Two added: the yo-yo's drag running the collision of the link behind (`nessyoyo::drag`), and, under the latent defects, the three line-ups' walk for a link out without a null test, which the chain's port stopped on and the string's and the yo-yo's skipped quietly: the shared line-up stops by name for all three. The strand's links stop by name for a count under two (its attributes' word from the disc; the items audit says why no match reaches it).

**The numbers.**

| --- | --- |

| Aliases | the same 18,078 `#[doc(alias = "...")]` and 2,236 list-form attributes after each commit (a sorted list diffed); `ledger --check` 19,827 of 19,827, only the CSV's locations moved |
| Oracle | the base build (`71653b16`) ran the full diff of 127 verify replays, 1,262,807 ticks with no difference, writing per-tick whole-state goldens: the 53 whose Slippi item updates name the chain (22), the string (20) or the yo-yo (12), the 55 more with Sheik, Zelda, Ness or the Ice Climbers, and the 22 with the grapple beam (for `retract_chain`'s move; 3 of them among the others); each commit's build then checked all 127: every tick's state hash equal |

Their 23 session diffs ran here (the worker's test lock was held by another job) with a scratch per-tick hash of the whole `Game` in `melee-oracle session` (env-gated, never committed): the base and the final build each compare with no difference, and every tick's hash is equal. 22 whole, 363,025 ticks: the chain's `m6e10-e09-zelda`, `m6r-stage8`, `gs-match1`, `rr-match3b`, `r2-match1c`; the string's `m6bb-nana`, `rt-train`, `rr-match15`, `rr-match4b`, `coin-ys`, `m6r2-belay`, `allstar-clear`; the yo-yo's `rr-mode2c`, `rr-match3`, `m6i-classic-quit2`, `m6i-classic-easy`, `m6i-classic-no`, `r2-stage3`, `rr-mode12` and the three long recordings (`2026-10-06_16-32-11`, `2026-10-06_16-58-24`, `2026-10-10_13-05-00`), besides `gs-match1`, `rr-match3b`, `r2-match1c` and `rt-train` above. `rt-attract` (the yo-yo from tick 15,973) is a partial check: its base run stopped at its four-hour limit after 31,920 ticks, the final build ran 31,000 (`--ticks`), and the two agree over those; the rest of the session wasn't hashed |

#### PGO for releases

Done 2026-11-21, `perf/release-pgo` from `1d38e93b` (main merged at `d23c237e`). Sarah, 2026-11-21: "PGO off for everything except release builds, in which case PGO mandatory". The dist tool's `--pgo` builds the player instrumented, plays a training set on it (`tools/dist/src/train.rs`: from power-on under both rulesets, the menus and a VS match against a level 9 computer player, the Slippi match's replay played back, and the six benchmark matches with both renderers, Extrapolate, widescreen and MSAA), merges the profiles and builds with them; for Linux, `--pgo-profile` makes the profile on a machine with the disc and `--pgo-use` builds with it on GitHub's 22.04 runner (for glibc 2.35), refusing a profile from another commit, toolchain or crate graph. A missing disc or `llvm-profdata` refuses; nothing falls back to a plain build; the workflow attaches no Linux build without the profile, and fails a program needing glibc past 2.35. The player's own bench on the worker: PGO 4 to 13% under the plain `dist` build (tick and frame, means) and 12 to 19% under `release` on every benchmark match, Sarah's 3.19 replay (no training run played it) and the exact renderer; a release's builds take about 45 minutes on the worker and 1 to 2 hours on this PC ([performance](performance.md#pgo-for-releases-2026-11-21), [the player](player.md#playing-it), Releases).

### Task 25, the held items and the steered shots

Two more item families share one implementation each, the item pairs' way ([design call 7](#design-calls)): a new module per family holds the shared functions, each carrying every original's `#[doc(alias)]`, over a descriptor per item that names what differs. Each item's file keeps its addresses, its `run` tables, its data and what only it does; the fighters' callers keep calling each item's one-line `muzzle`, `star_offset`, `spore_offset`, `spawn`, `let_go` and `owner`.

- **The held items** (`it::kinds::held`, `itfflower.c`, `itharisen.c`, `itlgun.c`, `itlipstick.c`, `itmball.c`, `itparasol.c`, `itsscope.c`, `itstarrod.c`): the Fire Flower, the Fan, the Ray Gun, Lip's Stick, the Poké Ball, the Parasol, the Super Scope and the Star Rod each lie still (state 0), fall (1) and are held (2). Shared: the spawn (the attributes' charges into `xD4C`, the item's own set-up, the fall), the landing, the fall, the still and idle states, the ledge's fall, gravity, the thrown fall, the two falls' landings (always, or only with charges left), the pickup, the drop and the throw, the hits' bounce, the reflection, the shield's bounce, the skid and its collision, the hand's offset (the Ray Gun's muzzle, the Lip's Stick's spores, the Star Rod's stars) and the charge spent, over a `Held` per item (`fflower::FIRE_FLOWER`, `harisen::FAN`, `lgun::RAY_GUN`, `lipstick::LIPS_STICK`, `mball::POKE_BALL`, `parasol::PARASOL`, `sscope::SUPER_SCOPE`, `starrod::STAR_ROD`).
- **The steered shots** (`it::kinds::steeredshot`, `itnesspkflash.c` and `itzeldadinfire.c`, with their blasts, `itnesspkflashexplode.c` and `itzeldadinfireexplode.c`): PK Flash (Ness's and Kirby's) and Din's Fire. Shared: the spawn beside the fighter, the flight's start, the growth while the special is held and its burst on the owner's letting go, the size, the stage check, the reflection, the owner's letting go, the owner, the end; the blast's spawn, start (state, lifetime, first frame, rumble), size, hitbox growth, stop and end, over a `SteeredShot` (`nesspkflash::PK_FLASH`, `zeldadinfire::DINS_FIRE`) and a `Blast` (`nesspkflashexplode::PK_FLASH_BLAST`, `zeldadinfireexplode::DINS_FIRE_BLAST`). The shots' variables share their first fields (`xDD8` to `xDE4`) and the blasts' theirs (`xDD4` to `xDDC`), read through a struct of references as the shells' are.

**What differed**, besides the addresses, each a setting:

| Family | Setting | Values |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Held | `charges`, `on_spawn` | the Fire Flower, Ray Gun, Lip's Stick, Super Scope and Star Rod load their charges; the Fire Flower's attack id and flame words, the Ray Gun's recoil, the Lip's Stick's spores not ready, the Parasol's flag, the Poké Ball's closed bits after |
| | `fell` | the Poké Ball's own (a closed ball keeps its animation); the others' state 1 |
| | `dropped`, `thrown`, `drop_update` | 6 and 6 without the drop's update (the Fire Flower); 4 and 4 (the Ray Gun), 3 and 3 (the Super Scope, the Poké Ball), 2 and 2 (the Parasol, its own throw with its sound); 4 and 3 (Lip's Stick, the Star Rod), 8 (its own drop) and 7 (the Fan) |
| | `skid` | 7, 9, 5, 5, 4, 3, 4, 5 |
| | `bounces_only_let_go` | the swung items (the Fan, Lip's Stick, the Star Rod) bounce off what they hit only once let go of or thrown |
| | `hits_disarm`, `skid_faces_motion` | the Poké Ball: a hit, reflection or shield bounce stops its pickup (a hit its hitboxes too); its skid turns to its motion |
| Steered | `launch` | the flash rises at its angle; the fire takes its angle, speed and flight's effect |
| | `let_go_of`, `owner_done` | the flash bursts when its owner stops charging (Ness's or Kirby's `PkFlash` by kind) and clears his special; the fire when Zelda releases it, and she forgets it |
| | `end_effects` | the fire's effect ended at its end; none for the flash |
| Blast | `life`, `on_start`, `end_effects` | 1,024 frames (its animation's end cuts it short) and nothing, or 60 with its effect on at the start and ended at the end |
| | `animate`, `rumble` | each blast's own frame and rumble ladder |

**Left as each item's own, and why.** The held items' animations that do something (the Fire Flower's looping states that end its flames, the Ray Gun's recoil, the Lip's Stick's readiness, the Parasol's spin), the Fan's drop (its size and speed back) and the drop's landing on its unset `xD4C` ([Melee's bugs](melee-bugs.md#a-dropped-fan-lands-or-vanishes-by-a-word-it-never-sets)), the Lip's Stick's thrown flight that bursts its spores, the Parasol's slowed falls, throw and opening, the Poké Ball's opening and its facing falls, the Super Scope's charge levels, costs and stand-in, the shots and swings themselves. The flash's and the fire's flights (a rise pushed sideways and falling, against a turn that speeds up), their bursts (the flash waits its delay or fizzles on the stage; the fire bursts at once and spawns its blast when its burst ends) and the order of their frames' steps differ throughout. The blasts' frames differ in more than a branch each (the flash's damage set once and unrounded, its hitbox grown whatever its state, ending with its animation; the fire's damage rounded to single and set every frame, its hitbox grown only while on, ending with its lifetime), and their rumble ladders differ in thresholds, comparisons and order, so each keeps its own and the shared start calls it. The `forget`s stay one-line calls of `lib26b1::forget`. The Fan's `xD4C` landing and the blasts' x speed ([Melee's bugs](melee-bugs.md#zelda-dins-fires-explosion-keeps-its-x-speed), now `steeredshot::blast_stopped`) keep their entries.

**The numbers.**

| | |
| --- | --- |
| Lines | the held items' eight files 2,300 to 1,929 with the new `held.rs` (411 lines, a third of them the aliases); the steered shots' four 1,365 to 1,399 with `steeredshot.rs` (442), the descriptors' and the shared code's docs taking back what the dedup removed (code lines, comments and blanks left out: 1,782 to 1,548 and 1,164 to 1,091). The near-copied lines (the survey's 100-token scan over each family's files) fall from 878 of 2,300 to 443 of 1,929 and from 469 of 1,365 to 232 of 1,399: what's left is the address lists, the `run` tables and the attributes' readers. 1,451 added and 1,777 removed in `crates` and `tools` (two commits: 738 and 1,107, then 713 and 670) |
| Aliases | the same 18,078 `#[doc(alias = "...")]` and 1,835 list-form attributes after each commit (a sorted list diffed) |
| Oracle | the base build (`7e7ac110`) ran the full diff of the 58 verify replays whose Slippi item updates name one of the 14 kinds (the eight held items, PK Flash, Kirby's, Din's Fire and their blasts), 618,973 ticks with no difference, writing per-tick whole-state goldens; the first commit's build checked the 34 with a held item (318,868 ticks) and the final build all 58: every tick's state hash equal |
| Sessions | the verify's session traces scanned for the 14 kinds in their item regions (144 of 537 traces; the Poké Ball alone in 93) and 37 of their session diffs picked to reach every kind several times, the flash and the fire in each session that has them where the data is: on the final build all compare with no difference, 550,726 ticks (24 on the worker, 455,549 ticks; 13 here, whose traces the worker lacks, 95,177), PK Flash in `idle-title`, `menu-attract`, `rr-match1`, `rr-match3`, `rr-mode2c`, `m6db-event-stage`, `gs-match1` and two long recordings, Din's Fire in `ev47`, `m6e8-e44-join`, `gs-match1` and `2026-10-06_16-58-24`, the held items in Training, Classic, Adventure, All-Star, the events, Multi-Man Melee, the routes, the unlocks and the long recordings; the session-syncs `tr-items` and `ev47` roll back clean, and `mutate-no-image` differs at tick 798 as `session-expect.txt` expects. Not run: `rr-mode12`, `rr-match3b` and `r2-match1c` (their data only here, 1.6 h with the others queued; the flash and the fire are covered without them) |
| Mutation | one write in the shared code switched on for one item kind at a time (an env-gated probe build, never committed) fails that kind's replay at its first use: the landing (`held::landed`) the Parasol `items-held-fd` tick 1509, the Ray Gun `items-zelda-sheik-fd` 515, the Super Scope `sscope-fd` 292, the Star Rod `items-swing-bf` 1459, Lip's Stick `items-zelda-flower-fd` 290, the Fan `items-held-bf` 558, the Fire Flower `items-shoot-fd` 1927, the Poké Ball `items-pokeball7-fd` 299; the growth (`steeredshot::grows`) PK Flash `own-ness-fd` 200, Kirby's `kirby ness-fd` 338, Din's Fire `items-powerups-fd` 135; the blast's size (`steeredshot::blast_scale`) PK Flash's `oracle-cpu-approach-ness-fd` 975, Kirby's `kirby ness-fd` 6384, Din's Fire's `items-powerups-fd` 183 |
| Sync | `sync-test --rollback 7`, no difference: `items-held-fd` (the Parasol, the Star Rod, Lip's Stick, the Fan; 7,318 ticks), `items-shoot-fd` (the Ray Gun, the Super Scope, the Fire Flower, PK Flash; 7,318), `own-ness-fd` (1,918), `own-zelda-fd` (1,918), `kirby ness-fd` (both flashes, 12,508) |
| Leaf-check | `items` (three shards, 585 tests), `items_on`, `items_held`, `items_shoot`, `items_pokemon`, `ness`, `zelda`, `kirby`: all pass (`items_on`, `items_held` and `zelda` call the shared functions now) |
| Other | melee-sim's unit tests (181 passed, 2 ignored; the items audit lists the two new modules), `ticks_and_restores_dont_allocate` at one thread, `fmt`, `clippy -D warnings`, `ledger --check` 19,827 of 19,827 after each commit (only the CSV's locations moved). Not benchmarked: no hot path changed beyond a descriptor read per call |

### Task 17, the player's build

Done 2026-11-21, `perf/player-build` from `8f421fde`.

**What players run against what the bench measured.** The player is built with `dist` (fat LTO, one codegen unit; `cargo run -p dist`), the bench with `release` (sixteen codegen units, no LTO). The bench can now be built with `dist`'s settings exactly, `--profile bench-dist` (a profile inheriting `dist`, in a target folder of its own so the player's and the bench's builds don't rebuild each other's), and `melee-bench`'s tables name the profile they were built with. [Design call 10](#design-calls) was answered the other way (Sarah, 2026-11-21): **`release` stays the build speed numbers and targets are measured on**, since it is quicker to develop against and the player's build only runs faster; `bench-dist` is there to report the gap, and the call may be revisited if the speed tasks run out of meaningful wins before a target is reached.

**The gap, and PGO, on the worker** ([performance](performance.md#which-build-2026-11-21)): `bench-dist` 7.7 to 9.6% faster than `release` on the six benchmark matches and 12.9% on Sarah's 3.19 replay; restore and 8 ticks 3 to 11% shorter. A PGO build (`bench-dist` with a profile of the benchmark matches and 171 of the author's replays) is 20 to 22% faster than `release` on the benchmark matches and 29% on Sarah's replay, 10 to 14% over `bench-dist`; restore and 8 ticks 13 to 21% shorter. A profile of the author's replays alone (Slippi 3.19's rules and Slippi's float mode, Sarah's 3.19 replay left out) gave the same gain on Sarah's replay (+15.5% over `bench-dist`) and none on the Vanilla benchmark matches, which run the console's float mode and Vanilla's rules (-0.2 to -3.6%): PGO pays only on the code its training ran, and costs a little elsewhere.

**PGO is proposed for the player's build, not made it here.** A profile is about 9.6 MB (over the repository's 5 MiB limit) and goes stale with every change to the functions it covers, so it would be made at build time: `dist` would build the player instrumented, play a training set, and build again with the profile. On the worker the bench's instrumented build took 9.3 minutes, the training 34 minutes (1.5 for the benchmark matches, 31 for 202 replays at three threads) and the final build about 5, against 7.3 for `bench-dist` alone; on this PC the player's `dist` build takes about 24 minutes when busy, so a PGO `dist` would take roughly an hour. The training would have to be the player's own runs, covering every path players take (both rulesets and float modes, the renderers, audio, the menus from power-on), or the paths it misses get slower, as the replays-only profile showed on Vanilla's matches. That is a decision for Sarah ([performance](performance.md#which-build-2026-11-21) has the numbers).

**Checks.** No simulation change (the bench's header and the profile only). Every native run was in sync. `cargo fmt --check`, clippy (`--all-targets -D warnings`), the ledger's `--check` (19,827 of 19,827), melee-sim's lib tests (175 passed) and `ticks_and_restores_dont_allocate` at one thread pass. `cargo run -p dist` built the player (`dist` profile, 24 minutes on this PC with other agents' builds running) and its `--bench 900` on `fox-marth-ps` ran headless to the end.

| Was | Now |

| Kirby's seven `match`es on his copy's fighter kind (`u` `HAT_KIND`) in `ft::kirby::copy` and `ft::kirby::init`, 115 integer arms: the neutral special on the ground and in the air (`ftKb_Init_803C9DD0`, `_803C9E54`), the hat on and off (`ftKb_Init_803C9CC8`'s pairs), the KO and hit cleanups (`ftKb_SpecialN_800F19AC`, `_800F1A8C`), the copy's items (`ftKb_SpecialN_800F16D0`); the hat jumps' `matches!` | each character entry's `kirby_copy` (`ft::kirby::copy::KirbyCopy`: `special_n`, `special_air_n`, `hat_on`, `hat_off`, `cleanup`, `hit_cleanup`, `items`, `hat_jumps`; `NO_COPY` for Kirby, Nana, the hands, the wireframes and the Sandbag), read through `copy::copy_of`; the charged glow and the breath's refill name their kinds by `FighterKind` |

| The computer kinds: 15 comparisons of `cpu.kind` with a number, `ftCo_800B2AFC`'s 17-arm `match`, `init_cpu`'s and the walk's matches, `CPU_KIND_NANA` and `gm::approach::CPU_KIND` | `ft::CpuKind` (`kind_enum!`, 30 kinds, the game's numbers): the debug menu's names for 0 to 17 (`CpTp_Stay` to `CpTp_Item`), the decisions' for the single-player modes' 18 to 29 |
| Fighter kinds by number: Kirby's 4 in `ft::motion`, `ft::commands`, `ft::cpu_attack`, Yoshi's 14 in `ft::cpu_move`, Zelda's 0x13 in `ucf`, the Ice Climbers' in `sfx`, Giga Bowser's in the CPU, the color animations', recovery's, hold's, hats', demo poses' and hands' entrances' matches, the importer's and a leaf-check test's | `FighterKind` (`fp.fighter_kind()` for a fighter's; `FighterKind::from_id` for a number that may be none) |
| Character kinds by number: Zelda's and Sheik's 0x12 and 0x13 in the endings, events, results, VS setup and debug menu, Kirby's 4 in the VS and event copies, `ChKind_None`'s 0x21, Luigi's 7, Giga Bowser's, Master Hand's and Popo's, the stock icon's matches, the bench's Young Link | `CharacterKind` (`CharacterKind::NONE` for 0x21) |

**Left, as tables or the game's data.** The original's tables by number stay tables (the save's per-character records `gm_CKindToSelKind`, the announcer's voices `vs_melee`, the events' and modes' data); a range arm that orders kinds (`ckind >= 0x13`, `0..=0x12`) stays a number. Effects' and sounds' numbers stay the game's (design call 13), the effect switches' arms included. Assignments of a computer kind in `gm` (`p.cpu_kind = 4`) are left to the modes' registry (task 24).

**Checks.**

- **The whole state, tick by tick.** All 330 of the verify list's replay diffs (every character, Kirby's copy matches, the CPU diffs `oracle-cpu-*`, the bench matches, the stages and items): the branch's build checked the goldens task 14's final build (this branch's base) wrote and checked, 3,552,272 ticks, every tick's state hash the same. ### Task 20, Pokémon Stadium's allocations and rollback's worst state: done (2026-11-21, `perf/stadium-allocs` from `7871ac5b`)

The second survey's task 20: Pokémon Stadium's ticks allocated (8 to 11 a tick, and one a restore), the one exception to `ticks_and_restores_dont_allocate`, and its tick 12,500 sat at the 4 ms line for restore and 8 ticks on the worker. [Performance](performance.md#pokémon-stadiums-allocations-and-its-worst-state-2026-11-21) has the details and numbers.

- **Allocations:** the text system's maps flat (`flat_map::FlatMap`) and restored in place, its static string buffers a struct, the materials' marks refilled on a restore (`#[clone_from(refill)]`), each text laid out into a kept draw's storage (`Display::spare_texts`, from the frames the frontend gives back). No tick, display pass or restore of any benchmark match allocates; the test has no exception left and counts the display passes too.
- **The worst state:** no single cause, several small ones (the water transformation's collision lines, each fighter's screen mark, particles, the screen's text). The camera's look-at, perspective and up vector are memo tables now, and the floor and ceiling searches pass over lines that can't reach the segment, working out the last line's r5 after the loop. On the worker every state of the benchmark matches and Sarah's replay is under 4 ms, at most 3.76; tick 12,500 went from 3.93 to 4.02 ms to 3.63 to 3.65.
- **Tools:** `melee-bench rollback-split [--at TICK | --every TICKS]`, a state's window taken apart (restore, each tick's engine part and display pass) over many runs, steadier than `snapshot-match`'s five.

Checks:

- **The whole state, tick by tick:** the base's oracle ran the full diff of 94 of the verify list's replays, each clean, writing a per-tick golden: Pokémon Stadium's 36, the benchmark's five (with `fox-fox-fd --heap`), every `stage-*` diff (23), and the Ice Climbers' replays beyond Stadium's three (27, Nana's DI reading the searches' r5) with three player-code replays of Ice Climbers games. The branch's oracle checked each golden, at the allocation commit for the first 41 and at the head for all 94: **1,080,373 ticks, every tick's state hash the same.** The goldens' coverage line was set to the branch's, since the comparer's sources changed only by `state_eq`'s map helpers and the derive's new option.
- `sync-test --rollback 7`: `fox-marth-ps` (`--fp hardware`, 13,579 ticks checked), Sarah's 3.19 Pokémon Stadium replay `Game_20260129T214824` (`--fp slippi`, 14,140) and `ics-marth-ps` (3.16, frozen Stadium, `--fp slippi`, 17,227): no difference.
- `ticks_and_restores_dont_allocate` at one thread with no exception, mutation-tested three ways; melee-player's prediction tests (every tick's draws compared between two runs that both give their frames back), state-only tests and `drawing_offscreen_fighters_changes_no_gameplay`; the memo and search unit tests, mutation-tested; melee-sim's unit tests (179, 2 ignored), the ledger's `--check` (19,827 of 19,827; only `docs/port-ledger.csv`'s line numbers moved), `fmt` and `clippy -D warnings`.
- The draws keep their shape (`Draw::SisText`, a `TextDraw` laid out as before into reused storage), so the renderers' frames weren't rerun.

The renames were scripted, one kind of rename per commit (`a68251fd` the tables and constants, `9c225d79` the functions by address, `e0e3d583` `vs_speed_scale`, `77deb296` the functions by offset, `eea8c6fe` the fields, `3b3058fa` `Unk1PData`, `60e2626e` the ledger): each definition renamed with its alias added, then `cargo check --workspace --all-targets` run until rustc's errors (no such field, method, value or import) had placed every use, a shorthand field written out (`stage: x0`) and the const fns that build the executable's tables (`decisions::d`, `one_player::cs`, `adventure::ae`) given the new names as parameters. Outside the six modules only the names changed: their uses in `ft`, `gr`, `it`, `game::export`, `game::session`, `lb::save`, the comparer (`tools/capture`), leaf-check's tests (two renamed with their functions, `reset_ko_ring` and `reset_bob_omb_ring`, and their `ledger/checks.txt` lines).

**Left in the six modules.** 90 locals named by the field they hold (`let x4 = e.params(self)`); `Tick::pads` and `tournament::Setup::pads`, which are the pads (controllers), not padding; the docs' offsets; the generated structs' fields the code reads (`vs.start.x6`, `_StaticPlayer::unk4E`), which are wave 6's; and the `ft` wrappers the player functions call (`ft_80086f4c`, `ft_80087bac`), wave 4's. A few names say only what's known: `player_unset_ratio` (the player block's `unk50`, 1 from the reset and set by nothing, which Master Hand's defense ratio copies), `vs_arrows_flag` (the rules' `x2_1`, which the offscreen arrows keep), `count_early_out_bonus` (bonus 10), the album dialog's `dlg_joint4` and `dlg_joint5` (by their place in its tree).

The branch's oracle checked them: all 40, 426,646 ticks, every tick's state hash the same. The goldens' coverage line was set to the branch's (wave 3's way): the comparer's sources changed only by the renamed fields in `tools/capture` (and rustfmt's reflow of their lines), and the state hash reads fields in order, not by name.
- **The menus' and modes' sessions** (each compares every tick's whole state against its trace): 263 of the verify list's `diffsession-*` with the branch's oracle, the 178 the last verify timed under two minutes and the quickest of each other family under 15 minutes (Classic, Adventure and All-Star runs, events, Multi-Man, Home-Run Contest, Target Test, the tournament, the trophies, the data screens, the unlocks, the endings, the debug menu among them): 979,007 ticks, every one ending as the verify expects (262 clean, `mutate-no-image` differing at tick 798 as it must).

#### Task 11, the enemies' round two

Done 2026-11-21 (`refactor/dedup-enemies-2` from `662858bf`, round one's last commit): the Koopa Troopa, the Koopa Paratroopa and the stages' other enemies (Yoshi's Story's Shy Guys, Great Bay's Tingle, Kongo Jungle's Klap Trap, Mushroom Kingdom II's Birdo) are on `it::zako`'s shared functions wherever they near-copied round one's. No other enemy item is left: the enemy spawner (`grZakoGenerator_801CA8B4`) makes only round one's six and the two Koopas, and the other stages' hazards are stage objects or the stages' hurtbox item (`ityaku.c`: Yoshi's Island's blocks, Jungle Japes' Klaptraps, Peach's Castle's switches and Bullet Bills), with none of the enemies' code. Their modules are named after them now (`kinds::koopa_troopa`, `koopa_paratroopa`, `shy_guy`, `tingle`, `klap_trap`, `birdo`, `birdo_egg`), each keeping its decompilation name (`nokonoko`, `patapata`, `heiho`, `tincle`, `klap`, `kyasarin`, `kyasarinegg`) as a list-form alias on its `mod` line. Their `*Vars` structs and `VarsKind`'s variants keep the decompilation's names, as round one's do.

**What's shared now** (each original's alias moved onto the shared function):

| Shared (`it::zako`) | New callers | Settings |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `answers_false`, `still`, `looped`, `fall_physics`, `flight_physics`, `knocked_physics`, `bounce` | 47 callbacks, and the first steps of 5 more: the Koopa Troopa's do-nothing animations and physics, falls, thrown loop and knockback physics; the Paratroopa's pause, held and thrown loops and fall; the Shy Guy's idle callbacks and its spin's and knockback's physics; Tingle's 11 idle callbacks and his fall; the Klap Trap's 8 idle callbacks and its fall's and spin's physics; Birdo's falls toward the screen and away | the state and flags |
| `knock_back` | the Koopa Troopa's knockback (`it_802DCCCC`) and its leaving its shell (`it_802DCFBC`), the Shy Guy's knockback (in `it_802D8EC8`) | `Knock::Airborne` (up at least 0.2 whichever way it points, and in the air), `Knock::Plain` (not floored), `Knock::Lift` (in the air if it points off the floor, else left as it is) |
| `turning`, `walk_velocity`, `along_floor`, `add_hit_damage`, `init_collision`, `set_coll_facing`, `stop`, `attr`, `health`, `walk_speed` | the Koopa Troopa's turn and walk; the Koopas' setups (`Item_InitZakoCollision`), damage and attributes; the Shy Guy's attributes and health | the timer's word |
| `enter_stompable` (new) | the Goomba's, the Koopa Troopa's and the Paratroopa's stompable state entry (`it_802D848C`, `it_802DD4A8`, `it_802E1648`) | the jumped-on callback |
| `ko_destroy`, `thrown_lands` (new) | the Koopas' KO with its effect and sound (`it_802DD78C`, `it_802E1694`) and their thrown states' collision (`itNokonoko_UnkMotion10_Coll`, `itPatapata_UnkMotion6_Coll`) | none |

**Left as they are, and why.** No new enemy takes `zako::damaged` or a `zako::Enemy`: the Koopa Troopa's damage (`itNokonoko_Logic3_DmgReceived`) shares its first steps (`add_hit_damage`, `health`), but a hit past its health knocks it out of its shell instead of beating it, and a hit while thrown is a KO without a fling; the Paratroopa's knocks the Koopa out of it, with no damage; the Shy Guy's drops its food and spins away at 80% of its health; Tingle's pops his balloon; the Klap Trap's spins it away; Birdo's adds the hit's damage up to flinch, turn away or fall off, flipped over and placed again before she's flung. The Paratroopa's flight, the Shy Guy's bone-driven flight and walk, Tingle's float, press and rise, the Klap Trap's ride and barrel and Birdo's walks, waits and eggs have no counterpart in round one's enemies. The Klap Trap's knockback (`it_802E1E94`, the barrel's shot `it_802E2330`) goes in the air before reading the knockback, so its "in the air if it points off the floor" never runs; it isn't round one's `Knock::Free` (which then lands it), and is left. Birdo's egg (`birdo_egg`) is a thrown item with nothing in common with the enemies but their common words. The Koopas' shells (`zgshell`, `zrshell`) were left to `refactor/dedup-item-pairs` (it shared the VS shells only, so they're untouched here). No new Melee defect.

**The numbers.**

| | |
| --- | --- |
| Lines | the six enemies' modules, the Goomba's and `zako.rs` 6,336 to 6,049 (−287): the Koopa Troopa 717 to 559, the Paratroopa 777 to 674, the Shy Guy 651 to 623, Tingle 1,131 to 1,103, the Klap Trap 455 to 434, Birdo 730 to 685, `zako.rs` 1,398 to 1,498; the two code commits 283 fewer in `crates` and `tools` (217 added, 500 removed) |
| Aliases | the same 18,078 `#[doc(alias = "...")]` attributes and the same 122 pending ports before and after each commit (sorted lists diffed); `ledger --check` 19,827 of 19,827 after each commit, only `docs/port-ledger.csv`'s locations moved |
| Whole state | the base (`662858bf`) ran the full diff, `--heap`, of 48 of the verify's replays (every one on Yoshi's Story, 19, Great Bay, 4, Kongo Jungle, Kongo Jungle N64 and Jungle Japes, 8, and Mushroom Kingdom and Mushroom Kingdom II, 2; round one's 15 enemy replays, `monsters-*` and `catch-grab-*`), each clean, writing per-tick goldens: 465,699 ticks. Each of the three commits' builds checked every golden: every tick's state hash the same |
| Sessions | 40 sessions from the verify's milestone 6 list in full, `--all --heap`, with the branch's build: round one's 37 (the Mushroom Kingdom route `mk-*`, the debug Adventure's `m6ad2-mk-shells`, `m6ad3-mk-meet`, `m6ac-mk-slope9`, the Adventure runs, Icicle Mountain and the Underground Maze), `coin-ys` (Yoshi's Story's Shy Guys in a coin match), `mh-15min-kongo` (Kongo Jungle) and `m6-dp-rollout`: 351,706 ticks, each its expected tick count with no difference |

### Task 33, the dedup's tail: the shooting Pokémon and the characters' item hooks: done (2026-11-24, `refactor/dedup-tail` from `654ef418`)

Two of the third survey's families, the item pairs' way ([design call 7](#design-calls)): one Rust item per shared function, carrying every original's `#[doc(alias)]`, the differences passed in. The stages' skeletons are left to after task 27, which owns `gr`.

**The shooting Pokémon** (`it::kinds::shooter`): Chikorita, Cyndaquil, Staryu, Blastoise and Charizard share their way out of the ball (into the air, growing, falling, landing), the rounds of an attack (landing to stand or fire, standing, each round with its first round's settings, the rounds' count down, the script's shot, leaving and gone at its end), the collision and gravity of their states, and their shots' launch and life (spawned ahead of and above the Pokémon with its sound, out with its life, its life ticked, gone on the stage, reflected, kept or gone). Each kind has a descriptor: a `Release` for the way out (all five), a `Rounds` for the attack (Chikorita, Blastoise, Charizard), a `Shot` for a launch (the leaf, the water, the star). Each unit keeps its addresses, its `run` tables, its spawn and what only it does: Chikorita's own fall and bone motion, Cyndaquil's flames and launch, Staryu's chase and kick back, Blastoise's cannons and push back, Charizard's breath, smoke and flames.

| Setting | Chikorita | Cyndaquil | Staryu | Blastoise | Charizard |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Release`: out state, hitlag hooks, posed | 2, no, no | 0, yes, yes (then no longer kept clear of, its own) | 2, yes, no | 3, yes, no | 0, yes, yes |
| `landed`, `fallen` | fires | blows flames | nothing; stops and follows | stands | stands |
| `Rounds`: stand, round, leave | none, 0, 1 | | | 0, 1, 2 | 1, 2, 3 |
| `first_round`, `on_round`, `each_frame`, `stops` | the count; none; its bone; no | | | the count, the push and its slowing; none; none; yes | the count; the smoke's and flame effects' timers; none; no |
| `Shot`: ahead, up, speed; flagged; depth; sound | `x8`, `xC`, `x10`; yes; none; its own | | `x54`, `x58`, `x50`; no; none; panned | `x8`, `xC`, `x14`; yes; the cannons; panned | |

Staryu's star multiplies by its facing first where the shared launch multiplies by the attribute first: the same products, as neither is ever a NaN.

**The characters' item and knockback hooks** (`ft::hooks`): the third survey named this family Kirby's copies' setup, but what its four files near-copied is Kirby's, Marth's, Jigglypuff's and Samus's own hooks: the item hooks (`Fighter_OnItemPickup`, `_Invisible`, `_Visible` and `_Drop`, or a character's own copy with its own hold poses), the knockback hooks' textures (`Fighter_OnKnockbackEnter` and `_Exit`), and creation's item articles and multi-jump attributes. `ft::hooks` holds one body of each (`item_pickup` with the poses and the two parts passed in, `hide_held_item`, `show_held_item`, `item_drop`, `knockback_textures`, `register_items`, `multi_jump_from_attrs`); each character's hook keeps its alias and is one line. Fox's four item helpers, which Donkey Kong, Bowser, Giga Bowser, Link, Pikachu and the Wire Frames already called, move there unchanged as the one-hand forms.

| Character | Hold poses (kinds 1 to 4) | Pickup and drop parts | Hide, show | Creation |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Kirby | 0, none, 1, 2 (his own) | 1, 1 | part 1; with no item held, hiding stops, showing does nothing (as before) | articles, multi-jump |
| Marth | 1, 0, 2, 3 (`Fighter_OnItemPickup`'s) | 0, 1 | part 0 | (with fighter creation) |
| Jigglypuff | none, none, 0, 1 (her own) | 0, 0 | part 0; with no item held, nothing | multi-jump |
| Samus | `Fighter_OnItemPickup`'s | 0, 0 | part 0 | articles |

No new Melee defect.

### Task 33, part 3: Mr. Game & Watch's held items and their moves' hooks, Donkey Kong's two carries: done (2026-11-24, `refactor/dedup-tail-3` from `8f188675`)

Three of the third survey's families, part 1's way ([design call 7](#design-calls)): one Rust item per shared function, carrying every original's `#[doc(alias)]`, the differences passed in as a descriptor; the same calls in the same order for each user.

**Mr. Game & Watch's held items and Kirby's chef's pan** (`it::kinds::gamewatch`'s `Held`): the parachute, the turtle, Sparky, the oil and the pan share their spawn, their going (the owner told), their removal, their hitlag, their pickup, their landing and their animation's end. Each unit keeps its addresses, its `run` tables and its `HELD` settings. The five units' `forget` copies, unreferenced, went: `it::forget` holds their aliases.

| `Held` | Parachute | Turtle | Sparky | Oil | Pan |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Its owner forgets it, has left the move | the neutral aerial's `ItemSlot` | the back aerial's | the up aerial's | Oil Panic's | Kirby's `ftKb_SpecialNGw_8010D074`, `_8010D160` |
| Script variables its pickup clears | `x40` | `x40` | `x40` | `x44`, then `x40` | none |
| Taken into its owner's ground or air | no | no | no | yes | no |
| Hidden as its landing animation starts | yes (before the state change) | no | no | (no landing) | (no landing) |
| Gone on a frame | 30 | no | no | no | no |

**His air attacks' and specials' item hooks** (`ft::gamewatch`'s `ItemSlot`): the five moves' items (the parachute, the turtle, Sparky, Judgement's hammer, the oil) share their set flag, removal, hitlag hooks, the check that they should go and the specials' damage and hitlag hooks; each `ItemSlot` holds the item's slot in `u`, the states it stays out in, the move's hitlag hooks, the item's removal and hitlag, and the move's own hook that runs its items again (the aerials' runs all three, in the original's order). The three aerials also share their item's setup and landing (`AirItem`).

**Donkey Kong's cargo and heavy carries** (`ft::co::cargo`'s `Carry`): his cargo after the forward throw (the common `ftCo_Cargo*` states, a fighter on his shoulders) and a heavy item's (his `ftDk_Heavy*` states) run the same states from a different first one, and share their wait, walk, turn's entry, knee bend, jump, fall, landing and hurt wait, with their checks, interrupts, physics and collision. What they differ by is data: the first state's word in the cargo attributes (`x4` against `0`), the throw (`ftCo_8009BF3C` against `ftCo_80094EA4`), the cargo's drop through platforms and its rider following each state, and the heavy wait landing first. Each keeps its turn's animation and interrupt (the cargo turns its rider by hand; the heavy item's uses `ftCo_Turn_Anim_Inner` and waits to have turned), the cargo its throws and shouldered states, the heavy item its hit.

No new Melee defect.

Left: a real 144 Hz display at 4K (the worker's panel can't show it), a cold driver cache's start (47.5 frames a second with the uber shader), the CPU's first frame after new content (8 to 17 ms, under 0.4% of frames), the uber shader's hitch for a configuration the list lacks, and the CPU frame's 1 ms (records from the `Draw` list, not done this round) | | | |

On the worker (deciding, 4K in flight): the frames over 6.94 ms on Fountain of Dreams from 14 (ticks 0 to 1934) to 1, the match's first (20 ms, was 35), on `fox-marth-ps` from 4 to 1; the saved list 0.63 s warm (1.8 on two threads) and 16 s cold (37); a replay's window drew no call with the uber shader (36,313 before). Found: an uber-shader pipeline the list lacks takes the worker's driver 1.4 to 2.8 s at the draw (a specialized one costs a few frames); not fixed (a specialized pipeline made at once in its place is the proposal). The CPU frame's 1 ms not started | | | |

On the worker (deciding, 4K in flight): from nothing cached a match's first frame went from 17.5 to 23.2 s to 3.7 to 4.7 s without the shipped list and 10.5 to 16.7 ms with it, on matches it wasn't made from too, and the frames over 6.94 ms from 26 to 167 a match to 5 to 9; with the driver's cache warm and no list, 81 to 97 ms to 9 to 12 ms; with a saved list 19.9 ms to 11.5 to 13.7 ms.
